What replaces it costs nothing: **separation is border and fill.** In-flow surfaces (Card, Panel, Notice) separate by containment and the alpha ramp. Detached components design their own detachment when they are built — Dialog's separation already lives in its backdrop (scrim + blur, §11), and whether Popover/Menu need anything beyond border + opaque fill is decided at Popover, against a real backdrop, not pre-modelled. A law asserts the surface layer names no shadow and no elevation.

### Material: backdrop defense, and it is the APP's (Theme-scoped since 2026-08-16)

**Decision (revised 2026-08-04; the OWNER rewritten 2026-08-16, and this sentence caught up 2026-08-20): `material = solid | thin | regular | thick`. Three designed thicknesses, like the emphasis ladder; `regular` is the middle, SwiftUI's own name for it. `solid` is the default and IS a material — the rung where light stops passing through, not the absence of one (corrected with the move; it read "not a material — the seal, the absence of one" from 2026-08-04, which is also why the axis kept looking like a boolean wearing four values). It is a THEME property and nothing else: no component takes a material prop, nine of them lost one, and what a component may state is PLACEMENT — `backdrop`, a boolean fact about where it sits.** The sentence that stood here — *available on any component that can float, buttons included* — described a per-component opt-in and has been false since 2026-08-16; what survives of it is true one layer over, and is the selectivity rule: a Button over a hostile backdrop DOES express the app's glass, it just does not choose a thickness to do it with.

**Material is backdrop defense, not decoration.** Its job is keeping the foreground legible over whatever is behind it, and it fires only when something floats over busy content. That framing makes the axis testable — does the label survive — instead of aesthetic, and it is why the axis is not surfaces-only: a Card in a solid layout has nothing to defend against, while a Button floating over a photo does.

**Three designed points, not a dial (amended 2026-08-04 — was "two recipes", superseded when the axis gained its middle).** Every lever in a recipe does legibility work, moving together by defense intensity. Values are tokens, mode-aware, and are v0 defaults judged against the photo backdrop in the preview rather than reasoned about (`regular` interpolates thin/thick as a starting point only — it is its own designed row). The alpha is what the component's **own** fill is mixed toward transparent at — material is a fill modifier, never a fill of its own (below):

```
                 alpha (light/dark)   backdrop-filter light                dark
thick            65% / 80%            blur(5.6px) saturate(241.5%)         saturate(208%)
regular          49% / 67%            blur(4px)   saturate(207%)           saturate(195%)
thin             34% / 52%            blur(2.4px) saturate(172.5%)         saturate(175.5%)
dim / scrim      0.18 / 0.32          blur(8px) saturate(0.8)              + brightness(0.9)
```

**This is the JUDGED ladder (2026-08-16, extracted from mounted lab panes — the written lab numbers sat behind two multipliers nobody had ever seen rendered), and it is a different material from the first table this section carried**: near-clear stone, four to six times sharper, because refraction needs detail left to bend and heavy blur erases what it bends. CONTROL-scale material re-prices these cells for a 40px box (lab, ported 2026-08-17): half the blur, a leaner veil, ONE alpha per thickness for every state — config is the one home for every number here, and this table is its summary, not a second home.

Why translucent and not near-opaque: if you cannot sense the backdrop you should have used `solid`. Legibility comes from blur, saturation, brightness — and, since 2026-08-16, from the LENS. Saturation is the material's own defense: its tint has to dominate so image colours cannot bleed into the label. The scrim's 4px only needs to push the app back, not frost it.

**The ~12px blur floor is RETIRED (2026-08-16), and what replaced it is a different mechanism rather than a lower number.** That floor was written for a material whose only defense was frost, and the judged ladder is a quarter of it (2.4/4/5.6) because the lab's direction was near-clear stone: refraction needs detail left to bend, and heavy blur erases exactly what it bends. For one commit the package had the sharp ladder and no lens, which is the only state in which the floor's retirement was a loss — recorded that day as unresolved, closed the same day by porting the lens. The ~30px mush ceiling is retired with it, for the same reason: nothing in the ladder now comes close.

**The LENS (§10, ported from the material lab 2026-08-16).** A glass pane's bezel bends its own backdrop: blur HIDES a backdrop, a lens RE-STATES it, and that is what buys legibility at 2.4px of blur. The map is computed from physics — the method from kube.io's "Liquid Glass in the Browser" (credited; the mathematics re-implemented, not their code): the bezel is a curved glass surface, each pixel's bend follows Snell's law (air 1.0 → glass, 1.45 to 1.62 by rung) on that surface's slope, direction is the outward normal of a rounded-rect distance field, and R/G/B are displaced at slightly different strengths and screened back so the edge splits light. The body stays true; only the bezel lenses.

**The lens is a LADDER, and its bezel is a BAND (2026-08-23).** It shipped as ONE constant, which meant thin, regular and thick differed in blur, in saturation and in veil alpha and bent their backdrop by an identical amount: the one axis where glass most obviously owes a difference was the one axis refraction could not see. Each rung is its own piece of glass now — a wider lip, more depth behind it, a harder split at the edge — and the ladder is held monotone in every lever it owns, the same rule the material recipes carry and for the same reason: thickness has to read as one dimension. The numbers are SOLVED rather than typed: `bezel` and `ior` are the judged shape, and `thickness` is binary-searched until the rung lands on its target bend (7.4 / 13.2 / 23.1px), the mechanism `--accent-label` and the solved edges already use. Regular is deliberately the old constant's strength to within 3%, so the default rung keeps exactly the lens that was approved. `boost` — the sanctioned override of the physics — stays 1 on every rung: this was bought with the model, not with a multiplier past it, which leaves the multiplier where it was designed to be.

**The profile was the larger half, and it was a measurement rather than a preference.** The bezel's surface ran at an exponent of 4, and at that exponent the whole lens lands in the first pixel of the lip: measured, the bend peaked 0.6px in from an 18px bezel and was down to 13% of that peak by the bezel's midpoint and 2% by three quarters. So the file declared 18px of glass and rendered a hard 2px line with a long dead tail — an EDGE TREATMENT, which is what "not glassy enough" turned out to name. At exponent 2 the same physics holds 38% of the peak at the midpoint and 17% at three quarters, and a pane reads as having a thickness instead of a lip; nothing was traded for it, the clamp and the per-pixel cost are unchanged. Generalising the exponent also required carrying the slope's constant factor: `H'(t)` is `P*Q*u^(P-1)*(1-u^P)^(Q-1)` and `P*Q` is exactly 1 at the old exponent, which is why the shipped spelling could drop it and still be right — and 0.5 at the new one, where dropping it would have halved every slope and left `thickness` no longer meaning a length.

**A clamped lip takes its depth with it, and that is what makes the ladder true at control scale.** The bezel is bounded by the box (half the short side is all the room a lip has) while the glass depth divided by that bezel is what sets the surface slope — so a lip squeezed from 18px to 10px on a 24px control kept its full depth and steepened by exactly the ratio it lost. Measured on the shipped constant, a 24px box asked for 15.4px of bend against a 10px clamp: the lens saturated and drew the maximum the box allowed rather than the lens it was asked for. Survivable for one constant, fatal for a ladder — two rungs both pinned to the clamp are one rung — so at 24px regular and thick measured an identical 10.0px before the fix and 7.2 / 8.9px after. Scaling the depth with the lip holds the judged slope at every box size: a small pane bends less in pixels and the same in proportion, which is the argument the generation cap already makes one scale down. The hook takes the MATERIAL now rather than a boolean each caller assembled, so the rung and the two spellings that bend by nothing (`solid`, the seal; `on-glass`, which declares no backdrop-filter for a map to be prepended to) are the ladder's question instead of twelve call sites' — `shell` shipped one arm short of its siblings for as long as that was each caller's job.

It lives in `system/refraction.tsx` and it is the one mechanism in the package that needs the element's resolved pixel geometry — a displacement map is an image the size of the box it bends, so it can never be a token. **This does not breach "no JS at interaction time":** the map is built on mount and on resize, never on hover, press, focus or scroll, which is the same seam the floating layer already measures its own box on — **and never while a pane is FLYING (2026-08-22)**. That sentence was true about hover and press and silent about the one gesture that resizes a pane continuously: the entry animates `inline-size` and `block-size` on the exact element the lens is attached to, so every frame was a distinct cache key and every miss ran the whole solve, the PNG encode and the filter graft synchronously inside it. Measured on one glass menu open: 27 distinct filters installed on a single panel, two after. It could not look right while doing it either, each map being generated for frame N's box and applied on frame N+1's. The measurement is DEFERRED rather than skipped — a flight ends by taking its stamp off, which is a signal — so the pane is measured once, at the seam. Cost is bounded three ways: memoised by (w, h, radius, params) so a list of same-sized panes mints ONE filter, generated at a 320px cap and stretched (the bend is a low-frequency field), and never edited in place — Chromium resolves a `url(#id)` reference once, so a changed box mints a new filter and releases the old. **The map is ASSEMBLED, not solved per pixel (2026-08-24, the performance audit):** the bend takes one varying argument — depth into the bezel — and a card's map asked it 17,924 times for 166 distinct answers while 86% of the pixels were visited and discarded, so the generator now memoises the bend on its exact float argument, solves ONE corner quadrant and mirrors it, fills straight edges one value per depth, and prefills the interior — every float still read off the real field at inputs that provably coincide, held BYTE-IDENTICAL to the frozen per-pixel generator by refraction.browser.test.tsx (measured 5-7× per map; the audit's nine-pane cold screen fell 137 → 58 ms of blocked main thread). **And it builds nothing under `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce`:** the pane computes `backdrop-filter: none` and the hook reads that answer off the cascade — the stylesheet stays the condition's one home; a media listener is only the wake-up for the flip back, which must rebuild because a preference is not a mount. High contrast is deliberately NOT a skip: it hides the glint by opacity while its flip can arrive by a route nothing announces (the app's own toggle stamping `<html>`), so a pane mounted under it keeps holding its mask — the law's negative control pins that choice.

**It is additive by construction, and that is the whole safety argument.** The lens is PREPENDED to the chain the stylesheet already declares, through `var(--kui-lens, )`. Unset — every browser that cannot render an SVG filter in a `backdrop-filter`, and every surface that is not glass — it substitutes to nothing and the declaration is exactly the blur-and-saturate glass that would have been there. The empty fallback is load-bearing and law-pinned: bare `var(--kui-lens)` is invalid at computed-value time when unset, and an invalid computed value takes the WHOLE declaration with it, blur included — the usual two-declaration CSS fallback cannot protect a var(). Order is law-pinned too (bend, then soften; the other order blurs away what there was to bend), and `--kui-lens` is registered non-inheriting, the fourth time that guard has been needed and the sharpest reason for it: a map encodes ONE box, so an inherited lens would bend a 40px button through a 400px pane's bezel.

**Support is measured, and one engine is an open question.** Chromium renders it. Firefox rejects `url()` in `backdrop-filter` at parse time and is safe by that. WebKit may parse it and paint nothing, and because the seam is a var() substitution that case would cost the blur too — it needs checking on a real Safari, and if it fails there the fix is to narrow the support test, never to change the seam.

**The brightness floor is load-bearing and its direction follows the label:** dark label means brightening the backdrop, light label means darkening it. That branch is what lets a material control survive an *arbitrary* photo rather than the demo one. It needs no new mechanism — section 7 already computes this signal as `--accent-contrast` (APCA-derived, "is the foreground light or dark on this fill"), so the recipe reads it. The label never moves; the material adapts around it.

**No vibrancy.** We take iOS's concept (a translucency axis, orthogonal to elevation) and skip the glass simulation. Two recipes, not five magnitudes, because ours vary saturation and brightness *against* opacity to make two characters rather than one dial.

### One scope, not two (2026-08-16, Kushagra — supersedes the clamp above)

The two-scope model died the day the axis was actually built, and the question that killed it is worth keeping: *if the theme says `thick` and a family sits a rung above it, what happens?* Nothing does — **there is no rung above thick.** A ladder-walk per family cannot exist, and reaching for one was the wrong shape.

**Material means what it says: what the app is BUILT of.** A table and a chair made of the same oak are the same oak; they do not get oak+1. So the Theme names one material and everything translucent in the app is that material — menu, dialog, popover, a consumer's own pane. No component picks a thickness, so no component can hit a ceiling.

What makes a dialog read heavier than a menu is therefore not its material but **coverage and the scrim**: the same glass over 900px of application obscures far more than over a 170px menu, and a dialog additionally pushes the page back behind a scrim it already owns. Nothing needed a second thickness to say that.

`solid` is the default and is a material, not the absence of one — the rung where light stops passing through. That is also why the prop is not a boolean: `glass` would still owe a thickness beside it, which is two props for one fact.

The old ceiling is subsumed rather than deleted: `material="solid"` on a Theme forces its whole tree opaque, which is exactly the kill switch the policy reading described.

**Two things stay component-side, and neither is a material value.** *Glass does not stack* — a pane inside a pane resolves solid, because a second pane has no unblurred backdrop left to defocus; that is a fact about nesting, enforced by a scope rather than asked of a call site (§5's note carries the mechanism, including why a `<Theme>` resets it and portals therefore stay glass). And a component whose job needs opacity states that in its own stylesheet, permanently — an identity, the way Card fixed its treatment, never an axis.

**And `layered` does not exist.** It was proposed as a performance switch — "look like glass, cost nothing" — and the audit of 2026-08-16 measured that its sealed composite cannot be identical: it baked a page colour the library has twice declined to own, lost a specificity fight in dark, and could not answer the thickness axis. There is no free glass. Opting out of the cost is the same act as opting out of the look, and that is spelled `material="solid"` on a nested Theme.

### Material is a fill modifier, not a fill (2026-08-04, Kushagra — supersedes the same-day "glass owns the fill")

**Material makes whatever fill the component would have painted translucent at the thickness alpha, and adds the filter.** It never chooses a colour of its own — the first cut shipped a white veil (mixed over the page colour) that erased tone and loudness under glass, and died within hours on Kushagra's question: if the veil can tint from neutral, why not from whatever fill the rung already chose? So the rungs publish their fills as sources (`--kui-fill-src[-hover/-active]`, `--kui-sf-fill-src`), the painted value reads through a fallback chain, and material re-derives every state as `color-mix(in srgb, var(<source>) var(--material-<t>-alpha…), transparent)`. Consequences, all of them simplifications:

- **Both axes survive the glass.** A loud accent button under glass is translucent accent-9; medium is a soft veil; **quiet is bare blur** — the absence of a fill, frosted. Tone and emphasis ride into the veil through the role layer, one rule per thickness, no tone × material product.
- **The rung keeps its own label pairing.** The fill is still the rung's, merely translucent, so loud keeps `--tone-contrast` and the others keep `--tone-label`. Thin-glass-over-a-bright-photo legibility is the deferred brightness-floor branch's job, not a label swap.
- **Interaction steps glass's one ramp — and the ramp is per SCALE since the control cells landed (lab, ported 2026-08-17).** At surface scale each thickness carries designed `[rest, hover, active]` alphas (§8's +1/+2 translated), stepping the veil toward its own seal, monotone per column. At CONTROL scale there is ONE alpha per thickness and the SOURCE moves instead — stepping a 40px veil's alpha laid an opaque pastel over the pane (lab: "hover mode looks weird") — and hover additionally answers in LIGHT: the filter's brightness term moves (`filterHover`), which is legal because the frozen term was always the BLUR — a blur change re-samples the backdrop and shimmers; a brightness change re-lights the same sample.
- **The opaque environments are the fill nearly sealed.** No `backdrop-filter`, or reduced transparency: every state mixes its own source at `--material-opaque-alpha` — a 30% veil defends nothing without blur, so the fallback is the rung's fill at ~95%; quiet honestly stays bare.
- **The surface layer is the same law one level up, and Card is its special case.** The quiet rung's source is the opaque seal, so a glass Card is the seal made translucent — page-coloured glass, which is why Card never visibly changed when the rule generalised. A tone-forward surface (Notice) will tint its own veil the day one floats. Interactive surfaces re-derive their hover/press steps the same way, so a glass card never flashes opaque under the pointer. The derived fills carry `@property inherits: false` guards — a plain card nested in a glass card keeps its seal.

The Theme policy clamp (still open, below) stays what makes this reversible tree-wide: forcing solid returns every component to its opaque fill.

### The glass contract — LOCKED (2026-08-24, Kushagra: "We need to decide and lock what glass means, every element cant act differently when its glass")

The trigger was a measurement: a glass Card and a glass Button wore the conic ring while a glass TextField and TextArea wore the pre-lab flat hairline (`--material-*-edge`, a uniform white 60% border — "the border is weird") and a glass segmented track wore no edge at all. Three renderings of one material is the red flag the lock answers.

**Glass is ONE anatomy, six parts. Every pane resolves all six identically; a component may vary WHERE a part is painted (a platform constraint), never WHAT it is (a value):** *(the headline said "five" while the list below ran six from the day the state vocabulary joined — the number drifted, the list never did)*

1. **VEIL** — the component's own fill mixed toward transparent at the thickness alpha (the fill-modifier rule above; opaque twins under the mix).
2. **FILTER + LENS** — blur/saturate per thickness; the lens prepended through `--kui-lens`, empty-fallback so its absence costs nothing.
3. **EDGE = THE RING** — the per-thickness conic, the ONLY edge: the border computes transparent on every glass element, **`bordered` included** (audit 2026-08-26: `<Button bordered backdrop>` — a composition this section explicitly sanctions — painted the tone's pigment hairline with the ring one pixel inside it, the 2026-08-07 doubled-edge defect; the button's `--kui-ct-glass-edge` hook, dead until then, is `transparent` and `[data-bordered]` reads it, so the pigment returns by itself in the two states that unmake the glass and nowhere else). Two spellings, split by constraint and stated here so a third never appears: panes, buttons and the segmented track paint it as the masked `::after`; **the field family paints it as a background layer under `background-clip: border-area`**, because a `<textarea>` — like every form control — renders no generated content (measured: the pseudo computes and never paints), and border-area clips a layer to exactly the border band, squircle included. Engines without border-area keep the flat hairline as the stated fallback (a conic washed across the whole field is not one). The agreement is law-held: the resolved conic a glass Button's ::after paints must appear verbatim in a glass field's and textarea's background stack, and byte-identical in the track's ::after.
   **The edge is one part with TWO renderings: the crisp 1px lip, and its BAND — the glint (2026-08-24, the "MILES ahead" pass).** Apple's own naming of what separates Liquid Glass from frosted glass is that previous materials *scattered* light where this one *bends, shapes and concentrates* it — and a 1px annulus cannot concentrate anything, which is why the ring kept vanishing at pane scale however its stops were priced. The glint is a band of light lying ON the bezel: its GEOMETRY is a per-box alpha mask the lens hook mints from the same signed-distance field the lens bends with (one bezel, two renderings — the displacement map bends the backdrop across it, the mask lets light lie on it, peaking at the lip and feathering inward), and its COLOUR is the mode's own glint conic resolved at the element, so an appearance flip recolours it with no JS and the light model is stated once. The glint conic is the ring palette with the spectral fold ALWAYS off — the split is the 1px lip's own phenomenon (its stops sit where light enters), and painted across a band thick's red/blue stops read as pink haze on a plain ground, measured on the first render and corrected the same day. Being a plain image rather than a filter, the band renders in EVERY engine: Safari and Firefox get the specular even though the lens never reaches them — the first piece of the glass identity those engines have had. Panes take it on `::before` (the `::after` stays the crisp lip); the button family and the segmented track the same; the FIELD wrapper's `::before` rides `--kui-ct-glass-glint`, declared beside `--kui-ct-glass-ring` and stood down by the same state arms — and a bare `<textarea>` — no wrapper, no pseudo (re-measured 2026-08-24: the pseudo computes, zero pixels paint) — took the band FLAT (Kushagra, same day: "text area still looks old") — an approximation that lived one day and is DELETED (2026-08-25): its first spelling baked a zero width (a theme-scope gradient token substitutes its var()s where it is DECLARED, so the element's own band never reached the paint — "text area isnt fixed still"), its second painted a full-width white bar over any dark backdrop ("so much white thing at top?"), and the ellipse that replaced the bar still could not spell the mask's corner wrap. Three verdicts in one day were the measurement: an approximation of a masked band cannot converge, so TextArea grew TextField's wrapper (the anatomy reversal is recorded at its own entry) and every member of the field family now paints the real band on its ::before. What the platform withholds there is only the corner wrap and the side arcs — the crisp ring carries those. Every ring stand-down reaches the band through the same `--material-ring-opacity` the ring reads — high contrast, reduced transparency and the floating 0.6 trim need no second arm — and the layer is dark by default (`--kui-glint-on` is 0 until the hook measures), so SSR, print and a box too small for a bezel keep exactly the 1px ring.
   **The rim RE-EMITS the backdrop inside the band (2026-08-24; kube.io's chain, credited).** Where the lens exists, the bent backdrop is shown again inside the glint band, hyper-saturated (`glint.rimSaturate`, kube's 4–9 range) and clipped to the band's own mask — so a red photograph's edge catches red, not only white light. Over a neutral page the stage is invisible by construction (saturating grey is grey), which is the pigment ring's own 2026-08-24 finding approached from the other side: the pigment arc draws the boundary where there is no colour to catch, and this catches the colour where there is. It lives inside the lens filter because it needs the displaced result, so it shares the lens's Chromium boundary; the white/pigment band on the `::before` is the every-engine half. **It SHIPS OFF** — judged the day it was built (Kushagra: *"the one thing I have issue with is rim saturation, Id like it off"*): `glint.rimSaturate` is 0, the stage is never built at 0, and the bench's dial is the road back if a later eye wants it.
   **Dark controls carry more specular than dark panes (lab 2026-08-15, Kushagra: "not enough"; ported 2026-08-24).** A small dark pane's rim is most of its evidence, and the shipped ring was the lab's CARD row — the judged "roughly double the card's dark peaks, still under light's" button row never left the lab, so a dark glass button wore a 340px card's faint lip for a week. `--material-*-ring-control` carries it now: dark is config's `ringControlDark` row, light shares the pane's ring VERBATIM from the same emission source (the lab never split light, and a second copy that agrees today is the copy that silently disagrees tomorrow). Law-held in both directions: dark's control catch measures stronger than the pane's by better than half again, light's two rings byte-identical.
   **The lip is LIT, not painted (2026-08-24).** It shipped as white light at every stop in both modes, which is a lip photographed against a dark room: over a photograph it read, over the page it was white on white and a glass segmented control, field and card each had no boundary at all — dark never showed it, because there the catch IS the boundary. A lip is bright where the light reaches it and dark where it does not, so light's ring keeps its catch and takes pigment through the shade arc and the flanks (anchored on `glassInk.light.border`), and it composites over the pane's own veil, so the dark arc reads whatever the backdrop is doing. Held by a law that reads the ring's own STOPS against the page and asks whether any arc is far enough from it to be a line — measured, the shipped defect scored 0.0125 of luma on thin and regular and 0.0773 on thick (its spectral stop, the faint coloured hairline that was the only visible edge in the whole set), against 0.1480 after.
4. **RIM** — the top light catch, one recipe at two scales (pane grain/bloom/sheen; the `-rim-control` variants). Its members are the ring's: panes, the button family, the field family **and the segmented track**, which joined the ring list at this lock and was left off this one until 2026-08-26 — five parts of six, measured at `thick` as `background-image: none` beside a Button's baked grain, which is Button's own 2026-08-16 exclusion reproduced one member over.
5. **STATE VOCABULARY** — a control on or in a pane paints its states in the pane's own currency, and the ranking is part of the contract: **the persistent state outranks the transient one**. The rules key on `[data-material]` — every glass STATE, never the three thicknesses, which is the list meaning *this element IS a pane*: a control inside someone else's glass is `on-glass` and must speak the same vocabulary, or its one piece of state falls back to a solid value and renders white on white (2026-08-24, measured on a segmented control in a glass Card). **And every state moves AWAY from its own mode's ground** — light darkens, dark lightens, both for hover and for the grip — which is the ring's own lesson at fill scale: on a light pane there is no headroom above it to be lightened into, so a white grip is as invisible as a white lip was. A member whose boundary cannot be a border (the segmented well stands its border down by width) takes the ring's annulus filled with the pane's pigment hairline rather than a conic lip, because it has no thickness of its own to claim. Hover is `--material-row-wash` (the value a glass menu lights a row with); a chosen grip is `--material-grip-fill`, carrying more alpha than the wash in both modes. A control inside a glass control may not fall back to the ordinary fill ladder: the opaque-twin re-point (2026-08-19) lives on the element carrying `[data-material]` and those roles INHERIT, so a child control with no material of its own paints the twin RAW — measured on a glass segmented control as a fully opaque near-black hover slab beside a 12% chosen thumb. Fixed in the pane's rules, never by registering the tone roles non-inheriting: a stamped tone must reach a descendant.
6. **MATTER AND THE LIGHT DISCIPLINE** — the pool settles inside the pane; **one lift per pane** (every cast stands down inside glass, the grips' included, 2026-08-24); glass never stacks (a member inside a pane renders its solid appearance at the pane's alpha — `on-glass`).

**The stand-downs are part of the contract, not exceptions to it:** state outranks material (an invalid or disabled glass field wears the state's pigment border, and the ring dies with the edge — both arms stand `--kui-ct-glass-ring` down beside `--kui-ct-glass-edge`); reduced transparency seals the pane and takes the ring with the filter — **on controls too since 2026-08-26**, when that arm was found standing down only the fill, the blur, the flat edge and the cast, leaving a sealed Button's `::after` conic and `::before` band painting at full opacity beside a Card in the same tree that correctly dropped both (it also re-arms the lens hook's own skip, whose condition is `sealed && ringDown`). **`contrast="high"` is NOT one of them since 2026-08-26 (Kushagra: "None of normal contrast appearance reduces contrast. We increased veil, why should there any other difference").** The edge trade — the ring zeroed and a tone hairline handed to every `[data-material]` element through a generated `--kui-glass-hc-edge` arm — lived 2026-08-19 → 2026-08-26 and is deleted whole, mechanism included: its premise ("the ring is white, 1.000:1 on every side", audit 2026-08-18) died when the lip became LIT-not-painted (2026-08-24, two paragraphs up) and the visibility law made the ring a boundary in both modes. Deleting light that paints inside the pane was the rim's own 2026-08-20 mistake one part over. High contrast's whole answer on glass is the VEIL — the `alphaHigh` floor per thickness, and the control-scale floor derived from it (the control cell's designed offset from the surface's resting alpha, carried onto the surface's own floor; a derivation, never a second judged number) — while ring, glint, rim and the control hairline paint byte-identically at both contrasts, law-held from both sides (the emitted scopes carry no ring re-declaration; a mounted pane's light layers compare equal). The track keeps no HC pigment fallback on purpose — its border is stood down by width (audit D2) and the solid track has no edge either.

### Material costs frames, so the levers are architectural

`backdrop-filter` has no cheap implementation; cost is driven by how many elements blur and whether they re-composite per frame. Four rules follow:

- **One glass per stack.** A material control inside a material container is double glass, the same error as double shadow. Prefer the toolbar being the glass and its buttons plain. **Questioned and re-affirmed 2026-08-16 (Kushagra: "generally, glass on glass doesn't be allowed")** — lab2's alert exploration put glass actions on a glass pane deliberately to ask whether the doctrine bends the way iOS 26 bends it, and the verdict is that it holds as the GENERAL rule: no consumer-reachable path may compose it. If the alert's glass actions ever ship, they ship as that component's carved, stamped exception — §6's role-corner shape, a named breach with a reason — never as a loosening of this line.
- **Prefer material on fixed chrome that content scrolls *under*.** A blurred element that itself moves re-samples its backdrop every frame.
- **Honour `prefers-reduced-transparency`** by falling back to opaque. An accessibility requirement and a performance escape in one.
- **`@supports` fallback to a higher-alpha opaque fill** where `backdrop-filter` is unavailable.

Blur radii are provisional until measured on a mid-tier device.

### Placement is the user's; the library only defines what material looks like

Material is only *situationally* correct — over a solid surface it blurs nothing and reads as a muddy smudge. We neither block it (rigidity leaks) nor stay silent (people ship the smudge and blame the library), so: **allow and guide.** Default `solid` everywhere so nobody stumbles into it, document that material is for controls floating over media or content, and add a dev-time nudge for material-over-a-solid-parent, in the same spirit as the double-glass warning. The bad case and the good case both require someone to type the prop deliberately, and the default is always safe.

**Border and material pair naturally.** Both answer "this control is sitting on something busy" — one by containment, one by defense — so `bordered` composes with any material.

**The edge belongs to the material (closed 2026-08-05, Kushagra: blur plus the ordinary card border read "cheap" — the border looked slapped on, and was).** A material is a pane, and a pane owns more than what light does passing through it. On surfaces, three of its parts are now the material's own, per thickness and mode, all v0 for the eye:

- **Edge (re-made 2026-08-17, the lab port):** the tone border on glass was opaque pigment on a pane of light — and the hairline that replaced it was still a LINE. The edge is now the RING: a masked conic gradient on an `::after`, its own 1px annulus wrapping the squircle (border-image cannot follow `corner-shape`), angled to the palette's one light source, spectral on `thick` (deep glass splits light). The border itself is `transparent` per thickness; the ring is the only edge, which is also what ended the double-line defects the hairline kept producing beside it.
- **Rim (three layers since the lab port):** grain (baked SVG turbulence — glass has tooth), bloom (light entering at the top corner), sheen (the wash down from the top edge) — painted as `background-image` layers, never shadow, so the one-box-shadow law stays out of it. **The lifted variant is DELETED (2026-08-17)**: elevated no longer swaps a brighter glint — the elevated world already speaks through cast and pool, and a second rim was the two-homes shape as paint. Solid panes carry their own matte recipe (`--material-solid-rim`: grain + one sheen, no bloom, both depth worlds — a matte slab has no glint to lift).
- **Separation stays the app's** — deliberately NOT the pane's. A first cut welded `var(--shadow-2)` to glass ("a pane floats by definition") and was reversed the same hour: a pane that always floats overrides the flat identity. Rim and edge are what the material *is*; depth follows `depth="elevated"`, so a flat world's glass has edge and glint, no lift.

**Elevation meets the material at two seams (2026-08-07, the four-worlds frame — Kushagra: the interaction "has to be deeper than what it is"; what makes blur read as substance is light responding to it).** Both are config values per thickness, both consumed through the elevated scope, so flat worlds are untouched by construction. **What a pane CASTS is the app's shadow transmitted:** glass passes light, so an elevated glass surface's chrome re-points to the surface row FADED by a per-thickness `transmission` factor (thin passes most, thick least, all weaker than a solid slab) — generator-DERIVED from the palette row, never authored, so there is still exactly one source of shadow truth (the same refutation that killed the bespoke button shadow), and a law re-derives each faded row. The pane's own faded value lives in `--kui-sf-cast`, registered `inherits: false`, and `.kui-surface` reads it BEFORE the world's `--kui-surface-chrome` — the two-name shape the control layer uses for the same problem. That is not a spelling choice (audit 2026-08-07): the glass rules first wrote the faded row onto `--kui-surface-chrome` itself, which is the name the world declares on the Theme element and every surface reads by INHERITANCE, so a plain opaque card inside a glass card cast a third of its shadow and, in dark, lost the rim-light the transmitted row does not carry. Registering that name non-inheriting is the obvious fix and is WRONG — the world's value would then never reach a card at all. The no-blur fallback and reduced-transparency both re-seal the pane and drop its own cast, so the chain falls through to the world's full chrome — a sealed pane stops transmitting. (That arm used to say `inherit`, which reads the parent ELEMENT rather than the world; harmless while transparency-reduction was global, wrong the moment the pane's cast had its own name.) **What a pane catches no longer rises under the sun (the lifted rim retired 2026-08-17 — see the Rim bullet):** one rim per thickness, both depth worlds, and what elevation adds to a pane is cast and POOL — the shade settling at a pane's bottom INSIDE it (lab: matter, not lift, so it joins the cast in both worlds and stands down with the disabled arm and the sealed fallbacks). Mounted laws: elevated thin glass casts exactly the faded row — weaker than the solid card's, still a shadow; flat glass casts none. **The pane parts reach the FIELD family and glass controls transmit (same day, Kushagra: "text field and area was left behind"):** a glass field wears the material's translucent edge (routed through one private name, `--kui-ct-glass-edge`, so the invalid and disabled arms stand it down with one line — state outranks glass) and the rim from the border box; and every glass CONTROL casts the control row transmitted (`--control-chrome-<t>`, faded row 2, generator-derived like the pane's) through `--kui-ct-cast-glass`, an intermediate only casting rungs consume — quiet glass stays uncast — registered `inherits: false` so a hosted control never casts as if it were itself glass. The disabled arm stands down the three per-thickness world names rather than this intermediate, and that is a specificity fact: two of the arm's three selectors outrank the glass rules, but the `:has()` arm — the one answering a field whose own input is disabled — ties and loses on source order, so for a day a disabled glass control cast exactly what its enabled twin did (audit 2026-08-07). Buttons keep no glass edge: borderless by rank. **And one lift per pane (same day, from the preview — buttons on dark glass cast "an extremely dark shadow"):** dark's shadow alphas assume a dark page that swallows them, but a pane swallows nothing — the backdrop shows through and a control's cast lands on it like ink. A material surface stands `--kui-control-chrome` down for its whole subtree (inherited, no selector into the control layer): the pane is the raised thing and its contents sit flush on it, the platform's own posture. The catch stays — it paints over the control's own fill, which glass never changes. Mounted: a loud button inside an elevated glass card casts nothing and keeps its gradient.

**`contrast="high"` leans on the glass, it does not unmake it — true in code since 2026-08-20, prose before that.** Each thickness carries a designed `alphaHigh` triple — more opaque, never fully, so the three thicknesses stay distinct (a first cut reused the reduced-transparency fallback and collapsed them into one). **The row raises a FLOOR, so the rung furthest from it moves furthest** (Kushagra: *"the rule isn't make everything even more high contrast, it's ensure baseline high contrast across"*): thin travels furthest, thick least, the gaps compress rather than stretch, and thick stays glass instead of landing at 94–97% — solid in all but name. **The RIM STAYS.** It used to empty here, on a reason that had outlived itself (the lifted-glint variant it guarded against was deleted 2026-08-17), and emptying it was backwards in its own terms: the rim is a gradient inside the pane, so it cannot lower any contrast, and removing it only deletes the cue that the pane is a physical thing catching light — an accessibility setting may not remove information. What still empties is the EDGE: the ring is white and disappears over a bright backdrop exactly as the veil does, so pigment replaces it and the border goes back to the tone system, which is the one place high contrast can reach it. One line, never two — a ring plus a border is the 2026-08-07 doubled-edge defect. Reduced transparency keeps meaning the near-seal.

### Two surface-only rules controls never needed

**A surface sets foreground context, not just a background.** It colors everything nested inside it. A soft-red Notice sets its text to `--accent-text` (red-11) and muted text accordingly so arbitrary children read correctly; a solid-accent one flips children to `--accent-contrast` (APCA) text. Mechanically the surface re-scopes the foreground role tokens (`--color-text`, `--color-text-muted`, `--color-contrast`) on its subtree. Translucent surfaces have a **legibility dependency** solid does not: text contrast rides on the backdrop, so they need a contrast floor (subtle scrim / min backdrop-darkening) or text fails over busy content. This is where "surface sets foreground context" grows teeth.

**Retracted (2026-08-04): the default surface fill is not alpha — it is the opaque seal.** The paragraph that stood here argued nested surfaces should default to alpha backgrounds so levels auto-differentiate by compositing. Built and judged, it failed twice at once: at the neutral end of the ramp the per-level difference (~1.2%) is invisible — nested surfaces visibly separate by **border**, not fill — and over media the "surface" was a border floating on a photo. The category error, named by Kushagra: translucency is **material's** job, and an alpha default was material's responsibility leaking into the shell. The default fill is now `--color-surface` (opaque, page-coloured, mode-aware). The alpha ramp keeps its real surface job in the tone-forward rungs (`--tone-a3` for Notice's dressing), where the fill carries chroma and is actually visible.

### Interactive surfaces

A clickable card, table row, list/menu item is a **control wearing a container**: it reuses the control state machine rather than inventing a surface one. One machine, two dressings.

The rest state is the surface's own fill, not a ghost — for Card that is the opaque seal, so rest is pixel-identical to a plain Card (a card is a card until you point at it) and the ladder steps the seal: `--color-surface` -> `--color-surface-hover` -> `--color-surface-active`, all three configured per mode. **This paragraph described a transparent-at-rest ghost stepping to `--tone-3` / `--tone-4` until 2026-08-03**, which the 2026-08-04 seal retraction had already falsified in every term: a transparent rest is exactly the fill that vanishes over media, which is what the retraction exists to prevent.

---

## 11. Per-component defaults

Resting position for each component across the four axes. Dash = axis not exposed (the component's nature lacks that question). Emphasis shown as the rung (resolves to the solid/soft/surface/ghost recipe).

**Controls** (size x tone x emphasis x states)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Button | neutral | medium | - | solid | primary CTA is explicit `loud` + `accent`; material only when floating over media |
| IconButton | neutral | quiet | - | solid | subtle, lives in toolbars |
| Toggle | neutral *(default; any family)* | *(refused — the STATE is the rung)* | - | theme's | **SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (§34).** Wears `kui-button` whole: off is the quiet rung, on is the MEDIUM rung of its own tone — the chosen Card's and the selected tree row's vocabulary, not a special accent (this row said "accent when active" from before the selected vocabulary existed; the 2026-08-23 rule that no family paints a faded wash makes the pressed fill grey for every tone with the tone in the word). The one rule of its own is the RANKING: an unpressed toggle's hover is the row family's half-step (`--kui-ct-lit-mix`), so pressed(soft) > hovered. `ToggleGroup` is always multiple; one-of-several is `SegmentedControl` |
| Select trigger | neutral *(stamped)* | *(none)* | - | solid | reads like a field, and since 2026-08-09 (§23) it IS one: it wears `kui-field`, so the fill, the edge, the material and every state arm arrive by membership, with only the two caret facts stood back down. `select.tsx` stamps `data-tone="neutral"` and nothing else — this row said `medium + bordered` until 2026-08-20, and no emphasis or bordered attribute has ever been written on it |
| TextField | *(fixed identity)* | *(none)* | *(always)* | solid | the seal + border; see "TextField is a field" below |
| Tabs | neutral *(stamped)* | quiet | - | - | active tab is INK plus the rule — never accent on the tab itself (corrected 2026-08-20; §26 has stated it correctly since it shipped). The label pair is the TONE-LESS foreground roles, `--color-text` active against `--color-text-muted` at rest, because the ink trio only exists under a stamped `[data-tone]` and tabs refuse tone; only the RULE under the bar takes accent. A louder fill would read as a button among links and a heavier label would reflow the bar on every switch. The bar's hairline is `--color-border`. Material never reaches it: a tab bar paints no fill and no box, so there is nothing behind it to defocus |
| Segmented Control | neutral *(stamped on the root)* | quiet *(on each segment)* | - | solid | SHIPPED 2026-08-18 (§26) as a RADIO GROUP. This row said `medium + bordered … selected segment bumps a step` until 2026-08-20 and was stale three ways: nothing is bordered (the track stands the skeleton's border down by WIDTH — a well has no edge to lose), the track is a WELL — wearing the FIELD's resting gray since 2026-08-24 (`--dress-field-fill`; Kushagra: "I see no reason for it to be any other color than text fields or areas"), while `--color-track` stays the instruments' deeper channel — and outside any dress axis with the switch and the bar, and the chosen segment does not step a rung — it is the GRIP, `--color-thumb` across rest/hover/press with `--color-thumb-label` on it, and it casts ALWAYS on solid ground (the seventh box-shadow consumer) — on or in glass the cast stands down (2026-08-24, one lift per pane), and when the TRACK is the glass the thumb trades `--color-thumb` for `--material-row-wash` under the full ink: the pane's own selection wash, the glass menu's hover value. The root stamps the tone the segments inherit; each segment rests at `quiet`. Takes `backdrop`: the track is the pane, and it scopes its subtree |
| Slider | neutral | - | - | - | track low, fill accent — SHIPPED 2026-08-06: root on the height ladder (the target), thumb = mark(n), a circle (the 2026-08-07 capsule reverted 2026-08-08), track = --color-track at --slider-track-N |
| Progress | accent *(fixed identity)* | - | - | - | the rail with no grip — SHIPPED 2026-08-08: well = --color-track, fill = --tone-solid, ONE thickness (`--progress-track`, **no size axis** — see below), extent from the container, outside the look axis (§19), no target mechanism |

**Binary controls** (tone x states; emphasis implicit)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Checkbox / Radio / Switch | accent (on) | - | - | - | neutral off, accent on — Checkbox shipped 2026-08-05, Radio 2026-08-06, Switch 2026-08-08 (the ON state written once, `.kui-mark:where([data-checked])`; the switch's "neutral off" is the `--color-track` well, still neutral through a role) |

**Inert atoms** (size x tone x emphasis; no states)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Badge | accent *(default)* / destructive | *(loud, fixed)* | - | - | **SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (§38)** — the PLATFORM's badge: the count or the dot that waits on a thing. Bare = a dot (the type requires a name), with content = a pill; both `--badge-box` of the line they sit in with digits at `--badge-text` of it, the tone's SOLID with the contrast label (a loud Button's pairing, law-asserted). No emphasis, no third tone, no position of its own — Avatar takes it through `badge` and owns the corner and the cut-out |
| Chip *(named Badge 2026-08-23 → 2026-08-31; renamed when `Badge` took the platform's meaning, §38)* | neutral | ladder-as-roles | - | - | **SHIPPED 2026-08-23** as the atom family's THIRD member, which is what promoted the shared identity into the type layer (`.kui-atom` — the fill, the corner, the inherited-size arm; `.kui-atom-box` — the one-line box Kbd designed). Body family at `chipScale`, `size` optional (unset = the line it sits beside), FLAT: no edge and no cast, which is the whole of what separates it from a key cap and is law-asserted against a mounted one. The row's old `medium` named a FILL rung and is corrected here for the same reason Code/Kbd's was: emphasis takes the TYPE resolution. **Tone is the category, not the volume** (§29's sentence, one family over) — there are no fill rungs and no `variant` |
| ~~Tag~~ | — | — | — | — | **NOT SHIPPED, and not an oversight (2026-08-23).** A tag is a chip you can remove, and a thing you can remove is a CONTROL: it takes focus, it answers a key, and its removal owes an accessible name. That is a keyboard model, not a dress, so it cannot be a prop on an inert atom. It waits for a forcing case — a real filter bar or a real token input — the way every other unbuilt component here does. A removable chip today is a `Button` |
| Avatar | neutral *(fixed — `tone` was a prop for one day, 2026-08-31)* | *(refused)* | - | - | **SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (§35).** An atom with a picture in it: `kui-type kui-atom kui-avatar`, one line tall (`1lh`) and as wide, a circle at every radius level, the atom fill under initials drawn at `avatarScale` OF THE DISC (not of the type step — the ramp's line ratio falls up the ladder, so a discount on the step filled the disc at step 9). `AvatarGroup` overlaps by `avatarOverlap` of a face with a `--color-surface` ring; a size on the group reaches its unset members because the group is a line with no words in it |
| Code / Kbd | neutral *(stamped)* | ladder-as-roles | - | - | subtle fill — SHIPPED 2026-08-08 as type-family atoms: a FIXED fill, `size` optional (unset = the line it sits in); Code wears the mono slot at `monoScale`, Kbd the BODY slot at `kbdScale` in a centered cap (revised same day — mono drew ⌘ compact); the row's old `medium` named a fill rung and is corrected below |

**Surfaces** (all four axes)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Card | — (shell: fixed neutral quiet + bordered identity, no visual props; `size × material` only) | | | solid | see §10 "Card is a shell" |
| Panel | neutral | quiet | flat | solid | flush (sidebar/inspector body) |
| Notice | neutral | medium | flat | theme's | ONE component, spec'd 2026-08-21 (§29) — the Callout and Banner rows collapsed into it. A condition that is true now, sitting on the region it is about. Tone is the CATEGORY, not the volume, so it rests neutral and the semantic families (success/warning/info) finally have a consumer. It never floats and always takes layout space. Placement is the caller's, which is why it is not called Banner: Atlassian needs two components because their split is *where it sits*, and §3 forbids a component owning its own position. At most one resolving action plus a dismissal that only acknowledges; the dismissal is the app's state, never the component's. An icon slot, empty-safe |
| Popover | neutral *(stamped)* | quiet *(stamped)* | floating | theme's | **SHIPPED 2026-08-23 (§31)** — the floating family's fourth member and the first whose CONTENT the system does not own. The pane is a CARD in its box (the surface band's corner and padding, NOT the concentric arithmetic — that arm moved to `kui-floating-rows` the day this shipped) and a FLOATING pane in its coverage (the cast, in both worlds; it has no scrim to state coverage for it). `size` prices the box alone, Dialog's rule; the title and description are the one sanctioned anatomy (`aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby`) and take the owned type steps. Refused: `modal`, an arrow, free positioning, a trigger-width floor, a drawn ✕ |
| ~~HoverCard~~ | — | — | — | — | **NOT SHIPPED (2026-08-23), and not an oversight.** A card that opens on hover is a hover-reveal, and what a hover-reveal becomes on a touch screen is an open question §17 and §18 both record as belonging to the pointer axis rather than to a component. Building one would answer it for the whole system by accident. The composition today is a `Popover` on a real press |
| Menu / Dropdown | neutral | quiet + bordered | floating | solid | thin/thick opt-in — SHIPPED 2026-08-09 (§22): popup = Card's stamped identity + kui-floating, surface-1 corner (overlay rejected by eye), casts in the ELEVATED world only — flat casts nothing since 2026-08-19 (§22) |
| Tooltip | *(inverted, stamped neutral)* | *(stamped quiet)* | floating | **solid, always** | **SHIPPED 2026-08-23 (§32)**, and this row's exception is now built: the pane paints itself in `--color-text` and writes on itself in `--color-surface`, so it inverts in whichever appearance is in force and `contrast="high"` reaches it for free (by moving the FILL alone — see §32; the ink is already at the palette's end) — no colour is minted and none can drift. It may only RESTATE the name its control announces, which is why it is hidden from assistive technology, why it needs no touch story (§17/§18's hover-reveal question, answered by having nothing to adapt), and why `children` is a `string`. No size axis, no material, no arrow; the delay lives on `TooltipProvider`, because a delay is a property of a region |
| Dialog | neutral | quiet | overlay | theme's | SHIPPED 2026-08-10 (§24): the panel is Card's stamped identity + `kui-overlay` (the overlay band's corner, the designed max width), the backdrop carries scrim + blur, and the panel casts nothing of its own. **Material is the THEME's since 2026-08-16 and this cell said "opt-in" until 2026-08-20**: a floating pane passes `backdrop: true` by construction — a popup always covers the app — so the panel expresses whatever material the app is built of, and "panel stays solid" is now what a `solid` theme means rather than a default the component holds |
| Sheet / Drawer | neutral | quiet | overlay | solid | content scrolls under: strong material candidate |
| ~~Toast~~ | — | — | — | — | **REFUSED 2026-08-21 (§29).** If an action deserves attention, it gets that attention BEFORE the action; if it does not, we do not narrate it afterwards. A toast is important enough to say and too late to act on, which is a category that does not exist. Its two real cases collapse on inspection: undo belongs in an undo stack (Apple owns the gesture at the OS level and can promise it; the web cannot, so build undo rather than rent its shape), and a copy confirmation belongs on the button that copied. The refusal owes a Button done state, recorded open |

**Interactive surfaces** (surface axes + states; reuse control state machine)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Clickable Card | neutral | quiet + bordered | raised | solid | quiet hover overlay atop the raised surface |
| Table *(static)* | — | — | - | - | **SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (§36)** as an inert type-family block: the semantic `<table>` in a scroller, hairlines in `--color-border`, header in the muted ink, cell inset from layout-space per index (the table size join, surfaces.css), type step the identity. Rows do nothing; the interactive row below is the NEXT row |
| Table row *(interactive)* | neutral | quiet | flat | solid | transparent -> hover 3 -> press 4; selected = accent — NOT SHIPPED; a row-family member, a different component from the static table above, never a prop on it |
| List / Menu item | neutral | quiet | flat | solid | active = accent — the ROW FAMILY (§21), shipped with Menu 2026-08-09: rides the control cells; a FLOATING row's box is line + rowInset, a standing row rides the ladder (2026-08-26), emphasis refused, highlighted-not-hover, checked = accent on the indicator |
| Command item | neutral | quiet | flat | solid | |
| Sidebar button | neutral | quiet | flat | solid | active = accent |
| Accordion trigger | neutral *(stamped)* | quiet *(stamped)* | - | - | **SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (§37)**, a row-family member for one day and a CONTROL since 2026-09-01: `kui-control` (not `kui-row` — a heading is pressed, never picked), standing on the height ladder, fill pinned transparent, Link's underline under the pointer, the tree's disclosure glyph in the trailing slot turning on the mark clock; the panel travels by `block-size` on the travel clock and its words start under the label through an accordion size join publishing the row's pill-corrected inset |

**Layout** (spacing only; no visual axes)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Flex / Stack / Grid / Container / Spacer / Shell | - | - | - | - | structural only |
| Box | - | - | opt-in | opt-in | escape-hatch surface; can take surface props deliberately |
| Separator | - | - | - | - | reads a border token |

**Typography** (size x tone x weight; Link adds states)

| Component | tone | emphasis | elev | material | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Text / Heading / Blockquote | any (opt-in) | ladder-as-roles | - | - | *read* the foreground context the surface sets; emphasis picks WHICH role (loud text / medium muted / quiet faint) and a chosen tone re-scopes the ladder onto the family's ink trio (§15) |
| Link | accent *(stamped)* | *(refused)* | - | - | SHIPPED 2026-08-21 (§11, §15): the type family's one interactive member — `.kui-type .kui-link`, not a `.kui-control`. Accent is one of the four named tone exceptions below, stamped rather than omitted because the tone indirection is declared per family. The underline is UNCONDITIONAL (WCAG 1.4.1 — a hover reveal serves no touch screen and no reader who cannot separate the hue from the copy); what moves under the pointer is its colour, resting on `--color-border` and stepping to the ink, which is §7's edge order one component over. **`emphasis` is refused** — the ladder's lower rungs sit at (Lc 60) and below (Lc 30) the reading floor, and a link that has faded is the one element whose job it was to be found; a link that matters less is a smaller link. **`:visited` is refused, and this row named it before the constraint was checked**: browsers restrict what `:visited` may paint and make `getComputedStyle` report the unvisited value to stop a page reading a reader's history, so no law in this repo can read it (ENGINEERING §6). It grows **no target** — SC 2.5.8's inline exception: a target in a sentence is exempt, and §16's expansion exists for a mark with no container, where a link's container is the paragraph |
| Breadcrumb | *(refused)* | *(refused)* | - | - | **SHIPPED 2026-09-01 (§39)** as the type family's second interactive member: a `<nav>` landmark holding one `<ol>`, three ranks of the TONE-LESS foreground roles — `--color-text` on the page you are, muted on the crumbs behind it, faint on the punctuation between them. `tone` is refused because a location carries no meaning to colour (Tabs' own sentence), and `emphasis` with it: the three ranks ARE the design, not rungs a call site picks from. **It is not built out of `Link`** — `Link` rests on `accent` and always stamps it, and a stamped family re-scopes all three foreground roles onto that family's ink trio, so a crumb built out of one could read a family's greys and never the system's. It carries Link's other two refusals by inheritance: not a `.kui-control`, and no target (SC 2.5.8's inline exception). The underline is the one place it departs — resting transparent and painting under the pointer, because 1.4.1's case is a link inside a block of TEXT and a crumb is a whole item in a list |

### Four rules generate every row

- **Material is `solid` for everything** in the defaults — and since 2026-08-16 it is the THEME's single value, never a per-component opt-in (this bullet described the prop until 2026-08-20; the `material` column above is therefore what each row RESOLVES to under the default theme, not a knob each row owns). `thin`/`regular`/`thick` are still opt-in, still correct only over media or scrolling content, and the opting-in now happens twice over: the app states the material once, and a component or a `<Box backdrop>` region states the PLACEMENT that makes expressing it worthwhile. An un-marked in-flow control resolves solid and pays nothing. The Apple-rollback lesson hard-coded into the resting state, and the selectivity rule is the second half of it.
- **Tone is neutral for everything** except the genuine accent-default spots: Checkbox/Radio/Switch (on), Link, and the active state of interactive surfaces. Status surfaces are tone-*forward* by intent but still default to neutral hue until a color is set — and since 2026-08-21 there is one of them, Notice (§29), because Callout and Banner were one component wearing two placement names and Toast is refused.
- **Elevation is deleted (2026-08-03)** — the `elev` column in these tables is historical; no component takes it, and what survives of its logic is the stacking rule: one glass per stack (section 10). **The overlap clause that amended this on 2026-08-09 — *overlap is not expression; a floating layer always states its coverage*, so a floating surface cast in BOTH worlds — is RETIRED (2026-08-19, §22).** In a flat world nothing casts, popups included: the floating chrome is still declared by both worlds, but flat's emitted value is the list-legal no-op layer (`0 0 0 0 transparent`), and a covering pane states its boundary in flat's own vocabulary, the hairline. Elevated is where the clause survives, and there it decides only that a glass floating pane casts the floating chrome rather than a transmitted fade. In-flow surfaces were never affected: flat has always meant none for them.
- **The primary action is always explicit.** No component defaults to `loud + accent`. The loud tinted thing is opt-in so the system guarantees one focal point per context instead of hoping you self-police.

### Progress has no size axis, and the absence is the decision (2026-08-08, shipped with the component)

§9 derives the axis from one question — *has a size step?* — and for a bar the honest answer needed the ladder that already exists to be asked first. It was, and it refused. `--slider-track-N` (4/5/6/7) is designed as **~0.25 of the fine MARK**: it holds a constant fraction of the thumb it serves. A progress bar has no thumb, so riding that ladder would size the bar against a box the component does not have — which is verbatim the sentence §6 used to kill the checkbox's corner (it rode `--radius-control-N` and so held a fraction of a height ladder a mark is not on), and the same wall control padding and the mark ladder each hit before it. Four instances of one mistake is enough to recognise the fifth on sight.

So the shape is **Separator's**, one family over: one designed thickness (`--progress-track`, 6px), extent from the container, no index. 6 rather than the default rail's 5 because a rail carries a grip that lends it presence a bar has to find in its own weight; it stays *inside* the rail ladder's designed range so a bar and a rail still read as the same kind of line.

**Withholding is also the reversible direction**, which is what makes it the right call to make without a taste pass: adding `size` later is additive, removing it is an API break. What stays genuinely open is recorded below — whether a bar earns the index at all, and whether granting it would promote the track into a FAMILY the way the mark family promoted on its third member (`--slider-track-N` → `--track-N`, one ladder, two consumers). That question needs a real screen with a bar and a rail on it, which is exactly what the playground now has.

Two mechanisms it deliberately does **not** have. It is not a `.kui-control`: the skeleton is the interactive one — a height ladder that *is* a target, a button cursor, a label's type, `user-select: none` — so opting in would mean overriding most of what opting in declares, and standing a 44px box on the coarse path around a 6px line. And it grows **no target**: every other §16 consumer does, but a bar answers no pointer and takes no focus, so the box is the paint and the 24/44 floors have no claim on it.

**Indeterminate ships, and it does not touch §8's zeroed-motion law.** That law bans a `transition` — the easing of a state CHANGE, which is what the motion system was deferred to design. A sweeping segment is the other thing entirely: motion that IS the content, the Spinner's category, and therefore the Spinner's reduced-motion answer — *slowed, never stopped*, because a busy indicator that stops moving is information lost. Nothing in the bar reads `--motion-duration` or `--motion-easing`, so the motion system still lands on an empty field.

---

## 12. Token resolution and multipliers

### Two distinct multipliers, keep separate

- `--scale`: global zoom. Affects type, height, spacing, radius.
- The Theme `radius` prop is **not** a factor: it selects a designed palette per level (section 6), so `full` can pill the controls while capping surfaces, which no single multiplier can express.

Fold them into one and you cannot express "square but not shrunk."

```css
--radius-1: calc(4px * var(--scale));   /* value comes from the level's palette */
```

### Which multiplier affects what

```
type              -> scale                        (never density)
space palette     -> scale                        (layout gaps, gutters; density never touches it)
radius palette    -> scale, then the radius set   (never density, never height directly)
control family    -> scale, then the density set  (control-height, control-px, radius-control)
                     control-height and control-px are designed lengths; radius indexes its palette
icon box          -> scale, size and pointer only             (--icon-size-N; its own designed ladder per world, never density — amended 2026-08-10)
icon-label gap    -> scale (via space), size and pointer only  (--control-gap-N; the label cluster, never density — amended 2026-08-04)
layout space      -> scale (via space), then the density set  (--layout-space-N picks; sections 3, 12 — decided 2026-08-04)
surface padding   -> layout space, fixed picks                (--surface-p-N; section 10 — density speaks through the layer)
color             -> neither                      (compiled static; not a runtime multiplier axis)
```

**Density is not a multiplier. It selects a designed set.** Each level (`compact`, `default`, `comfortable`) re-declares the control family — height, inline padding, and control radius — as placed values, emitted at build time under a `[data-density]` block. The icon-label gap left the set 2026-08-04 (Kushagra): density grows the box and holds the content, and type, the icon box, and the gap binding them are one **label cluster** — the gap is size-indexed, re-declared per pointer world (the coarse cluster spreads with its box, section 16), and a law asserts no `[data-density]` block declares it. The icon box joined the same shape 2026-08-10 (section 4): pointer-indexed, density-invariant, which is what the cluster claim implied all along and one of its three members was not doing. Heights and inline paddings are raw numbers per level (padding joined them 2026-08-05, section 3); the radius family moves by a step offset into its palette, with per-cell overrides where an offset lands wrong. Nothing is a product, so nothing resolves to an arbitrary 26.78px, and every cell is correctable on its own — a multiplier can only move a whole level at once, which makes every taste correction global. **Layout space joined the set 2026-08-04** (Kushagra; it superseded, the same day, a morning mechanism that gave surface padding its own per-level sets — one lever, not two): each density level places twelve picks into the untouched space palette (`--layout-space-N`), the default level being the 1:1 identity map. Every layout prop resolves through the layer, surface padding picks from it (re-baked per density scope — substitution-at-declaration, section 6), and the v0 shape shifts steps 1-8 by one while the gutter band (9-12) holds at identity, so "compact must not collapse page gutters" survives as a placed choice rather than a hard rule. Density still never touches the space palette itself; a level only picks different steps from it. The pointer axis never touches the layer (section 16).

**Radius participates.** A large density delta changes visual size enough that a fixed corner reads boxy; section 6's rule that a bigger control wants a bigger corner is about visual size, not about the size index.

**What density never touches:** the space palette, so compact cannot shrink page gutters (the coarseness section 4 rejects in Radix's single `scaling` knob), and type, which is the whole point — density buys breathing room at a fixed label size, where `size` would move the type too.

**One invariant, law-tested:** for a given size, compact < default < comfortable. Nothing orders across both axes; a comfortable size-2 sitting above a compact size-3 is what density means, not drift.

### Strict dependency direction, no cycles

```
raw px constants
  -> base scale (via --scale; radius and control values come from the radius and density sets)
    -> semantic tokens (--radius-control-N, --radius-surface-N, --control-px-N, ...)
      -> variant recipes (soft/solid/...) -> components consume via tone x emphasis
         (user-authored components may consume base directly)

color: OKLCH anchors + curve -> build-time generator -> static --accent-N (+ alpha) -> role tokens -> recipes -> components

Theme props drive the factors and the material at the top. The colour hue is NOT among them:
it is config, baked by the generator at build time (section 7), and no Theme prop re-scopes it.
```

---

## 13. Public token contract

**Both layers are public, with different jobs:**
- **Base scales** (`--radius-0..10` + `--radius-full`, `--space-1..12`, `--accent-1..12` + alpha): the palettes, sampled freely by user-authored components. Public, complete, primitive.
- **Semantic tokens** (`--radius-control-N`, `--control-px-N`, `--accent-solid`, the variant recipes, etc.): the convention, for our components and for users building control-like things.

**Why the base palettes must ship:** once users compose their own components, the palette is the contract, not an escape hatch. A user who writes `8px` opts out of the theme (frozen). A user who writes `var(--radius-3)` or `var(--space-4)` stays in, their component reflows with the theme exactly like a native one. The token sells theme-reactivity that a raw value structurally cannot. The base scale's job is to make the themed path more attractive than the hardcoded one.

**The rule users follow:** consume the numbered tokens and the semantic axes; control them via Theme props. Never redefine a resolved token (`--radius-3: 10px` leaves the system). The `calc()` and the color compilation live inside our layer where users do not touch them. Private `--kui-*` mechanism vars (the responsive remap plumbing, section 2) are not part of the contract: undocumented, unstable.

**`--scale` is reserved, not public.** It stays unprefixed because it is the system's one remaining factor and is intended to become public if it ever earns a Theme prop (section 5); `--kui-*` is for plumbing that never will. Until then it is undocumented and unsupported: set it and you own the fractional values that result.

**Tailwind bridge (optional, sanctioned):** for teams that keep Tailwind in app code, ship a preset mapping its theme onto our variables (`spacing: { 4: "var(--space-4)" }`, colors onto `--accent-N`, …). The threat Tailwind poses to the system is its parallel token scale, not its syntax; with the preset, even `mt-4` written by a defector resolves through the token contract and reflows with the Theme. Our own components never use it.

---

## 14. Build order (first vertical slice)

Architecture is locked; open items are tuning values resolved as the component that needs them is built, not before. Build in this order so every layer and the CSS-size thesis are validated before scaling to the full component set.

1. **Scaffold.** pnpm + Turborepo + tsdown (Rolldown/Oxc) + Lightning CSS + Changesets + Vitest. `packages/ui`, `apps/docs`, shared config. The CI budget gate is wired on day one, even while it measures nothing.
2. **Token pipeline.** Space, radius, and type scales first — static calc chains, a day's work, exercising the whole path (config -> generation -> tokens.css -> measurement) with zero research risk. Then the OKLCH color generator: the long pole; budget it at 2-3x anything else, and test with hostile hues (brand yellow, neon lime, near-black navy) before calling the law proven. **Measure with a two-accent config** (alpha ramps x modes x tones) so the budget covers section 7's full output.
3. **Box + the responsive mechanism.** Variable remap, `@property` inheritance guard, the full prop table on one primitive. **Measure its CSS** to prove O(props x tiers) before any other layout primitive ships. (The blocker-1 proof; needs only the space tokens, so it can overlap the color work.)
4. **Theme + layout primitives.** Theme (scoping the CSS vars, nesting/inheritance) + Flex, Stack, Grid as typed sugar over Box. Proves token consumption and the layout layer.
   - **4b (added 2026-08-02): the pointer axis.** Coarse designed sets in config, generator emits the (family × pointer × density) cells, Theme gains the `pointer` prop, preview gains the coarse matrix (pinned via `data-pointer`), budget re-measured. Laws: the rendered floor holds under coarse (asserted against the box, including under a hostile root font-size), per-world monotonicity, the space palette untouched by pointer (§16).
5. **Button, end-to-end.** Full axis model (tone x emphasis x states x size) on one control, wrapped over Base UI's Button (§1) — the package's first runtime dependency. **Measure its CSS** to prove variant-as-token-remap (additive, not multiplicative) before scaling. Gates cleared 2026-08-02: focus-visible, disabled, loading, icon box decided (§8, §4); motion deferred without gating (§8); coarse v0 numbers accepted as refinable, not blocking (§16); Theme-as-query-container decided yes (§2). **No gates remain.**
   **Shipped 2026-08-03.** Base UI behind the Kookie surface; `size × tone × emphasis × bordered × loading`; Spinner as a one-element primitive (a `<span>` wrapper joined 2026-08-06 — the rotation must ride an HTML element to stay composited; §8, LOG). Measured: **+1,206 bytes gzipped for the whole control layer** (8,105 total against the 40,960 ceiling), of which `button.css` is ~480 — the rest is `system/recipes.css`, shared by every control that follows. The additive claim is asserted structurally as well as measured: a law forbids `button.css` from naming any tone, rung or size, and forbids the recipe layer from ever selecting one axis against another. `material` deliberately did **not** ship on Button — §10's recipes were v0-pending-measurement and step 6 is where they got judged. **Material returned 2026-08-04**, after Card proved the recipes: the control layer gained the material block, Button the prop, and `button.css` still names no axis — material included, law-tested. The first cut (a white veil owning the fill) was superseded the same day by the fill-modifier model (§10 "Material is a fill modifier"): the veil is the rung's own fill at the thickness alpha, so tone and loudness survive the glass.
6. **Card, end-to-end.** One surface, proving elevation + material + foreground-context + alpha-nesting.
   **Shipped 2026-08-03; elevation deleted the same day on first judgment; stripped to a shell 2026-08-04 (§10).** The surface layer (`system/surfaces.css`) is recipes.css one level up: rungs resolved through the seal and the alpha ramp (the opaque `--color-surface` quiet, `--tone-a3` medium, solid loud — the quiet rung was `--tone-a1` until the same-day retraction below), the §10 material table as tokens with the `@supports` fallback and `prefers-reduced-transparency` override in cascade order, and foreground context as `--color-text` / `--color-text-muted` roles re-scoped by the tone-forward rungs. No shadows: the v0 ladder was built, judged by eye, and killed — separation is border and fill, and a law asserts the layer names no shadow and no elevation. **Card ships zero CSS of its own — a law asserts card.css does not exist.** Measured: +426 bytes gzipped for the surface world after the deletion (8,538 total). v0 taste still pending judgment: surface padding (12/16/24/32 on the size index, default 3), material values against the hostile backdrop. Deferred with §10's open list: Theme material policy clamp (naming still open), the brightness-floor branch, the material border edge, dev-time nudges, whether surface padding takes density, the opaque-seal opt-out for a plain surface over media.

This slice exercises every layer (tokens -> variants -> control -> surface) and the CSS budget. Everything after it is repetition across the component set.

**Repetition, first entry — TextField, shipped 2026-08-04 (§9, §11).** The second control, and the additivity claim's first test with something to compare against: **+247 bytes gzipped** (9,574 -> 9,821), against Button's +1,206 for the control layer it created. What is genuinely new is the wrapper pattern and one shared-layer addition (the `invalid` tone remap, §8); everything else was already paid for. Laws: 23 mounted, written to the post-audit bar — computed values only, both appearances — plus structural laws for the invalid remap, the icon-box mechanism, and the two claims that had quietly gone stale (every focus rule reads the ring tokens; every `box-shadow` reads the world's chrome), both mutation-checked.
