Outlined's borders WERE `initial` until 2026-08-17; they now hold real values (surface `transparent`, field/mark the dress edges) — **and that is exactly what broke `contrast="high"`, measured: the request reached NO boundary on any of the three families**, byte-identical to standard in both appearances, because the HC stand-down lands on the element carrying `data-contrast` (`<html>` on the supported path) while a look block's value lives on the NEARER Theme element and wins by inheritance. The fix is look-SCOPED stand-down arms (`[data-contrast] [data-*-look]`, derived from `lookAxes` so a widened axis cannot leave a family behind) that match the Theme element itself and outrank the look block by specificity — the fallback then resolves the solved edges at the element, exactly as before.
- *A look role holds a COLOUR, so it repeats in every appearance scope* — the same rule the surface roles follow, and it was missed for half a day. The fills bake where they are declared, so emitting them only at `:root` baked the LIGHT seal, and every dark region that was not *itself* a look scope inherited a white card, a white field and a white mark. Through `Theme` this is invisible, because Theme stamps both look attributes beside `data-appearance` on one element and the `per-axis look blocks re-declare there — it was the preview's hand-written `data-appearance="dark"` sections, the un-themed path the emitted stylesheet explicitly promises works standalone, that showed it. Both appearance scopes now carry the default look's roles, the `per-axis look blocks come later and win on the co-located element, and two laws hold it: the emission law requires the roles in all three scopes, and a mounted law renders a Card through a **raw** `data-appearance` div and asserts it matches the themed dark render and differs from light. Consequence stated rather than hidden: a bare appearance scope carries the DEFAULT look, so a raw `[data-appearance]` div inside a filled app resets to outlined until it stamps a look. Theme always stamps both, which is why the sanctioned path cannot reach it.
- *State outranks dress:* the shared invalid and disabled arms stand `--look-field-border` down, so under `filled` the error edge and the flattened disabled edge replace the softer resting one rather than competing with it; marks need no arm — their border IS `--tone-border` and the remaps win that battle on specificity. The material also outranks the look on glass: a pane's edge is what the material is, in either look. (The arms were written when `filled`'s resting border was `transparent` and were load-bearing for a different reason then — they kept a state from being *swallowed*. They now keep it from being *muted*, which is the same rule and a smaller stake.)

**Both dressed control families are wired in the shared layer, and the mark family proves why.** The field family's edge rule is `.kui-field, .kui-textarea` in `recipes.css` — named members until a third field-shaped control promotes the family (TextArea's rule) — because a component sheet never mentions the painted variable. The mark family's wiring was written against `.kui-checkbox` the day before Radio and the slider thumb promoted the family into `.kui-mark`, and rebasing it there is the promotion paying its way: **one rule now dresses all three members**, Radio and Slider answer the axis without a line of their own, and a mounted law pins all three to the same computed pair in both looks — the divergence a per-component copy would cause fails there first.

**The slider is OUTSIDE the axis, whole — settled 2026-08-07 after three passes.** Kushagra: *"slider has basically no reason to subscribe to look axis, like button has no role."* The reversals are recorded in LOG because the next value control meets the same fork, and the short version is that each pass argued about the parts and none argued about the thing. The rail was excluded at first on "an edgeless well has no border-versus-fill trade to make", which stopped being true when `filled` stopped being a trade — so it was let in. It was then drawn as an outline, which is `outlined` applied to a path by analogy with surfaces, and it read as a bead resting on nothing. The thumb then left on its own merits, which forced dark's rail down to the page step to stay off the thumb's colour, at which point a filled rail was nearly invisible on its card.

**The rule that survives is about what a slider IS.** The axis dresses things whose resting state is a *surface with a boundary* — a card, a field, a checkbox — because for those, "how does this app draw a resting surface" is a question with an answer. A slider has no resting surface: it is a rail, a fill and a grip. It is an instrument, and every part of it is shaped by what it does rather than by the app's identity. Button belongs to no dressed family on exactly this argument, **Switch landed here 2026-08-08** — its off state is the `--color-track` WELL (§11's own row, decided with the role the day Slider minted it), a channel felt for rather than a small surface read, so the whole control is byte-identical across looks by mounted negative law while its checkbox sibling stays dressed — and **Progress landed here 2026-08-08**, the prediction's third and last standing entry cashed — a bar is a rail with no grip, so it has no resting surface at all, and a mounted law pins every part of it unmoved across both looks in both appearances. The membership test therefore has two halves, not one: a family is dressed because its sheet consumes the roles (the mechanical half), and it *should* consume them only if its resting state is a surface (the design half). The mechanical half alone is what let a slider in twice.

A law pinned every painted part of the slider — rail, rail edge, fill, thumb, thumb edge — unmoved across both looks, with a checkbox in the same app as the negative control so it could not pass in a world where the look axis had simply stopped working. Asserting all five together was deliberate: a component leaves an axis completely or it does not leave it, and a law that checked only the rail would have missed the thumb, which was dressed until the day before. **Those negative laws left with the axis (2026-08-20)** — with no prop there is no second look to flip, and every instrument exclusion this section argues for is structural now.

---

## 20. Portals: re-theming and the stacking frame

**SHIPPED 2026-08-09, groundwork ahead of Menu.** Every floating component to come — Menu, Select, Popover, Dialog, Tooltip — renders its popup at `document.body` to escape clipping ancestors, and that landing spot is outside the Theme element carrying every token. Measured before the mechanism existed: a dark/compact/elevated app's card portalled bare came back **white, at the wrong corner radius, wrong padding, no shadow** — not an error state but a correct-looking light-mode card inside a dark app, the failure that never throws. Two rules close it, both measured in a mounted browser and adversarially reviewed before landing.

### Rule one: portalled content re-themes

Portal content is wrapped in a bare `<Theme>` **when, and only when, a React `<Theme>` is above it (corrected 2026-08-09, audit)**. React context crosses portals while CSS attributes do not, so the wrapper re-resolves every axis from the nearest Theme in the REACT tree and re-stamps the full attribute set at the portal's landing spot. The condition is the correction: an unconditional wrapper resolves from the context DEFAULTS when nobody is above it, and cannot tell *nobody chose an appearance* from *someone chose light* — so on the un-themed path, where the axes are carried on the DOM (`<html data-appearance="dark">`, what the emitted stylesheet promises and what Card's own laws enforce), it overrode all six and opened a white, flat, default-density menu inside a dark, elevated, compact document. That path needs no wrapper at all: `<html>` is an ancestor of `document.body`, so its stamps reach the portal already. `useThemeRooted()` is the distinction, and it is deliberately not exported — a consumer has no use for it. **A blanket "stamp nothing when nobody chose" was tried and rejected**: `:root` carries the default values but cannot carry the coarse-pointer arm, which is emitted as `@media (pointer: coarse) { [data-pointer="auto"] }` — so a root Theme that stamped nothing would lose the whole coarse world on touch. The root keeps stamping; only the portal wrapper stands down. Measured identical to an in-flow sibling across a hostile axis set (dark × compact × large × coarse × elevated × filled), under `contrast="high"` (the `contrastSet` bookkeeping crosses too), and under `appearance="inherit"` with `data-appearance` stamped on `<html>` — apps/docs' own shape. The wrapper is inert around positioned content: full-width, zero-height, steals no clicks. Radix Themes ships this exact mechanism in production, which is the cross-browser evidence the Chromium-only suite cannot supply. The law is an AGREEMENT (the two-implementations rule, 2026-08-06): portalled must compute identical to in-flow, per axis set.

**Rejected: portalling INTO the Theme element** (`Portal container={themeEl}`). It inherits everything with nothing copied — and re-inherits the exact problem portals exist to escape: a Theme on a scrolling sidebar clips the menus opened inside it, and a subtree Theme is a placement this system explicitly sells (§5).

### Rule one and a half: direction crosses too

**Added 2026-08-09 (audit).** A portal drops `dir` exactly as it drops the Kookie axes, and it drops it TWICE — in two layers, which is why RTL needed two mechanisms rather than one.

The **CSS half**: `dir` is a platform attribute, not an axis, so the wrapper Theme had nothing to re-stamp. Inside `<div dir="rtl">` the app computed `rtl` while the portalled popup computed `ltr` — measured identical to the LTR control to the pixel, the trailing slot 82.44px from the row's start edge in both — so every shortcut hint and chevron sat on the wrong side. The **JS half**: Base UI reads direction from its own context (`useDirection()`, defaulting to `'ltr'`, settable only by `DirectionProvider`), which this repo never rendered. So even under `<html dir="rtl">`, where CSS direction *does* reach the portal and content mirrored correctly, submenus still opened to the physical right and overlapped the panel they came from.

**One measurement answers both.** The trigger is the only node a menu owns that stands in ordinary flow, so its computed direction IS the ambient direction — the same read whether the app spelled it `dir` on an ancestor or `direction` in CSS, which no attribute-only check would get right. It is taken once, in a ref callback at commit, and feeds `DirectionProvider` (positioning) and a `dir` stamp on the portal's landing spot (paint). LTR apps never see a state change; the server renders the `ltr` branch the client's first render also produces, so there is nothing to mismatch.

**Not a prop, and `DirectionProvider` is not re-exported.** Direction is ambient, like appearance on the un-themed path: an app states it once on `<html>` or on a pane, and asking every Menu to be told again is the `device` prop's mistake. If a case ever needs the two apart — a deliberately LTR panel inside an RTL app — it arrives as a prop then, with a reason.

**What is mirrored, and what is not.** Only the submenu chevron, because it is the only directional glyph: it points the way the panel opens, and a panel that opens toward the inline end opens LEFT under RTL. One transform on one path, not a second drawing. Row layout needs nothing — the trailing slot's `margin-inline-start: auto` was already logical, which is the payoff for §21 having written it that way.

### Rule two: the stacking frame

The DOM-outermost `.kui-theme` declares `isolation: isolate`. Every z-index inside the app then resolves inside the app's own stacking context, and a body-level portal — a later sibling — paints above it by DOM order, with no number anywhere. Measured before: a `z-index: 50` header inside the theme covered the portal. Measured after: the portal wins; `position: fixed` descendants stay viewport-positioned (`container-type` no longer creates a containing block — CSSWG resolution, shipped in all three engines); the glass is untouched (backdrop blur byte-identical, with `opacity: .99` as the sabotage control proving the instrument could see damage); zero new composited layers; no measurable style/layout cost on an 8,000-element page.

- **DOM-outermost by selector** (`.kui-theme:not(.kui-theme *)`), not a React sentinel: the fact is CSS-expressible, and a sentinel would add a context field for nothing. A portalled Theme wrapper at body level also matches — harmless, it is a later sibling. Nested themes deliberately get no frame: a toolbar Theme becoming a stacking boundary is a side effect nobody chose.
- **`isolation` and only `isolation`.** Radix's spelling (`position: relative; z-index: 0`) has the same stacking effect but also makes the theme a positioning anchor. `opacity`/`transform`/`filter`/`will-change`/`contain` each achieve isolation while breaking something real — a backdrop root under the glass, or a containing block trapping `position: fixed`. A node law pins the spelling; a build assertion proves the rule survives minification (the browser laws read committed CSS, so nothing else covers the Lightning path).
- **The deal, named:** nothing in-app can sit above a portal by z-index — **true of z-indexes INSIDE the theme, which is what the frame contains; corrected 2026-08-09 (audit)**, see the second placement below. That is the point — an open popup is the thing being interacted with — and the escape is symmetric: what genuinely outranks popups (a banner, a call window) portals too, making "above everything" an explicit act instead of a bigger number.
- **Rejected: the z-index ladder** (MUI 1000–1500, Mantine 100–400, shadcn's `z-50`). Call sites memorise numbers, the top rung always breaks, and a numbered vocabulary is the opposite of this system's shape. shadcn needs one only because it inherits Radix's primitives without Radix Themes' frame.
- **Tied, recorded, not solved:** third-party widgets (chat bubbles, cookie banners) at 999999 beat both approaches equally. Legitimate overlays (payment challenges, consent) *should* win; illegitimate ones can't be distinguished mechanically. No mechanism ships for this.

### The two placements the frame cannot survive

**The root Theme must never be rendered onto `<body>` or `<html>`** (`render={<body/>}`, a natural Next.js spelling). Portals land at `document.body`; a theme ON the body makes every portal its own DOM descendant, the portalled wrapper stops matching the outermost selector, and the frame inverts silently — a React sentinel would fail identically, so this is a constraint of portalling itself, not of the CSS detection. A dev-build warning fires on the tag (the Box `container` warning's shape); the JSDoc on `render` names it. Also recorded: a document with no Theme has no frame — un-themed pages get browser-default stacking, honestly.

**And the Theme must not sit inside a positioned element with a positive `z-index`** (added 2026-08-09, audit). `isolation: isolate` gives the outermost `.kui-theme` a `z-index` of `auto`, so it paints at step 8 of the painting algorithm; an ancestor positioned with `z-index: 1+` is a stacking context in the ROOT context at step 9, and step 9 beats step 8 regardless of DOM order. `<div className="relative z-10"><Theme>{children}</Theme></div>` — an ordinary Next.js `layout.tsx` — therefore puts app content above every open popup, with no warning, no failing law and a green `pnpm run ci`. The frame contains the z-indexes that are its DESCENDANTS; it says nothing about where it paints among its own siblings, and CSS cannot reach outside it.

The measured boundary, and the warning matches it exactly: **positive z-index inverts; `z-index: 0` does not** (the shell lands at step 8 too and loses on DOM order — Tailwind's `z-0` is safe), and neither does a stacking context created without a z-index (`isolation`, `transform`, `opacity`). A dev-build warning walks from the frame to `<body>` and fires on exactly that shape, the same answer §20 already gives the body case; the law asserts both directions, including the five safe shapes, because a warning that fires on `z-0` is one nobody reads.

**This is the one case where the rejected z-index ladder would have survived** — shadcn's `z-50` or MUI's 1300 sit in the root stacking context and beat a shell at 10. The ladder stays rejected on its own merits (memorised numbers, a top rung that always breaks), but the rejection argument was incomplete rather than wrong, and recording that is the point of writing it down.

**First consumer: Menu (2026-08-09, §22)** — `MenuContent` owns the re-theming wrapper, and its agreement law (portalled ≡ in-flow, hostile axes + `contrast="high"`) is the enforcement ENGINEERING §2.1's portalling clause names.

---

## 21. The row family: interactive rows in a list (2026-08-09, shipped with Menu)

Menu item, command item, list item, sidebar button — §11 had already named them one family with one sentence (neutral, quiet, active = accent), and this section is that sentence designed. A row is a **control wearing a container** (§10): full width, start-aligned, the one control state machine, riding the EXISTING control cells — inline padding, gap, icon box, font, `--radius-control-N`. Declared family-first rather than promoted on the third member, the mark family's own argument: four separately designed rows in one visual weight class WILL drift, and the spec had already promised they are one thing.

**The row family has two POSTURES since 2026-08-26, and the notch below is the FLOATING row's, not the family default (Kushagra: "menu is exception — row, tree, sidebar, button all stay as is").** The line-plus-inset box below was judged on a MENU and became the family default only because Menu was the family's first member; every standing member since has had to opt back out — the sidebar row on the day it existed (2026-08-21), and the Tree landed on the menu's number and sat 30 in a column of 32s, with a corner priced for the 30 box it did not have (15px of capsule on a 32px row at `radius="full"`, while the tree row beside it was a true pill — the checkbox-corner defect reached through a height override). So the record flipped to match the pattern: a STANDING row (Row, Tree, the shell's nav) rides the height ladder and wears the control band's corner — level with the button beside it, and a capsule of its own box at `full` by construction — while a row inside a floating panel keeps its judged notch and the row capsule. The scope is `.kui-floating-rows`, the panel class whose whole meaning is "hugs rows", which makes the notch set, the row-capsule set and the concentric-corner set (§22) one set by one selector. The nav group's heading keeps the short box by its own declaration — a caption is not a target. Net −1 gzipped byte.

**The floating row's box is its text line plus one designed inset (2026-08-09, Kushagra: the menu read uncomfortably sparse; it shipped for a day on the height ladder).** A button prices its box for standalone pressing; a row sits in a list, and the desktop systems that read right compress rows below button height by dropping the height entirely — shadcn's item is padding + line under an `h-9` button, macOS runs ~24px rows under 28px buttons — while touch systems hold rows at or above the target (iOS 44, Material 48). Both worlds fall out of one derivation: the box is `line-height + 2 × rowInset` (the mark family's own argument — a row's box is a property of the type inside it), and the pointer answer arrives through §17's type bands, which already raise the coarse line. `rowInset` is a designed per-size tuple in the six control sets (per size since the same day: a constant inset read "a bit cramped" at size 4 — air grows gently with the index, flat at sizes 1-2, +1/+2 at the top, so the ratio still falls as type grows): fine default `[4,5,5,6]` renders **24/30/34/38** against buttons at 28/32/40/48 (size 2 re-judged 28 → 30 the same day: the full 4px cut read too aggressive) — the shadcn/macOS one-notch-down relationship — and coarse default `[8,8,9,10]` renders **36/40/44/48** against 36/44/52/60. There is **no invented row TARGET**: coarse size 2 sits a stated 4px under the 44 its own button carries (the cost of tighter rows on touch), exactly as the height ladder's own size 1 sits under the target. The locked 24 is a different number and the family does not clear it by design everywhere — fine + compact + size 1 derives 20 — so since 2026-08-26 the notch floors the painted box at `--target-min` (§16). Exactly one cell needs it, and the set is law-pinned in both directions: a second cell arriving on that floor is a design decision, not a default. The spelling cedes the border term (`padding-block: calc(var(--kui-ct-row-inset) - var(--border-width))`, the inset joining the size join's stems, the checkbox target's audit lesson) so the rendered box is exactly line + 2 × inset; `min-height` stands down from the height ladder to `var(--target-min)` on source order, the family's own tie mechanism (it was `auto` until 2026-08-26, which stood the §16 floor down with the ladder). Air grows gently with the index rather than tracking the ladder's 12/12/16/22 (whose empty fraction ROSE with the index — the fraction wall's shape, in the vertical): 4/4/5/6 keeps the ratio falling as type grows, which is how line-height itself behaves.

**Emphasis is refused on rows (Kushagra, 2026-08-09).** Button carries emphasis because actions rank; a menu is a list of peers, and emphasis-as-differentiator inside a list names nothing — the TextField sentence one family over. Quiet is the family's fixed identity, STAMPED by the component (`data-emphasis="quiet"`, Card's stamped-identity precedent), so rest/hover/press arrive from the quiet rung with nothing re-declared.

**The lit row is `data-highlighted`, never `:hover`.** Base UI unifies pointer and keyboard into one highlight; a row that also answered raw hover would keep a pointer-rested row lit after the keyboard moved on — two cursors on one menu. The family styles the attribute, and a guard-block rule stands the shared hover fill back down on rows (inside the one `(hover: hover)` guard, so touch synthesis stays handled). **It wins on SOURCE ORDER, not specificity — corrected 2026-08-09 (audit)**: `:not()` takes the specificity of its most specific *argument* rather than summing its list, so the shared rule and the stand-down are both (0,3,0) and the later one wins. Same mechanism the checked rule uses one block up, and now pinned by a node law, since the arithmetic was standing in for a guarantee nothing checked. `[data-popup-open]` lights a submenu's trigger row by the same rule. Press stays the shared unguarded `:active`.

**Selected speaks accent, written once — and the INDICATOR is what speaks it** (reversed 2026-08-09, Kushagra's eye; it shipped for a day as whole-row `--accent-label` ink). `--accent-label` is a TEXT ink — it lives between ladder steps 11 and 12 because prose owes the APCA body floor — so a checked row read as dark navy emphasis while a checked checkbox one family over fills with the bright solid: two selected vocabularies for one question. A glyph owes only the 3:1 non-text floor, so the tick wears the real `--accent-solid` (`.kui-row:where(:not([data-disabled])) > [data-slot][data-checked]`), the mark family's own ON value — the label keeps saying WHAT while the tick says CHOSEN, every platform's spelling. The surviving precedence fact: the accent is named directly rather than through a tone role, so the disabled arm's remap cannot reach it — the `:not([data-disabled])` on the row stands it down, and a dead tick falls back to the dimmed inherited ink. Naming the accent family in the shared layer is the focus ring's precedent (§8): selection answers one question system-wide.

**Rows wear content dress: regular weight, stated by the family (Kushagra, 2026-08-09, same day).** The control skeleton's medium is a BUTTON-label decision, and it reached every row through `kui-control` — the TextField value's sentence one family over (a value is content; so is a line in a list you read). Every platform menu agrees. Stated once in the row block; a member that ever needs medium re-declares it as its own fact.

**A trailing slot sits one inset from its three nearest edges (added 2026-08-16, Kushagra: "the top, right, and bottom must match for kbd").** The row's inline padding is the control family's `px`, priced for where TEXT starts; its block clearance is `rowInset` — two different numbers, so a boxed chip in the trailing slot (a Kbd, an indicator) sat equally off the top and bottom and visibly farther from the end. The slot's end margin cedes the difference (border term included, the checkbox target's lesson in its third home), so the clearance is the row's own inset on all three sides — and at `radius="full"`, the default, that equality is exactly what makes capsule-in-capsule CONCENTRIC: the panel corner's sentence (§22), one nesting deeper. The leading side deliberately keeps `px`: it aligns the label with every slotless row, which is the reserved-gutter fact. Law-tested as the sentence itself — three measured clearances, one number — with the text padding as the negative control.

**What varies per member is designed picks, never props** — and Menu's pick is "the control family's own cells", deliberately: inventing per-member numbers before the eye pass is a value nobody judged, and re-pointing four join lines later is the reversible direction. **What is never shared is behavior**: a menu item is roving focus and typeahead, a sidebar item is a link, a select option is a listbox option — the family shares paint, never machines (the Checkbox/Radio cut). **Both open questions closed 2026-08-20 with the family's SECOND member, `ShellNavItem` (§27).** The public face: the row is a package export, so nothing in kookie-blocks has to reach the recipe by hand. And the "current" state is **two stamps and no CSS** — `data-tone="accent"` plus `data-emphasis="medium"`, a fixed identity the component states rather than an axis a call site chooses (Card's precedent; the emphasis refusal above is about ranking peers in a list, which this does not do). That pair is what keeps current and hover in DIFFERENT CURRENCIES rather than on one ramp: quiet rests transparent and hovers to soft, medium rests at soft and hovers to soft-hover — so grey meant the pointer is here and accent meant this is where you are, and a hovered current row still has somewhere to go. **The colour half of that sentence is RETIRED (2026-08-23):** `undilutedTones` sends accent's washed roles to neutral, so a current row's fill is now the same grey a hovered row takes — measured byte-identical — and what says "you are here" is the accent LABEL plus `aria-current`. The two-currency mechanism survives at the EMPHASIS level (medium rests where quiet hovers, so a current row still moves under the pointer); only its pigment went. The collision is pinned by law in `shell.browser.test.tsx` rather than tolerated, so buying the distinction back with a fourth rung has to re-open the decision. The first spelling painted the hover fill straight onto `[aria-current]`, which read correctly at rest and computed byte-identical when hovered; its own law caught it. **The member also does NOT take the family's height stand-down** — it stands level with a Button, law-asserted against a mounted one at every size (the segmented control's law verbatim). The menu row is the shifted member and its reason — a panel opened for a second, scanned, dismissed, with no buttons anywhere near it — does not reach a permanent column that sits beside real buttons all day, so this costs no new designed number: `min-height: auto` is a stand-down, and this member simply does not take it. **Hover is restated here too**, because §21's stand-down exists for a ROVING highlight (Base UI folds pointer and keyboard into `data-highlighted`, and a row answering raw hover would stay lit after the keyboard moved on) and a sidebar has no roving highlight at all — without the restatement the row is dead under the pointer, measured. Stated as this member's own fact rather than by re-scoping the shared rule: the second member self-keys, and a third non-roving row is what promotes it.

**The family's STANDALONE member shipped 2026-08-23 — `Row` — and the open question about what lights a row closed with it.** Three members existed and the fourth did not, so a list that was not a menu and not a sidebar had nowhere to go: apps/docs' own search rendered its results as full-width quiet Buttons with a comment saying it was standing in for this, and the builder's command palette painted its highlighted row `var(--neutral-a3)` by hand — a copy of the family's lit fill that had already drifted, because that fill grew material remaps and a high-contrast arm while the copy stayed one flat step.

**`Row` ships no stylesheet at all** (Card's limit case, one family over) and no per-component class either: every other member wears one because it is a PART of something — `kui-menu-item`, `kui-select-item`, `kui-shell-nav-item` — and a standalone row is a part of nothing. It is `kui-control kui-row` and that is the whole of it. It takes the STANDING posture (the height ladder, since 2026-08-26 — the notch is the floating row's), stamps quiet like every row, and carries `tone`, the slots, `current` and `disabled`. **Net −17 bytes gzipped**, because the promotion below deleted more than the component added.

**What lights a row is now a decision made once per LIST, and the marker is on the row.** §21's open question guessed at "a Kookie-owned lit attribute the family stamps, with Base UI's two mapped onto it at the Menu boundary", and that is not what the shape turned out to be. The distinction is not per COMPONENT — the same `Row` is roving inside a command palette and not roving in a settings list — so it cannot live at a component boundary at all. It is per INSTANCE: `Row` stamps `data-hover-lit` when nobody else is driving it, and stops the moment a caller states `highlighted`. The shared layer then carries one rule, `.kui-row[data-hover-lit]:hover`, which wins on SPECIFICITY (0,4,0 against the stand-down's 0,3,0) rather than on the source order the stand-down above it depends on and its own comment calls fragile.

**The exception marks itself, and the direction matters.** Answering the pointer is what every control in this library does; a row that must not is the special case. Gating the stand-down on "is this roving" was the other spelling and it is wrong for a reason worth keeping: a menu LABEL is a `.kui-row` that is not a control at all, so gating would have handed hover to every row that never asked, headings included. An opt-in marker leaves anything that says nothing inert, which is the safe default.

**`ShellNavItem` GAINED `leading`/`trailing` AND NOW STAMPS ACCENT ALWAYS (2026-08-23, Kushagra: *"row and sidebar row render their icons in accent color always"*).** Apple states the same rule in as many words — *"By default, sidebar icons use your app's accent color… people expect all sidebar icons to appear in that color"* — and it is the one place a system's colour reads as identity rather than as emphasis, because a sidebar icon IS the item. The row had no slot at all before this (raw children), so the ask was an API addition rather than a colour decision; the props are `Row`'s and `Button`'s verbatim, and the icon box, gap and trailing clearance all arrive from `.kui-control`/`.kui-row`.

**The unconditional stamp was unsafe until the day it was written**, and that is the dependency worth recording: `data-tone="accent"` on every row would have painted a column of pale blue fills, and only `undilutedTones` (§9, same day) makes a stamped row resolve neutral at every washed rung so that just the glyph and the ink arrive in colour. **The LABEL is stood back down in `shell.css`** — `--color-text` ordinarily, `--tone-ink` under `[aria-current]` — because a column of blue words is not a sidebar, and because the label is now the whole of what says "you are here": the fill no longer distinguishes current from hovered (see §9's stated consequence). Keyed on the ANNOUNCED attribute rather than a private stamp, so the painted state and the announced one cannot disagree. **`ShellRailItem` deliberately does not follow** — it is not a `.kui-row` and has no slot, so its icon keeps carrying current, which is right for a control whose icon is its only channel.

**The icons-always-accent half is REVERSED 2026-08-26 (Kushagra, holding Finder over the docs sidebar: "make resting icons neutral not accent" — then, judging the first cut in both appearances: "the icon color and label not matching bothers me. In dark mode I prefer the icon color, in light mode the text color"), and the two asks resolved into one rule: A ROW IS MONOCHROME.** No slot carries a colour of its own — the leading icon, the words and the trailing punctuation all inherit the row's one colour, and the STATE decides which colour that is. At rest it is the label ink (a plain row neutral, a destructive row red on icon and words together, a dead row dimmed whole through the remap); on the row that IS the current place (`aria-current`) it is **`--tone-current`** — a new generated role, ONE value for icon and label so the mismatch is structurally impossible, resolving per mode through config's `currentInk` pick: the family's INK in light (the rich dark blue; light's glyph is nearly the solid and reads thinner than the words), its GLYPH in dark (the saturated mid-blue; dark's ink is a pastel). The per-mode pick is the focus-ring/invalid-edge pattern, and its stated cost is written in config: dark's glyph is solved to the non-text floor (45), under the reading floor — accepted for a short, triply-announced nav label on the quiet-rung precedent for stated carve-outs. The diff against the platform is what decided the reversal: when every row is accent, accent stops meaning "you are here" (the HIG sentence the 2026-08-23 rule cited describes the tinted-icon default, not selection; Finder shows what selection needs). The first cut kept a slot-specific glyph rule and the label arm made it redundant — deleted rather than left: inheritance IS the mechanism, and the menu tick stays the family's one slot-coloured exception. The accent stamp survives (the `[aria-current]` arms resolve the family through it); the same-day chromaCurve change is the other half — the resting ink carries full chroma, so family survives at rest and only volume is reserved for the current row. `ShellRailItem` states its own `[aria-current]` arm (not a `.kui-row`) and resolves the identical value. The load-bearing law detail, learned from a sabotage that survived: `--tone-current` ≡ ink in light, so only a DARK run can tell the role from its candidate — the icon/label laws loop both appearances for exactly that reason.

**The family's slot wrapper promoted with it** (ENGINEERING §3): `slot()` moved to `system/render.ts` on its FOURTH consumer, one past due — Button, MenuItem and Row each carried a byte-identical private copy under a comment saying "exactly as Button and MenuItem write it", a duplication that had already noticed itself.

**`ShellNavItem`'s private `:hover` rule is deleted** — the promotion §21's open question predicted in writing ("its author's likely response is a `:hover` rule in a component sheet, which is exactly the per-member drift the family-first argument was made to prevent") and which `shell.css` had been carrying since 2026-08-16 with its own expiry beside it ("the second member self-keys, and a third non-roving row is what promotes it"). Two shell laws that had encoded the old state came due with it: the sanctioned-`@media` set went from three forms back to two, and the "the shell paints no bed" law's second exemption died exactly as its comment said it would.

---

## 22. Menu (2026-08-09)

The first floating component: the §20 groundwork's first consumer and the row family's first. **The part vocabulary and composition shape follow shadcn/ui's dropdown-menu (MIT), adopted with credit** — Root/Trigger/Content/Item/CheckboxItem/RadioGroup/RadioItem/Group/Label/Sub(+SubTrigger/SubContent), flat exports (`Menu`, `MenuTrigger`, …; a namespace export would silently escape the coverage laws). Behavior is Base UI's menu end to end.

**The corner is CONCENTRIC — row corner + panel padding, derived per (size × radius level) — and it took three spellings in one day to get there (Kushagra's eye, all three).** `--radius-overlay` shipped for an hour (24px is priced against a DIALOG's box — the fraction wall's seventh instance); the fixed `--radius-surface-1` that replaced it "judged perfect at large/size-3" and was then caught matching ONLY where row corner + padding happened to land on it (medium × sizes 1-2, large × 3 — broken at every other cell, because the row corner moves with BOTH the size index and the radius level while a surface pick moves with neither; the same day's open note had already predicted the size half). The rule is Kushagra's sentence verbatim: "menu item radius + padding = container radius" — the two curves share a centre in every cell by construction, `full` included. **`full` needed a corner of the row family's own, added 2026-08-09 (audit).** The clause here used to read "the control band states the capsule as h/2 and the panel wraps it exactly", and that was the fraction wall's EIGHTH instance hiding inside the fix for its seventh: a row does not ride the height ladder, so half `--control-height-N` is half a box it does not have. Measured, the panel declared 18/20/24/28 against rows the browser can only paint at 12/14/17/19, missing in **21 of 24 cells by up to 9px — wider than the panel's whole padding** — while the law agreed with it, because the law read the same declared numbers the CSS did. `--radius-row-N` states the row's own capsule (line + 2 × inset, halved) at `full` and is the identity at every other level, so nothing else moved. Mechanism: the popup stamps `data-size` (reversing the ship-day refusal, whose real target was the surface join's padding pick — the FLOATING SIZE JOIN in surfaces.css re-states the padding as it derives the corner, `.kui-surface.kui-floating[data-size]` outranking the surface join by specificity), and the panel's padding crosses through the `--kui-floating-p` hook (the `--kui-sf-cast` shape: the system declares the fallback, the pane's sheet supplies the value), so the next floating pane inherits the derivation whole. Concentric arithmetic is undefined at zero — `none` would round a square panel by exactly its padding — so a guard states none means none. The law reads BOTH boxes' browser-resolved corners and asserts the sum — and since 2026-08-17 it re-prices through the squircle knob, because **under squircle the drawn corners are deliberately NOT concentric**: the panel's paint is the authored sum × its knob (1.75) while a row's corner is unscaled (controls never take squircle), Kushagra's own by-eye call. The authored DERIVATION (row + padding) still holds and is what the law asserts; the drawn divergence is the knob's, stated in §6. The overlay band returns to unclaimed; Dialog is its real first consumer.

**`MenuContent` is the fold**: Portal → bare `<Theme>` (§20) → Positioner → Popup, so no call site assembles plumbing and the re-theming cannot be forgotten. The Popup is a surface wearing Card's exact stamped identity (neutral, quiet, bordered, `material` opt-in — a glass menu is the case the blur machinery was built for) plus `kui-floating`. It STAMPS `data-size` — see the concentric corner two paragraphs up, which derives from it; this sentence said the opposite for a day, in the same section that recorded the reversal, and following it would have left a panel wrong in 14 of 16 size-and-roundness cells (audit 2026-08-09). The panel's padding is one designed value (`--floating-p`, a layout-space pick re-baked per density) — its ROWS answer the index. Public positioning vocabulary: `side`/`align`/`sideOffset` with designed defaults (bottom/start/4); everything else is a designed default, not API. Width: `min(max(--floating-min-w, --anchor-width), max(--floating-min-w, --available-width))` — never narrower than the designed floor or the trigger that opened it, and never wider than the room — **and the anchor half is scoped to the ANCHORED panel, corrected 2026-08-09 (audit)**. (The viewport cap arrived 2026-08-22: in CSS a minimum beats a maximum, so the `max-width: var(--available-width)` this section already claimed as "the hard stop" could never apply, and select.css had carried the repair alone since 2026-08-09. Measured with `side="right"` on a 900px trigger: 951px painted at x=967 in a 1280 window, 638px off screen with the items unreachable.) The anchor term is the entry's own `--kui-anchor-w`, the trigger's LAYOUT box, and it OUTLIVES the flight — see §22's entry. (The two token names were `--menu-p` / `--menu-min-w` until Select promoted them to the family; this paragraph kept the old spellings for a day while the section below announced the rename, so the spec gave two opposite present-tense answers eighteen lines apart. Writing either dead name into a rule computes 0px for the padding and 0px for the floor — and because an undefined custom property invalidates the whole declaration, even a written fallback is discarded.) It shipped on the shared popup rule, and a submenu's anchor is not a trigger but its trigger ROW, which is `inline-size: 100%` of the panel it sits in: the floor silently became "a submenu is never narrower than its parent panel", and it compounded — measured 446.59 → 437 → 427 across three levels, a child panel holding one character 427px wide. No platform implements that rule and §22's Button-shaped argument never contemplated it. The floor's own sentence is what fixed it: it means the thing you PRESSED, and only a top-level popup has one.

**The submenu's seam is MEASURED, not designed (corrected 2026-08-09, audit).** It shipped as two hardcoded numbers — `sideOffset={0}`, `alignOffset={-4}` — under a comment saying the second "must track `menuPadding`". It cannot: `--floating-p` is a layout-space pick that moves with density (2/4/8), and a JS constant does not move with anything. The panel's border was never in the arithmetic at all. Measured, the child's first row aligned with its trigger row in NONE of the six density × pointer cells, and "sits flush against its parent panel" was false in all of them — the child overlapped the parent by padding + border. Both offsets are now one reading of the parent panel's own top padding and border, taken at position time through Base UI's function form, so a density change tracks live. Read off the DOM rather than re-derived from the token, because the padding is `max(--floating-p, ring reach)` and a constant that reconstructs the author's arithmetic is the audits' own lesson one layer down.

**Menu takes `size` like Button (Kushagra, reversing the withhold proposal): a size-4 button must not open a size-2 dropdown.** The root provides the index through context (it crosses the portal); every row stamps it on its own element, where the size join fires. The trigger is `render={<Button/>}` — the caller matches the two size props; the menu cannot read its trigger's.

**~~The popup casts in BOTH worlds~~ — the overlap clause is RETIRED 2026-08-19 (Kushagra: "in flat world, nothing will cast shadow, including menus and dialog"), and flat now means flat for every pane.** The clause it replaces (2026-08-09: *overlap is not expression; a floating layer always states its coverage* — flat kept a half-faded cast under popups, `floatingFlatFactor` 0.5) was written when a flat world had NO other way to bound a covering pane; it retired the day after flat regained the hairline (2026-08-19, "no light means the line is back"), because a menu panel is a surface and now draws its boundary in flat's own vocabulary — the faded cast had become a second voice saying the same thing. What stands: the **floating chrome** is still the third chrome role and both worlds still declare it (the pointer chain is one mechanism), elevated still reads palette **row 3 since 2026-08-16** (a cast is priced by the size of the box throwing it; dark adds the inset rim), and flat's emitted value is the list-legal NO-OP layer — a value, never `none`, which poisons fallback chains. `floatingFlatFactor` is deleted. The paint still re-points the surface layer's ONE cast site (`.kui-surface.kui-floating`, last in the sheet), the count law holds, and a glass floating pane in flat now casts nothing like everything else — "coverage outranks transmission" survives only in elevated, where it still decides that a glass menu casts the floating chrome rather than a transmitted fade. Laws: the emitted no-op in both appearances (falsified against the restored fade), and a mounted flat menu whose every computed shadow layer is invisible, with the elevated popup as the negative control. The glass-transmission × floating product question closes with the fade: in elevated the answer stays "the floating chrome, unfaded"; in flat there is nothing left to ask about.

**Rows carry the vocabulary; the surface stays neutral.** `tone="destructive"` on an item — a union of ONE, resolved by the existing tone indirection with zero new CSS; a wider palette on rows is a decision, never a default. Checkable items ship day one because they ARE the family's selected state; their indicators (the checkbox's own stroked artwork) stay mounted, so the gutter reserves in both states — which is most of shadcn's `inset` answered by geometry rather than a prop (the rest — plain items beside checkable ones — is recorded open).

**Refused, and the refusals are the API** (registry carries them): `Shortcut` (a keyboard hint is the trailing slot holding a `Kbd`), `MenuSeparator` (Base UI's is a re-export of the standalone; use `Separator` — menu.css supplies only its rhythm and its inset, and it spans the LABELS: full-bleed until 2026-08-09, the rows' own extent until 2026-08-31, each move the same argument one layer further in on Kushagra's eye ("I need it to extend to where text labels start and end"). A line divides what it is as wide as, and what a reader groups is the words — a row's wider box is its highlight, which is a different fact, exactly as iOS table views and Material's inset rule have it. The inset is `--kui-sf-row-px`, the rows' own pill-corrected text padding, published to the pane by the floating family's size join because a separator is the rows' SIBLING and never inherits `--kui-ct-px-pill` from any of them; the anchor is a bare-edged row, which is where the group label starts too, and a checkable row's label sits one indicator further in by §21's reserved gutter), `Arrow`, `Backdrop`, `Viewport` (as API — the panel itself never scrolls; since 2026-08-17 a system-owned ScrollArea viewport inside it carries the overflow, the padding and the height bound, and the caller still cannot reach it), `LinkItem` (waits for Sidebar), collision knobs, `modal`/`openOnHover` (designed defaults). Motion: the panel unfurls out of a seed and dissolves on dismissal (§8 — Menu is the motion system's first floating consumer; the control layer joined it 2026-08-09).

**The entry is not settled, and what is open is named (2026-08-10).** Two faults co-occur on every menu opening from a trigger near a right edge. The first is fixed: a block child lays out from its container's inline-start, so an END-aligned panel's growing edge and its content's origin are the same edge, and every row travelled the panel's whole width on the way in — measured at 175px against a start-aligned panel's 21px. The body is now pinned, for the whole flight, to the edge the box is *not* growing from (absolutely, not by auto margin: an auto margin resolves to zero when the available space is negative, which is the entire flight). The second is **open**: sampled frame by frame, an end-aligned panel reports `data-align="start"` for its first several frames and flips about a third of the way in — correctly, because a 40px seed does fit beside the trigger and a 209px panel does not. **Collision is being decided against the animating width**, so the panel re-anchors mid-flight with the wrong origin and the wrong lean before it. The candidate fix is a change of mechanism — animate a `clip-path` inset rather than the box's size, so the panel holds its final box from frame zero, collision is decided once, and the body pin becomes unnecessary — and it waits on a judgment, because a panel whose real box (with its backdrop-filter and its shadow) exists at full size from the first frame is a different thing on screen.

**The registry grew a `parts` concept with this component** (the one law amendment): a compound component's exports are explained inside the parent's entry rather than on stub pages — the coverage law accepts either home, holds part blurbs to a floor, forbids a name living in both lists, and proves its own parse against Menu's ≥10 parts.

---

## 23. Select (2026-08-09)

The floating family's second member, and the ship whose real content is what it did NOT need: the fold (Portal → PortalScope → Positioner → Popup), the row family with its line-box height, the concentric corner (the panel stamps `data-size`, the floating size join derives), the floating chrome in both worlds, the reserved indicator gutter and the accent tick — every one arrives from §20-§22's mechanisms with nothing re-designed. Two promotions happened ON the second member, both argued from the audits' own pattern (a mechanism living in one file and never promoted): the §20 machinery — PortalScope, the ambient-direction measurement, the two-layer RTL answer — moved to `system/floating.tsx` (a JS mechanism with laws promotes on its second consumer, the render.ts precedent; the CSS second-member-self-keys rule is about paint and stands), and the panel tokens renamed to the family (`--floating-p`, `--floating-min-w` — a select consuming a menu-named token would be the two-homes drift wearing a component's name). The family's one selected rule widened to Base UI's second spelling (`data-checked` on a menu indicator, `data-selected` on a select indicator — one rule, two vocabularies).

**The trigger is a FIELD-shaped button, and membership is worn, not copied.** A Select's job in a form is the native `<select>`'s: it stands beside TextFields and must read as their sibling — so the trigger wears `kui-field`, and the seal, the field edge, the look axis, material and the family's state arms all arrive from the field family's own rules (the third field-shaped control promotes the family — TextArea's recorded rule, honored by membership). select.css stands the two caret facts back down at (0,2,0) (cursor, selection — a trigger is PRESSED, not entered), and the field's focus-mode ring is inert by construction (keyed on an input holding focus; there is no input), so the skeleton's `:focus-visible` ring applies: a trigger's focus is a keyboard affordance. The value wears content dress (regular — the TextField value's sentence), an empty trigger shows its `placeholder` in the muted role (an empty select INVITES, §15; faint until 2026-08-10), and the chevron is a muted trailing adornment that points down, which no direction mirrors.

**The value machinery is the half a menu does not have.** Base UI renders the hidden input (`name`/`required`/Field validation), so a Select submits with a form like the element it replaces; `role="combobox"` opens a `listbox` of `option`s — the a11y contract a styled menu cannot fake, and why the two are different components. `items` (value → label) exists because Base UI resolves labels from MOUNTED options and a closed panel has none: without it, a select whose panel never opened displays the raw value string. The chosen row stays marked (`data-selected`), its mounted indicator reserves the gutter and wears **`--accent-glyph`** — the accent's ink, not its fill (this said "the accent solid" until 2026-08-26; the row family moved to the glyph role on 2026-08-23 because `--accent-solid` measured |Lc| 43.4 against dark's page under the floor of 45 a tick is held to, and the two are materially different colours in dark). A dead chosen row's tick dims through the row's own stand-down.

**Positioning is the system's, whole, and the CHOSEN ROW SITS ON THE TRIGGER (2026-08-17).** No `side`/`align`/`sideOffset` props: the designed offset, start-aligned, always anchored — a select has no submenus, so the width floor (`min(max(--floating-min-w, --anchor-width), max(--floating-min-w, --available-width))`) lives on the panel itself, its anchor term being the entry's own `--kui-anchor-w` — the trigger's LAYOUT box, kept for the panel's life rather than handed back at release (2026-08-22: floating-ui keeps re-measuring the anchor as the held press travels, so a floor handed over at release is handed to a number still in motion; measured `--anchor-width` walking 400 → 388 → 390 while the panel sat at 400, then a 10px snap one frame after it had visibly stopped). `alignItemWithTrigger` was pinned FALSE on 2026-08-09 and is Base UI's own default again, which is the macOS and Radix placement: the panel overlaps the trigger so the chosen row lands exactly on the value it replaces, and the rest of the list falls above and below it. Base UI drops the overlap by itself for a keyboard open and near a viewport edge, stamping `data-side="none"` when it is live. Still refused: the scroll ARROWS that pair with the mode — a mouse-only affordance for a list taller than its panel, where wheel, trackpad and keyboard already work, and an arrow is a control nobody here has designed. Also refused, with the reasons in the registry: emphasis/tone on the trigger, `SelectValue` as a part (a prop, not an element), `render`/`children` on the trigger, `multiple`.

**The panel hangs LEFT of the trigger, and that is the placement working (2026-08-17, asked and closed).** Item-aligned means the chosen option's TEXT lines up with the trigger's value text, not that the two boxes share a left edge — and our rows reserve a gutter for the tick that the trigger has no equivalent of, so lining the text up moves the panel left by the difference. macOS does the same. It was raised as a suspected bug and kept as designed (Kushagra: *"the lean doesn't bother me, I wanted to ensure it's not a bug"*). The alternative is recorded rather than left implicit: Base UI falls back to aligning the left edges when there is no `Select.Value` element in the trigger, which would mean rendering the value ourselves and taking over label resolution — the price of a straighter column, if a real shell ever asks for one.

**The entry is the family's, unchanged — what moves is where it LANDS, and the ordering that forces.** A select flies out of its trigger exactly as a menu does (§22's silhouette); the difference is that the box it flies into straddles the trigger instead of hanging below it. Base UI computes that overlap from the panel's REAL box, so the panel must be PLACED before it is POSED — the runner's plan carries `placedByContent`, and the entry waits for the box to hold still rather than posing on the mount frame. Nothing is lost by waiting: the pose is transparent until it is aimed. Two consequences are load-bearing. The family's pose rules are keyed on the FLIGHT (`data-unfurling`) and not on the visibility gate (`data-seed`), because a select wears the gate through the placement window and a pose applied there shrinks the box the placement is measured from — 66px of misplacement, of which the body's squish alone was 58. And the flight BORROWS the inline `height: 100%` Base UI writes for an item-aligned panel, restoring it at release: an inline declaration beats every rule in a stylesheet, so with it in place the entry's block-size channel is simply dead.

---

## 24. Dialog (2026-08-10)

The floating family's third member and the first one anchored to nothing, which is what makes it a section rather than a paragraph: everything §20-§23 built assumed a trigger to measure and an edge to open from. **The part vocabulary follows shadcn/ui's dialog (MIT), adopted with credit** — Root/Trigger/Content/Title/Description/Close — and behavior is Base UI's Dialog end to end: focus trap, scroll lock, dismissal, the a11y wiring.

**A dialog is a Card that covers.** It wears the surface identity whole — seal, edge, look, material — and its padding steps ONE SIZE UP through the overlay join (promoted from the alert's own arms 2026-08-17, Kushagra: "dialog seems to have less padding than it should"; the reasoning was never alert-specific — an overlay wears the corner of the card one size up, and a bigger curve wants a bigger inset or the words crowd the sweep; size 4 holds at the top pick exactly as the corner band's does) — so the only things this component designs are the three a card never needed: the scrim, the viewport, and which band its corner comes from. It carries a second family class (`kui-overlay`) exactly as an anchored panel carries `kui-floating`, and the shared layer's overlay size join answers it with the overlay band's corner and the designed maximum width. **What it must NOT wear is `kui-floating`** — that class means an anchored pane hugging control-scale rows, and it delivers two things a dialog wants neither of: the concentric corner (row corner + panel padding, and a dialog's content is prose) and the floating cast.

**It takes `size`, and the index prices the BOX alone (Kushagra, 2026-08-10 — reversing §6's "a dialog has one size").** Width, padding and corner move with it; the type inside does not, because no surface in this system sizes its children's type. `DialogTitle` and `DialogDescription` therefore state §15's composition steps — the card-title step and body copy in the muted ink — which is also what makes a dialog and the playground's confirm card the same typography by construction rather than by two people picking the same number. The width is a MAXIMUM (`--overlay-w-1..4`, one designed raw-px ladder, density- and pointer-invariant: how wide a modal should be is a reading-measure question and neither axis is asking it); the viewport's gutter wins when the window has less room, which is what keeps a size-4 dialog usable on a phone.

**The scrim is minted, and it is deliberately not a material (§10).** The recipe has sat in §10's table since 2026-08-04 and had never been emitted: black at a designed alpha per mode with a 4px blur, and dark leans harder because a 40% veil over a near-black page moves almost nothing. It is not a member of the material ladder and does not resolve through it — a material defends a FOREGROUND's legibility by making that component's own fill translucent, while this pushes the whole app back so the thing on top of it is unambiguously the thing you are using. Its blur sits ABOVE every material rung since the judged ladder landed (8px against thick's 5.6 — re-priced 2026-08-17 with the lab port): the ladder went near-clear, and a scrim's job reversed with it — thin glass stays clear so structure ghosts through, while the scrim defocuses the whole app to push it back. Desaturation joined as a lever the same day (`saturate(0.8)`, dark adds `brightness(0.9)`): a backdrop drained of colour recedes further than a merely darkened one. The old below-the-thinnest ceiling was never load-bearing, and its law now records that. `contrast="high"` and `prefers-reduced-transparency` share one answer — more pigment, no defocus.

**And the scrim is the separation, so the panel casts nothing of its own (Kushagra: "no cast, but needs to respect material").** §22's overlap amendment said a floating layer always states its coverage — that clause retired 2026-08-19, but the dialog's own argument never rested on it: a dialog states its coverage with the entire viewport going dark, so a second mechanism would be saying it twice. What the panel does keep is the world's ordinary surface chrome, by the same inheritance every card takes it by — lifted in an elevated app, flat in a flat one — and a law asserts exactly that: a dialog's shadow equals a Card's in both worlds, and never the floating chrome, with a menu in a flat world as the negative control so "no shadow anywhere" cannot pass for it. `material` reaches the panel untouched: a glass dialog is glass, and the dim behind it does not move with the pane in front of it.

**The anatomy is the one case §10 reserves.** Title and Description are parts because something non-visual forces them (`aria-labelledby` / `aria-describedby`), and nothing else is: Header and Footer are Card's own cut re-argued (layout, not anatomy), and the corner ✕ is the same positioned slot refused for the same reason — Escape and an outside press dismiss, and `DialogClose` puts a real Button wherever the composition wants one, which is also what a touch screen-reader user needs to leave a trapped panel.

**Two mechanisms fell out of it being the first unanchored member.** Ambient direction (§20) was measured off the trigger, and a dialog need not have one — `<Dialog open>` driven by app state is ordinary — so with nothing measured the hook rested at its `ltr` initial value and PortalScope STAMPED that, overriding the `rtl` the portal would otherwise have inherited: the runtime-switch fix's own sentence (a stale stamp is worse than no stamp) reached by a different road. `useAmbientDirection` falls back to the document element, which is what the portal would inherit anyway. And the panel is centred by AUTO MARGINS inside Base UI's scrollable viewport, not by `align-items: center`: a centred flex item taller than its scroll container overflows equally in both directions and the top becomes unreachable, so a long dialog would lose its own title.

**Refused, with the reasons in the registry:** `modal` and `disablePointerDismissal` (designed defaults — an open dialog IS the interaction; a panel that leaves the page live is a Popover or a Sheet, and one that cannot be dismissed by pressing outside is an alert, a decision rather than a flag), `initialFocus`/`finalFocus`, AlertDialog, and a `size` on the title. **The entry is DEPTH, NOT DISTANCE (locked 2026-08-16; the materialization it replaced is AlertDialog's, §25).** The recipe that formed here first — a circle of surface rising from below the card's footprint and unfurling into the measured box — was judged at the lab's half-screen and 88vw masses to be an ARRIVAL: travel, overshoot, a point origin, an interruption announcing itself. Correct at alert mass, wrong here, because a dialog is SUMMONED — opened by the user's own hand — and at modal mass that inertia reads as the room moving rather than as a thing arriving. So the panel comes into focus, not into view: **3% in z on the `poised` spring (ζ0.8 — one crossing at ~1.5% of the travel, damping's whole allowance spent on the approach, because mass forbids overshoot), zero travel in x or y, presence as paint on its own faster clock.** The scrim pushing the app back IS the arrival (§10), which is what buys the panel the right to move almost not at all. **The content shares exactly one channel with it and nothing else: blur → sharp, on the box's own clock.** Depth of field is a property of the mass — a page does not come into focus after the page does — and blur is the only channel that presumes nothing about arrangement, which is what makes it the one animation the system may honestly apply to content it does not own (the alert's molten hold and top-down print are legal there precisely because that anatomy is system-owned). The four large-mass principles the lab settled, kept because they predict the next heavy surface rather than describing this one: mass lowers frequency, mass forbids overshoot, mass shortens travel — until at the limit the environment moves instead of the object — and mass softens onset. **Mechanically it is the lightest entry in the system: no measurement, no pose runner, no release clock — Base UI's own transition stamps carry all of it, so a dialog's whole motion is CSS.** The body wrapper exists only to hold the blur (§10's mechanically-forced sanction, Spinner's `<span>` and the floating body's argument). Its own clock family (`dialogMotion`), and the ABSENCE of every size channel is asserted by law, because the absence is the design. The exit is the family dissolve with `scale` still listed, so a dismissal mid-arrival retargets the running spring instead of cancelling it. Reduced motion stands the whole thing down — including the content's focus, since a blurred body is a worse failure than any motion. Values are v0 for the eye pass: the depth, the blur, and both clocks. **A size-1 dialog carries a smaller title than a size-4 one — closed 2026-08-21 (Kushagra), and §24's last open question with it.** The rule "no surface sizes the type inside it" is intact, because the title and the description are not inside it in the sense that rule means: they are parts the SYSTEM owns, forced into existence by the a11y wiring, and everything the call site wrote is untouched. Ownership is the same argument §25 used to let an alert price all of its own content, applied to the two parts a dialog owns. The description moves with the title — a smaller dialog says its supporting line smaller too, or the pair stops reading as a pair. **The steps promoted to one home the same hour** (`OVERLAY_TITLE_STEP` / `OVERLAY_BODY_STEP`, system/floating.tsx): an alert and a dialog at the same index must be the same typography, and two copies of one type map is the drift this repo keeps finding. Size 3 is the anchor and holds what both components already shipped, so the change moved every index except the one anybody had judged — law-asserted against a mounted Heading and Text, never against a number. **The other half — whether a long dialog scrolls its own body rather than its viewport — closed 2026-08-21: BOTH, and which one is the call site's word.** State a height (or a max-height) on `DialogContent` and the body scrolls, with the title and the action row staying put; state nothing and the panel grows and the viewport scrolls it. The plumbing shipped the same day (§3's dialog-body clause), and the DEFAULT stays the viewport, Kushagra's call when the alternative was put to him: "it's fine if it scrolls with the page". So a dialog does not cap itself at the window even when it holds a scroller, which is where every peer differs — and the reason it can afford not to is that the page behind it is locked, so the second scrollbar is the panel's own, never the document's. +295 bytes gzipped, all v0 values on the eye-pass list: the width ladder, the gutter, the scrim's two alphas and its blur, and the overlay band's picks.

---

## 25. AlertDialog (2026-08-16)

The overlay family's second member, split from Dialog the day the motion work proved they are different things (LOG 2026-08-16): a dialog is SUMMONED — opened by the user's own hand, holding the consumer's work — while an alert comes AT you and stops you. Base UI ships the pair separately for the same reason (`role="alertdialog"`, no outside-press dismissal), and dialog.tsx has carried the refusal "a decision, not a variant" since §24 shipped. **The part vocabulary follows shadcn/ui's alert-dialog (MIT), adopted with credit:** Root/Trigger/Content/Title/Description/Cancel/Action.

**The fixed anatomy is licensed by three forcers stacking — §10's criterion, which Card and Dialog each failed differently.** The role wires a name and a description. The behavior forbids outside-press dismissal, so actions must EXIST and initial focus must land on the safest one. And the entry animates the content itself, which the system may only do to content it owns — the exact reason Dialog could not keep the materialization. The cascade is the design: role → closed content → closed box → owned motion. If the slots are ever opened up, the content animation leaves with them.

**`size` prices EVERYTHING — the box, the corner, the padding, the title's and description's type steps, and the two buttons — where Dialog's size stops at the box.** The same index, a wider reach, and the difference is ownership: no surface sizes the type of content it does not own, and an alert owns all of its content. The steps walk §15's composition ladder (size 3 IS the confirm card: title 6, body 3), law-asserted against a mounted Heading rather than against a number. This also closes §24's open question for the alert's half: yes, a smaller alert carries a smaller title.

**The width is the component's alone: a designed FIXED width per size (`alertWidth`, 280/320/360/400), no prop, strictly narrower than the dialog of the same index at every step (law-tested).** Fixed rather than a maximum because closed content means the box does not stretch to it — the component states the width and the words wrap — which is also what holds the action row's shape. The ladder is published through the shared overlay size join re-pointing `--kui-overlay-w` (the switch join's shape one layer up), so alert-dialog.css consumes the same name dialog.css does and nothing size-indexed lives in a component sheet. The viewport's gutter still wins on a narrow window. **The padding steps one size up with the top held** (same day, Kushagra in lab2: "the padding is less") — the corner band's own sentence one property over. **Promoted to the whole overlay family 2026-08-17** (the dialog was judged under-padded for the same reason), so these arms went back to owning only the width.

**The component owns the layout — the one place in the system a surface arranges its children.** The entry's body is a two-column grid: whatever is not a button spans it, the actions take a column each — equal space, filling the row — and a lone Action spans whole (the acknowledgement alert). Cancel first in the DOM is the whole focus mechanism: it reads first, sits at the start (RTL flips it free), and Base UI's first-tabbable rule hands it initial focus — the APG's "least destructive action" answered by document order. **Cancel and Action are real Buttons the component prices, with no `render` escape**: Cancel MEDIUM — the standing end of a rung that moved four times in one day (quiet+bordered → medium → bare quiet → medium): the near-solid dark reading that killed medium's first run was the LAB glass veil's compositing, not the rung's, and when one-glass-per-stack was re-affirmed and the lab examples returned to plain buttons, the objection went with the dressing — on a plain pane, bare quiet is an absence at rest and medium is visible-but-subordinate, which is what the safe action was always asked to be. A rung means one thing system-wide: if dark's medium ever reads heavy on shipped surfaces, the fix is the rung's dark value at the eye pass, never an alert-local reprice — and Action LOUD — the one place loud is a legal default, because an alert has exactly one Action, so §11's one-focal-point rule holds by anatomy instead of self-policing. Action carries `tone` (destructive is most of why this component exists) and the caller's `onClick`; both close, because an alert's job ends when a choice is made.

**The materialization is this component's entry, moved whole (LOG 2026-08-16).** The recipe re-keyed from the family class to the alert's popup, the OverlayBody runner promoted to system/floating.tsx (a JS mechanism's second consumer — the render.ts/PortalScope precedent), and three motion laws moved with it, re-targeted. Dialog is entry-less by sequencing until its large-mass entry lands in the follow-up commit; the recipe was never ownerless.

**Everything the Dialog audit found, this component had too — closed 2026-08-21 in the same change.** Measured, not assumed: `role="alertdialog"` with `aria-labelledby` null and `aria-label` null, and `aria-label`, `id` and `data-*` all dropped by a Content part that declared four props. The two were built the same week by the same author, which is exactly when a defect is copied rather than inherited. All four repairs are the family's now, promoted on the second consumer (the render.ts rule — CSS lets a second member self-key, a JS mechanism with laws behind it promotes): the open-change reason and its `cancel()`, the missing-name warning (which NAMES the component it fired on, because a shared hook that always said "Dialog" would send an alert's author to the wrong file), the clipping warning, and the composition steps. **One reason union for both**, because Base UI declares one — `AlertDialogRootChangeEventReason` IS `DialogRoot.ChangeEventReason`. An alert simply never emits `outside-press`, since it refuses that dismissal: an absence at runtime, not in the type, and a shorter union here would assert something the primitive does not say.

**A dialog presents as a SHEET on a narrow window (§18, §24 — 2026-08-21, Kushagra).** He made the argument: *"the drawer that Shell built is exactly what we need — there a panel not floating, floats on narrow devices. We're using the same definition for dialog, no?"* He is right, and it had been written down as a Shell fact when it is a system one: **presentation is dress, not a component.** One element, always mounted, and the window decides how it sits. The dialog is the EASIER case — a shell pane has to be in the layout at one width and floating at another, which nothing can be at once, while a dialog is floating either way, so this is only position and shape and nothing is swapped, remounted or lost. That matters more here than there: a pane that remounts loses a scroll position, a dialog that remounts loses a half-filled form.

**The boundary is the system's one definition** — config's `narrowMedia`, from `windowClass.narrowMax` — the same 48rem the narrow type band and shell.css use, with two node laws: one derives the literal from config, one asserts dialog.css and shell.css resolve at the SAME width, which is the claim a reader cares about.

**No prop**, on §24's own designed-default argument (the one that refused `modal`): the window class answers "which interface should this app show", so a call site choosing to stay centred on a phone is choosing against the answer the system just gave. It widens the day a real screen forces it.

**Three things the narrow arm must state, each forced rather than chosen.** The gutter goes, because a sheet is the window's width. The panel CAPS at `calc(100% - var(--touch-target-min))` — the Shell's own expression and its own reason: pinned to the bottom with no maximum it runs off the top with no route back, and a strip of scrim must stay tappable. And the BODY scrolls, because the cap obliges it — a pane clips (§3), so a capped panel whose content overflowed would delete it, which is the defect closed the same day walked into again by a rule written hours later. The corner is the overlay band's at the top and nothing at the bottom, since the bottom edge is the screen's. **A first for the package: `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`**, because a sheet pinned to a phone's bottom edge puts its last control under the home indicator otherwise; it resolves to nothing everywhere it does not apply.

**AlertDialog does NOT do this, and the exclusion is the design.** Every platform keeps an alert centred: it is a question that stops you rather than a surface you work on, and its narrower fixed width is already the phone-shaped answer. Law-asserted as a negative control — without it, "a dialog becomes a sheet" would pass equally well written as "every overlay becomes a sheet".

**It carries NO motion, and the absence is deliberate (Kushagra, same day: "we will design a separate motion system for sheet, so what it has right now is wrong").** §24's entry is depth-not-distance, and every word of that argument is about a CENTRED panel at modal mass — the scrim pushing the app back is the arrival, so the panel need only step forward in z. A sheet does not arrive that way; a 3% scale on a box already resting on the bottom edge reads as the wrong gesture rather than a small one. Both halves are stood down — the clock AND the pose — because with no transition the browser still paints one frame at the starting values, which is a 3%-smaller blurred panel popping into place. Menu's precedent: shipped instant, moved the next day. The scrim keeps its fade, which is not an exception — the backdrop's recipe is about the app going back, not about how the panel arrives.

**The ring was being cut, and the cause was a promotion nobody writes down (Kushagra, by eye).** `overflow-y: auto` beside a `visible` inline axis resolves that axis to `auto` too, so the body became a scroll container on BOTH axes — and a scroll container clips at its own padding box. With the padding on the popup, the body's box ended exactly where the field's did (measured 0px of clearance) and every focus ring inside a sheet was sliced at both edges. The panel's padding moves onto the body in the narrow arm, in the shared layer's bleed spelling, so a ScrollArea still reaches the edges by the same arithmetic. This is the menu's own 2026-08-17 repair and the general rule the card work stated a day earlier — **a clipping box must pad at least the ring's reach** — reaching its second consumer.

**Open: the corner.** The sheet inherits the overlay band whole, which paints 77px on a 390px-wide sheet — judged on desktop at 560px and never at this width. It waits for the eye pass, with the sheet's own motion. **What a sheet does not do is DRAG** — swipe to dismiss, pull to a half height — and that is the same trade Shell deferred: a gesture is JS on every pointer move, against §2's no-JS-at-interaction-time, and it is what a separate Drawer component would be for. Base UI ships one (stable since its 1.3), and using it here would mean rendering a different component at narrow, which is exactly what §27 refused.

**The boundary with Dialog, stated once:** if it needs any control beyond its two buttons — a type-to-confirm field, a checkbox, a form — it is a Dialog. An alert's only task is choosing. Escape still closes (a keyboard "not now" is Cancel by another route); scrim, viewport and centring are §24's, self-keyed (the second member self-keys; the third overlay member promotes them). Refused, with reasons in the registry: a width prop, outside-press dismissal, Header/Footer, `render` on the actions, arbitrary children. Open and recorded: whether the action row stacks vertically when labels run long (iOS does; decided by eye when a real case forces it). 17 mounted laws including the four moved motion laws, six sabotage passes; the width ladder and the type-step maps are v0.

## 26. Tabs and the segmented control (2026-08-18)

Two components that look alike and are not the same thing. **Tabs switch what the page shows; a segmented control sets a value in place.** §11 has always priced them apart — tabs quiet with an accent active state, the segmented control a track with a raised selected segment — and they share no CSS. The confusion is worth stating in one place, because reaching for the wrong one is the actual mistake: if the thing under it changes, it is Tabs.
