- **Indent per level is a DESIGNED number** per size, stated INSIDE the row (padding, not margin), so a row keeps `m="bleed"` full-width paint and the press target spans the pane — §27's own sidebar geometry.
- **Rows are row-family members** (§21): the paint, the posture (STANDING since 2026-08-26 — a tree row rides the height ladder like the nav row beside it; only floating panels keep the notch), the hover rule and the size join all arrive by membership; Tree contributes structure and machine, never a second row recipe.

### The internal architecture is part of the spec

**The visible nodes render as a FLAT list** carrying level attributes — the ARIA-sanctioned spelling of nesting on flat DOM, and the architecture Headless Tree independently proves out. Three things fall out of it rather than being built: rows are ordinary row-family members with no nesting CSS, `m="bleed"` stays trivial (no ancestor between row and pane), and virtualization has a door to arrive through if a consumer ever forces it. Nested DOM (`<ul><li><ul>`) is REJECTED: it puts ancestors between the row and the pane (the bleed breaks), and it makes indent a property of structure instead of a designed value.

### Refused, with reasons (the recorded edges)

- **Drag — wrong encapsulation, not never.** Reorder-by-drag is its own pattern (it applies to flat lists as much as trees), the accessible-reorder answer is genuinely contested (THESIS §7's caution: a default is only shippable where research is settled), drop semantics are the app's data model, and it would be the largest "no JS at interaction time" exception yet. When it lands it lands as its own encapsulation composing with list AND tree; React Aria's keyboard drag mode is the researched reference. The builder's Layers drag stays app code until then.
- **Cascade selection** (choosing a folder chooses its children; the tri-state shape): product-dependent, no consumer here does it.
- **Async children** (`aria-busy`, placeholders): no consumer.
- **Virtualization**: a DOM-contract change; the flat list keeps the door open, nothing walks through it yet.
- **Rename-in-place**: the instrument's behaviour, i.e. the block's.

### The dependency survey (2026-08-26, so it is not re-run from memory)

Base UI has NO tree (confirmed in the installed 1.7.0; open request mui/base-ui#3327, nothing shipped) — waiting is not a plan. TanStack has none (Table v9's expanding rows are a data-grid model: hierarchical data, zero tree ARIA, zero tree keyboard). React Aria's Tree is the most rigorous and ships accessible drag — REFUSED as a second runtime dependency with its own collection/focus architecture beside Base UI's ("the package's one runtime dependency is Base UI", LOG 2026-08-26). Headless Tree (react-complex-tree's successor) is the best shape fit — renders nothing, flat-list interface, plugin features — REFUSED for now on maturity (beta) and the same one-dependency identity; it survives as the architecture's existence proof. **Self-authored, Shell's precedent**: at this scope the machine is disclosure + keyboard + selection over a flat list, inside what this package has already proven it can own and hold with laws.

### The build gate, and how it was passed (2026-08-26, same day — Kushagra: "I dont know enough to judge, you decide if its ready to build")

The gate's three terms and their resolutions: (1) the first consumer is the builder's Layers pane, named and real (adoption is the recorded follow-up; the docs nav is the second consumer and brought the NAV vocabulary with it — SHIPPED 2026-08-26 as `NavTree`, the paragraph below); (2) the load-bearing laws were written and falsified — 13 mounted laws, four sabotage passes each caught by exactly its law (the indent rule deleted, collapsed children rendered, the `data-selected` stamp dropped, the selected rung downgraded to quiet); the selection-paint agreement reads a mounted `<Row current>` rather than a chosen Card, because both resolve the family's medium rung and a row, not a card, is the member a tree row must match; (3) the part vocabulary was delegated ("names are last"): ONE export, `Tree`, with `TreeNode` as the data shape — no `TreeItem` part exists because the API is data-driven.

**`NavTree` shipped 2026-08-26 — the machine's NAVIGATION member, and the docs sidebar became one whole (Kushagra: "swap docs shell sidebar's internals with tree"; the whole-nav shape was his pick over a Components-only swap).** Same data, flatten, derived indent and disclosure glyph as `Tree`; what differs is everything the two-vocabularies bullet promised. No tree role anywhere — the over-claim law is the member's first law, falsified by stamping `role="tree"` back on. An expandable section is a real `<button aria-expanded>` whose WHOLE row is the disclosure (the instrument's Finder split protects a selection this member does not have, so a separate chevron target would be a second control with no job); a leaf is a real link through the `renderLink` escape (`(node) => <Link href/>`; the element's own props win — the Button-as-anchor lesson); the page you are on is `currentId`, announced `aria-current="page"` — LOCATION, with selection refused outright, which is the same distinction that keeps a tree's selected rung off `aria-current` one member up. The keyboard is the platform's (normal tab order; the roving walk and typeahead are the instrument's, law-asserted as no negative tabindex). The current row's identity is ShellNavItem's deliberately — accent stamped always under `undilutedTones`, label stood down to the neutral ink unless current, `--tone-current` on the current row — self-keyed in tree.css as the pair's SECOND home (shell.css is the first; the third member promotes). `TreeNode` gained `href` and `leading` for this member; `Tree` ignores both. In the docs the sections became collapsible level-0 nodes (all open by default; Components opens when you stand in it), `NavDisclosure` was deleted on its own instruction ("WHEN THE TREE LANDS, DELETE THIS FILE"), and `currentId={pathname}` is the entire current-page wiring because a leaf's id IS its href. Six new mounted laws; two sabotage passes (the role stamp, the current-ink arm) each caught by exactly its law.

**What building settled that the spec had left open:**

- **Data-driven, not JSX** (`items: TreeNode[]`): the flat-list architecture needs the hierarchy as a value, and a nested-JSX walk would be the child-scanning the Shell deleted. Recorded as a refusal ("JSX children") in the component reference.
- **Rows self-render** wearing `kui-control kui-row` — ShellNavItem's pattern, not a `<Row>` composition, because `Row` owns its emphasis stamp and a tree's selected rung must not be spelled through `aria-current` (location ≠ selection). Selected = `aria-selected` + `data-selected` + the family's `medium` rung, so selection and hover share one currency.
- **The indent is `--kui-ct-icon` itself** — one level is one icon box, zero new numbers, priced per size and pointer world by the ladder it derives from. The icon-box restatement law gained its second reasoned exemption (tree.css CONSUMES the ladder as a distance, spinner.css's own shape).
- **The chevron is passive paint with a click handler**, never a nested button (the row IS one); a leaf keeps the hidden glyph box so labels at one level share a left edge. Toggling by chevron selects nothing — Finder's split.
- **No motion yet**, Menu's own precedent: the chevron's turn and the reveal's entrance are the recorded follow-up, and `rotate` is used so the motion pass can spring the channel without restating the pose.

+158 gzipped bytes for the machine, its stylesheet and the flat renderer. Preview section, component-reference entry (six refusals), example file and builder exclusion (data-prop, not canvas-expressible) shipped in the same change, as the coverage laws require.

## 34. Toggle (2026-08-31)

**A button that holds its state.** Bold in a formatting bar, "show hidden files" in a toolbar, a filter that is on or off: one control, two states, `aria-pressed` from the primitive. It is the multi-select half of the question §26 split — picking ONE of several is a radio group and this library spells that `SegmentedControl`; a set of INDEPENDENT on/off buttons is this.

**It is a Button, and it wears the class.** `kui-control kui-button kui-toggle`, the SelectTrigger-wears-`kui-field` precedent: the size join, the press travel, the ring, the material, the disabled remap and the emphasis ladder all arrive by membership, and the mounted laws are AGREEMENTS with a mounted Button at every index rather than restated values. What a toggle adds is exactly one fact, stated as a rule about who may speak: **its emphasis IS its state.** Off is the quiet rung; on is the medium rung of its own tone — the soft wash a chosen Card and a selected tree row rest on (§23's `data-selected` vocabulary one family over), so "this is on" is spelled once across the library. `emphasis` is therefore refused: loudness is what the toggle is saying, and a call site that could set it could make an OFF toggle louder than an ON one. The stamp rides Base UI's resolved state through the render function, never a local mirror, because inside a `ToggleGroup` the pressed state lives in the group and a mirror would be a second home for it.

**Tone reaches the word, not the wash.** Since 2026-08-23 no family paints a faded wash, so a pressed `destructive` toggle is a grey wash with a red word — exactly the medium destructive Button, law-asserted as the agreement. §11's row had said "accent when active" since before the selected vocabulary existed; a pressed toggle is neutral by default, and a caller who means accent says `tone="accent"`.

**The one rule of its own is a RANKING (§10's fifth clause).** Quiet's hover is `--tone-soft` and medium's rest is `--tone-soft`: measured before toggle.css existed, a pressed Bold and a hovered Italic were byte-identical — the segmented control's 2026-08-24 finding and the tree's 2026-08-26 finding a third time. The answer is the row family's: an UNPRESSED toggle's transient light is the half-step, `color-mix` toward transparent at `--kui-ct-lit-mix`, so pressed(soft) > hovered(soft × mix) everywhere toggles exist, and a pressed toggle under the pointer still darkens because the medium rung keeps its full step. Keyed on `:not([data-pressed])`, not on the quiet rung, because here the rung IS the state and the selector should say what it means; (0,4,0) against the shared hover's (0,3,0), by rank rather than by file order. **The percentage moved to one home with this, its third consumer**: it was a literal in two row rules and is `--kui-ct-lit-mix` on the control skeleton now — a judged lever, taste, there and nowhere else. The law reads the ranking off a real pointer on an unpressed sibling; every agreement law above it stays green over the defect, which is why a token-name law could not have caught it.

**`ToggleGroup` is always multiple.** Base UI's group defaults to single-select, where pressing one releases the rest — a radio group announcing itself as something else, which is the exact shape §26 refused. The prop is not offered; the group is a `role="group"` div with roving arrow keys (focus moves, nothing presses — a toggle is not a radio) and one value array; it owns no layout and no appearance, and `render` stays open so `render={<Flex gap="1"/>}` makes the group the row it lays out.

**The press outranks the light, and it did not (2026-09-01, ultracode audit).** The half-step rule was written to beat the shared hover at (0,3,0), and `:not()` takes the specificity of its most specific ARGUMENT rather than summing its list — so it landed at (0,4,0) and beat the shared PRESS as well. Measured with a real pointer held down: an unpressed toggle painted its hover fill while the quiet Button beside it moved `0.055` → `0.078`, so the one press a toggle exists for was the only press in the control family with no colour. The repair is in the SHARED layer rather than here — a press is the last word on a control's fill, so the rule that says so is now (0,4,0) itself (the same `:not()` split, spent deliberately) — and this sheet's guard moved inside `:where()`, which leaves it winning the hover fight by file order and losing the press fight by rank. Two rows of the row family had the identical fault, one of them outside the hover guard and therefore reaching touch; all three are held by one mounted law that HOLDS a press and reads the painted fill (`system/motion.browser.test.tsx`).

**Refused, on record.** `emphasis` (above); `loading` (a toggle does not wait — a control that starts a job is a Button); `render` (the emphasis is stamped from the primitive's state onto the element the primitive renders, and a toggle is a button); a single-select group (above); a pressed toggle HOLDING the press travel (proposed on Button's latched-trigger rule, refuted: that rule exists to still a panel hanging from the trigger, and a toggle hangs nothing — a pressed Bold sitting 1px lower than Italic beside it would read as a misprint).

+257 gzipped bytes, of which the rule is a fraction: the one-home move lengthened the two row rules it replaced a literal in. 22 mounted laws, three sabotage passes (rule deleted → the two ranking laws fail and nothing else; `multiple` unpinned → the group law fails; emphasis stamped statically → eleven fail). Preview section, component-reference entry, example, builder catalog entries and the API table shipped in the same change.

## 35. Avatar (2026-08-31)

**A person, a team or a thing as a small round picture, with something to stand in for it.** No §11 row existed; it was the first of the "simple components we are missing" (Kushagra, 2026-08-31, with Toggle, Table and Accordion). Base UI carries the loading machine (image shown once loaded, fallback before and after a failure), and this system carries the box.

**The box is the atom family's, and that decision is the whole component.** An avatar is inert, sits beside running text, and is a property of the type beside it — the mark family's own argument, and the reason it wears `kui-type kui-atom` rather than a ladder of its own: `block-size: 1lh; inline-size: 1lh`, Kbd's one-line box, so an unset avatar in a list row is exactly that row's line, one in a page header is the header's line, and it rises on a phone with the handheld band. `size` is a type step 1–9, OPTIONAL with no default (Chip's rule): unset means "take the line beside me". Rejected: the control height ladder (a picture beside a Button is level with it, but a picture beside a NAME is what an avatar mostly is, and the control ladder stops at four steps where profile headers need the top of the ramp) and the mark ladder (five steps, priced for a tick box). The fill and the inherited-size arm arrive from `.kui-atom`; the file states the circle, the clip (`clip`, never `hidden`), the picture (`object-fit: cover`, absolutely filling the disc) and the initials.

**The initials are a share of the DISC, not a discount on the type step — measured.** The first spelling rode `--kui-ty-scale` like the cap and the chip, and step 9 filled the disc (0.7 × 56px = 39px in a 62px box) while step 3 kept a face, because the ramp's line ratio falls from 1.33 to 1.107 up the ladder and a constant discount cannot hold against that. In `font-size`, `1lh` is the PARENT's line height — the avatar's own box — so `font-size: calc(1lh * var(--avatar-scale))` on the fallback states the letters against the disc they sit in at every step and in every inherited line, with no step named in the component sheet (the no-axis law). `avatarScale` = 0.45 (Radix parks two letters at half); taste, one home. A string fallback is uppercased at medium weight; no fallback draws a generic person glyph on the 16-grid at `glyphStroke`, sized in the atom's own `em`.

**No `tone` (removed the day it shipped, Kushagra: "I question the need for tone on Avatar itself").** Since no family paints a tinted wash (2026-08-23) a tone could only move the initials' ink — too faint to be the per-person colour a coloured avatar is for, and the strong version is refused system-wide. The face is stamped `neutral` because the atom fill reads the tone indirection; colour-as-identity is the picture's job. It returns only if the wash rule changes.

**A circle at every radius level.** A person is a disc on every platform; the radio's exception one family over (§6). A rounded square for a workspace or an app is a real case and waits for the screen that forces it — recorded, not built, because a `shape` prop today would be a prop with one value.

**`AvatarGroup` is a line of text with no words in it.** It wears `kui-type`, so a `size` on the group sets the line every unset avatar takes (an avatar that states its own still wins — the same two bounds as `ControlSizeContext`, by CSS inheritance rather than React). Each later face sits `avatarOverlap` (0.25) of a face over the one before it — a share of `1lh`, so it grows with the size — and every face in a group wears a `2 × --border-width` ring in `--color-surface` drawn OUTSIDE the box by a `::after` (Kushagra, 2026-08-31 — a border inside the box shrank every grouped face by 4px, so a group read smaller than the lone avatar beside it; a pseudo-element costs no layout and shrinks nothing, the picture rounds itself so the root need not clip). Not `outline` (every outline in the package is the focus ring, by law) and not `box-shadow` (fenced to the depth roles). Overflow ("+3") is the caller's `<Avatar fallback="+3" />`, already the right shape; `max` is refused as a product decision.

**Three repairs from the ultracode audit (2026-09-01).** The GROUP sat on the baseline and spread the line it was in — `<Text size="9">Team <AvatarGroup/> x</Text>` rendered a 72.95px paragraph against a 62px line, +1.61 to +10.95px up the ramp — because `.kui-avatar` states `vertical-align: top` and the group stated nothing; no law had ever put a group in a line, which is the half of the family the member's own law could not cover. The FALLBACK carried no name: with `alt="Kushagra"` and a picture that never loaded a reader heard "K D", and with the default `alt=""` the initials were announced anyway — it takes `role="img"` with the supplied name, or `aria-hidden` when there is none, so whichever of the two is showing answers for the same thing. And two laws were reading one cell of a ladder (the icon-only fill at default/fine/size 2, where every input is 30; the generic glyph inside a window wide enough to admit a `rem` typo) — both now walk their cells and read the derivation.

**Refused, on record.** `shape` (above); `emphasis` (no avatar is louder than the one beside it — rank in a list of people is order and size); a status dot (a bare coloured dot is WCAG 1.4.1's own case; a Chip with a word sits beside it); a press (an avatar is inert — a person you can open is a Button with an avatar inside it); `max` on the group. **A press was re-argued the same day (Kushagra, from the shell: "Avatar should be able to play as a button, no?") and stays refused, with the flaw it exposed fixed instead**: the press machine — ring, travel, disabled remap, the required name — exists once, in Button, and Card-as-button borrows it only because a surface already reuses the control states; an atom does not. What was wrong was the LOOK: an avatar in an icon-only Button sat one line tall inside a taller square. Now it FILLS the button (`--kui-ct-h` less the skeleton's hairline, read by inheritance — the segmented control's move — with its line re-stated to that height so the initials keep their share), so `<Button iconOnly backdrop><Avatar/></Button>` is one disc the size of a button, glass included. **And `backdrop` reached Avatar directly** (Chip's wiring: `useMaterial`, the lens, `GlassScope` around the slot), for the inert glass face; a picture is opaque and covers it. **The `badge` slot joined the same day (§38)**: top-end of the disc — riding the rim since 2026-09-01 (`avatarBadgeOut` 0.1 of the slot line per axis seats the badge's centre ON the rim; flush at the corner it sat ~0.065 of the face inside it and read stuck on the face) — the badge wearing the group's surface-coloured ring as its border so the disc reads notched; the slot hands the badge a SMALLER line than the face's (`avatarBadge` 0.55 of it) — judged in the playground, a badge priced on the face's own line was 60% of the face and covered the initials — so a pinned badge lands near a third of the face, iOS's own ratio, and a bigger face still carries a bigger badge with nothing said twice. The "status dot" refusal above is superseded by it — what stays refused is a status VOCABULARY. `alt` defaults to empty, marking the picture decorative, which is right beside the name it usually sits next to; the name goes in `alt` when the avatar is the only thing naming them.

**Recorded, unresolved.** The group's ring reads `--color-surface`, which is the right ground on a card and on the light page (the page IS the seal's white since 2026-08-25) and one step off on the dark page (`--neutral-1` against `--neutral-2`); a ring that reads the nearest ground is the surface padding hook's shape and waits for a second consumer of that fact.

+161 gzipped bytes. 16 mounted laws, four sabotage passes each caught by the laws written for it (the share rule, the overlap, the ring, the width). Preview section, component-reference entry, example, builder catalog entries and the API table shipped in the same change.

## 36. Table (2026-08-31)

**A static data table: rows and columns as the real `<table>`, inside a box that scrolls sideways.** Seven exports in shadcn/ui's vocabulary (MIT, credited): `Table`, `TableHeader`, `TableBody`, `TableRow`, `TableHead`, `TableCell`, `TableCaption`. No primitive underneath — Base UI has none, and a static table needs none; the semantics are the platform's.

**The forcing case was the docs' own table.** `prose.css` had drawn one by hand since 2026-08-21 (the docs-site entry recorded it as one of three unshipped prose primitives), and the component reference's generated props table was a second consumer of the same rules — two homes for one judged cell. The component is that table promoted: size 2 shipped AS the docs' 4 / 8 cell (one rung looser since 2026-09-01, below), and both consumers swapped to the package in the same change (the MDX element map, the `[slug]` API table), deleting `.kd-table` whole.

**Two elements, TextField's split.** The wrapper is the scroller (`overflow-x: auto` — honestly a scroll container, the case the flight rules' `clip` argument is not about: a table is the one block whose content can genuinely exceed its measure, and a page that scrolls sideways because of one table is worse than a table that does), so `className`/`style` dress the wrapper and `ref` reaches the `<table>`.

**`size` sets the inset and the step at one index.** The cell inset is PUBLISHED by a table size join in surfaces.css (a component sheet may not name `data-size`; the joins are where per-index spellings live) as `--kui-tb-py`/`--kui-tb-px`, picked from LAYOUT-SPACE — a cell's inset is a layout distance, so it tightens under `density="compact"` with the Stack around it (§3, §12). Picks: block 2/3/4/5, inline 3/4/5/6 (4/8, 8/12, 12/16, 16/24 default) — stepped up one rung 2026-09-01 (Kushagra: the shipped inset read "a bit less"; the promoted 4 / 8 cell was judged inside prose, and a table standing alone breathes wider). The type step is the identity through the type join (`Table` wears `kui-type`), default 2 — tables are dense by nature. Refuted before picking: `em` insets (the atoms' currency is for glyph-owned room, and a table's rows must move with density, which `em` cannot hear) and a designed raw ladder (the palette already holds the values, and inventing a ladder where one fits is the fraction wall's own warning from the other side).

**What it draws, and only that.** A hairline under every row in `--color-border` (law-asserted equal to a mounted Separator's paint and thickness), none under the last body row (the table's edge is the end), the header in the muted ink at medium weight (a column's name is secondary to what it names — macOS's and shadcn's headers), the body in the text role, baseline alignment so a Code chip and a word in one row share a line, and a `caption` below in the muted ink (the table's accessible name). `align` on head and cell is a closed word — `start | center | end`, never `left`/`right` — because a numeric column is basic and a physical direction is wrong in RTL.

**Rows are inert, and the interactive row is a DIFFERENT component.** §11's "Table row" (transparent → hover 3 → press 4; selected = accent) is a row-family member with a keyboard and a name, and it will not arrive as a prop that turns this table into it: a static table and a selectable list of records are two things wearing one element, and the boundary is the same one that separates Card from a chosen Card. Refused with it: a sticky header (a pinned header decides the height of the room around it — no component owns its position; the pattern belongs to a ScrollArea when it is designed), sorting and column controls (state and a keyboard; the header control that carries them is a Button), tone and emphasis (nothing of its own to colour or rank; a cell's words carry a Chip or a toned Text).

+206 gzipped bytes (the join and the sheet; the docs' deleted copy was never in the budget). 11 mounted laws, four sabotage passes each caught by exactly its law (the last-row rule, the scroller, the header ink, the join). Preview section, component-reference entry, example, builder catalog entries (seven, the parts reachable only inside a Table) and the API table shipped in the same change.


**Three repairs and three laws from the ultracode audit (2026-09-01).** The wrapper is a scroll region and a scroll region is keyboard focusable — that is WCAG 2.1.1 satisfied, not a defect — but its whole attribute set was `["data-size","class"]`, so Tab landed on a node with no role and no name while `aria-label` on `<Table>` rode the rest spread onto the `<table>`, where it could not name the thing the reader had just reached. `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` are declared props now and land on the WRAPPER with `role="region"`, only when a name is given (an unnamed region is a landmark that says nothing) and with no `tabIndex` (the browser makes a scroller focusable exactly when it overflows). The last-row rule read `tbody tr:last-child` — the BODY's last row, not the table's — so a `<tfoot>`, which is ordinary markup a caller can pass, inverted the sentence: no line between body and footer, one under the table's own bottom edge. And declaring one overflow axis promotes the other, so the box clipped the BLOCK axis, where nothing can be scrolled back: a focused `<Link>` in a header cell lost 2.00px of its 4px ring at compact/size 1. It pads the ring's own reach now, `--kui-menu-pad`'s sentence one component over. The laws that let all three through were each reading the half that was right: the inline inset never had to track density, the hairline was read on the first cell of each row, and `align="center"` had no reader at all.
## 37. Accordion (2026-08-31)

**Sections that open and close, one under the other.** Four exports in shadcn/ui's vocabulary (MIT, credited): `Accordion`, `AccordionItem`, `AccordionTrigger`, `AccordionPanel`. Base UI carries the machine — the value array, one-open or `multiple`, `hiddenUntilFound` for find-in-page, `keepMounted` — and this system carries the rows. Base UI's `Header` is folded into `AccordionTrigger` (a heading holding a button is the APG's required anatomy, not a choice), with `headingLevel` because an accordion's headings belong to the page's outline.

**The trigger is a HEADING that wears the control skeleton — it shipped as a row and was corrected the next day (2026-09-01, Kushagra: "is it a row tho?").** `kui-control kui-accordion-trigger`, `data-emphasis="quiet"`, the disclosure chevron in the trailing slot, standing on the height ladder (law-asserted level with a Button at every index), with the ring, the size join, the disabled remap and Button's medium weight arriving by membership. It shipped as `kui-control kui-row` on the Tree's analogy, and the analogy was backwards: a row is a line in a list you read and PICK — it rests quiet, lights under the pointer, and can be selected, and its siblings are alternatives — while an accordion's heading is pressed to disclose what is under it, nothing is picked, its siblings are independent and "open" is not "selected". A tree row is a list item that happens to expand; this is a disclosure that happens to sit in a column. Everything the row supplied was the SKELETON's (ladder, ring, join, remap); the one thing the row added was its fill light, which is exactly what a heading should not do. Its nearest kin is the tab trigger: a text label that acts, never marked by a fill. So the fill triple is pinned transparent at every source and the hover is the LABEL's — Link's mechanism verbatim, an underline resting in no colour that takes the ink under the pointer, eased on the paint clock — and the weight is the skeleton's medium (the row family's stand-down to regular no longer reaches it), because a heading over words read at the same step in the full ink needs the rank. The disclosure glyph is the tree's chevron in the trailing slot — the slot wrapper already sizes it to the icon box — pointing into the reading direction and turning 90° when the panel opens, on the mark clock, with the tree's own RTL sign flip.

**The panel travels by height on the spring (§8).** Base UI publishes `--accordion-panel-height`; the panel is that tall open, zero at `data-starting-style`/`data-ending-style`, and `block-size` rides `--motion-travel` on `--motion-spring` — geometry moves on a spring, everywhere. `overflow: clip`, never `hidden` (a hidden box is a scroll container, the select flight's own hazard). The panel's words start UNDER THE LABEL: an accordion size join in surfaces.css publishes `--kui-ac-px` as the control family's pill-corrected pick — the exact value the row's `--kui-ct-px-pill` resolves at the same index, so the two cannot disagree, law-read at `radius="full"` where the row pads wider — and `--kui-ac-pb` one layout-space step under the card's rung. **Each item breathes `--kui-ac-py` between its hairline and its row (2026-09-01, Kushagra — the table's "a bit less" one component over)**: a layout-space pick per index from the same join, padding the ITEM and never the row's box, so the row stays level with the Button beside it and a compact app tightens the air with everything else; law-asserted symmetric and non-zero at every index. The index travels root → trigger by a private context (Menu's shape), deliberately not `ControlSizeContext`, which is Field's one supply.

**It paints no pane.** Hairlines between items in `--color-border` (the table's sentence; law-equal to a Separator), none above the first — and **inset to the labels since 2026-09-01** (Kushagra: "the separators extend beyond the text, they go till hover boundary, and we recently fixed this with menu"): the menu's own sentence, the row lights its box and the reader groups the words, so the line is a pseudo-element running from `--kui-ac-px` to `--kui-ac-px`, the exact inset the panel's words start at. No fill, no cast. **The list fills its container** (`inline-size: 100%`, same day): a flex column inside a flex row shrink-wrapped its widest child, so the whole list breathed with whichever panel was open — Table's answer, the extent is the container's. **And the panel's words take the heading's step** (same day: "the expanded text doesnt respond to size"): the body wears the type join at the identity step, so plain text scales with the section like a table cell's, while a `Text` inside still states its own — the content is the caller's, the line it is read in is the section's. **The heading's weight is Button's** (same day: "like button, the trigger should be a thicker weight"), law-read as the agreement and as a rank over the panel's words. An accordion is a list of headings in whatever surface it sits in, and a Card is how it gets an edge.

**Seven repairs from the ultracode audit (2026-09-01), and six of them are one sentence: leaving the row family took more than the light.** `.kui-row` declared a block padding and `.kui-control` does not, so a heading that WRAPS computed `0px/0px` and sat flush against the border box its focus ring follows (measured at 220px: 62px of glyphs in a 70px row) — the trigger states the row's own expression now, because the question is the row's. The row's auto margin on the trailing slot went too, and `justify-content: space-between` is only "label one end, chevron the other" while a heading holds exactly two items: a Badge after the word floated 172.75px into a 320px heading. The chevron is pushed by an auto margin again and the rest packs. The underline's `transition` was copied verbatim from `link.css`, where the element is not a control — a shorthand, so it REPLACED the skeleton's five channels with one, and the heading's ink stopped easing on any state change; the list is restated in full and a law derives it from a mounted Button's. The glyph names a tone-LESS role, which the shared disabled remap cannot reach, so a dead heading kept a full-strength chevron beside its receded label. The pinned fill triple and the hover guard between them left a touch device with NO feedback on press, so `:active` shows the underline unguarded — the shared layer's own "the only feedback touch has", in the label's currency. And the hairline used one inset at both ends while the skeleton resets the TRAILING padding to the plain pick for any control holding a trailing slot, so the line stopped 3px inside the chevron; the join publishes both picks and each end reads its own side. Separately, `--kui-ac-py` padded the first and last items, which is air outside anything it separates — the outer ends carry none.

**Refused, on record.** A horizontal orientation (a different keyboard for a thing nothing asked for); tone and emphasis (a heading list has no meaning to colour; no heading is louder than the next); a custom chevron or icon slot (one disclosure glyph, the tree's — two ways to say "open" is the drift); a boundary of its own; `render` on the trigger (the element is the machine's, and the emphasis stamp lives on it). **Arrow keys between headers are not claimed**: the APG lists them as optional and Base UI 1.7 declares `loopFocus` without implementing any key handling (measured — focus stayed put); Tab is the required keyboard and works. The law that claimed the arrows was deleted rather than left green over nothing.

**One law was thrown away by its own sabotage pass.** "The chevron turns" ended in `await until(() => rotate === "90deg")` with no assertion after it; `until` resolves false on timeout, so deleting the turn left fourteen laws green. The degenerate-law shape, in a file written the same hour the rule was re-read. +297 gzipped bytes. 14 mounted laws, four sabotage passes each caught by exactly its law after that repair. Preview section, component-reference entry, example, builder catalog entries and the API table shipped in the same change.

## 38. Badge — the platform's badge — and Chip (2026-08-31)

**The name went to the thing the platform calls by it.** Apple's badge is the red count on an app icon and `.badge()` on a tab or a list row: a number or a dot, on a corner, that WAITS until you look. What this library had called `Badge` since 2026-08-23 — "Paid", "Failed", "Beta" beside a row — is a word with a tint, which Apple calls a label. So the chip renamed to **`Chip`** (one scripted pass over 30 files; `chipScale`, `--chip-scale`, `kui-chip`, slug `chip`) and **`Badge`** is now the marker. Kushagra: *"we're making a design system, and it is a set of refusals… I know iOS provides a way to add status badges or indicators to app icons, or tabs… I think we should as a system provide it."*

**The argument that shaped it, in three turns.** (1) The first proposal was `emphasis` on the chip plus a `status` slot on Avatar with an `online | away | busy` set — refuted on two counts: a loud rung on a free-standing chip turns every status column into traffic lights (Button earns `loud` by anatomy; a chip has no rule holding it to one), and a status set is a product vocabulary the system has no business owning. (2) Collapsed to one slot taking a Chip — refuted by the case that actually exists: a count and a dot are not a word, and a chip cannot be bare. (3) So a NEW component, and §29 had already named its category: *"every OS solved the background job with a location rather than an announcement… a badge waits."* This is that. Not `Notification` (an event, §29's territory) and not `Indicator` (a made-up word where the platform has one); the platform's word, once the collision was ours to fix.

**What it is.** Bare = a dot, with content = a pill; ONE identity: the tone's solid with the contrast label — the loud rung's pairing, stated directly because a badge has no ladder to climb (a marker that whispers is not a marker; law-equal to a loud Button of the same tone). Sized as a share of the LINE it sits in (`badgeBox` 0.6 of `1lh` for the height, `badgeText` 0.42 for the digits; the atom argument, and the avatar-scale mechanism one component over), with `size` optional and no default, so a badge on a size-2 tab label and one pinned to a size-9 avatar are one shape at two sizes. A single digit is a disc (the min-inline-size), more digits grow a pill in their own `em`. The bare dot needs no rule of its own — one was written and deleted when its sabotage pass proved it redundant.

**The system's tones, never a vocabulary of the badge's own.** `tone` is the same closed set every tone-carrying component reads (`accent` default = "something is here"; `destructive`, `warning`, `success`, `info`, `neutral`, the data families), so an app maps ITS words onto it — an alert is `destructive`, a pending job is `warning` — and the system never learns "online" or "busy". The first cut limited it to two (Kushagra: "we have an orthogonal mapping to existing vocabulary; the problem with online/offline was that it wasn't a fixed set"). **A bare dot must be NAMED** (the type: `children` or `aria-label`), which is the WCAG 1.4.1 point answered rather than refused — what a sighted reader infers from the marker, the name states. No `emphasis`.

**It owns no position; the host does.** `Avatar` takes `badge` and places it TOP-end riding the rim (the platform's corner for a count on an icon; `avatarBadgeOut` 0.1 of the slot line per axis steps it past the box so its centre lands on the rim — flush it sat inside and read stuck on the face, 2026-09-01), on a line `avatarBadge` (0.55) of the face's — the pinned badge near a third of the face — with the group's surface-coloured ring drawn OUTSIDE the badge by a pseudo-element (a border with `content-box` let the padding add on top of the disc's minimum and a pinned digit went pill, 22.7 x 16.5 measured) so the disc reads notched and the pinned badge's box is byte-identical to a free one. **In flowing text the badge's centre sits on the CAP centre, not `vertical-align: middle`'s seat (2026-09-01)**: middle parks the centre at half the x-height — measured 4.26px above the baseline against a cap centre of 5.44 at step 3, visibly low beside the capitals it counts for — and the correction is a relative lift (`badgeLift` 0.05 of the line) over a kept `middle`, because a baseline-relative `vertical-align` length is content-dependent (a digit hands the flex box a real baseline, a bare dot does not; measured 4.4px apart) and a relative offset moves paint without letting the line grow. The pinned badge stands the lift down — it is seated by the slot, not by a line. Generalising the anchor (icon Buttons, tabs) waits for the second consumer to force the geometry — the cut-out works because a face is a circle.

Chip's own refusals are re-keyed with it ("an empty chip": a chip is a word; the bare mark is a Badge). +181 gzipped bytes. 9 mounted Badge laws + 2 on Avatar's slot, three sabotage passes each caught by exactly its law. Preview section, component-reference entries (Badge new, Chip renamed, Avatar amended), example, builder catalog entry and the API table shipped in the same change.

## 39. Breadcrumb (2026-09-01)

**No primitive, and nothing for one to do.** Base UI ships none. A breadcrumb has no state, no keyboard machine and nothing to open; what it has is announcements — a landmark, an ordered list, and `aria-current` on the end of it — which is semantic HTML. So this is Table's shape (§36) rather than Menu's, and the parts wear shadcn/ui's names (MIT, credited).

**Five exports of shadcn's seven. Two do not survive §10's anatomy criterion, and one of them is the whole design.** `BreadcrumbList` collapses into `Breadcrumb`: a breadcrumb is always a landmark holding a list, so nothing would ever choose between the two — shadcn splits them because its parts are styling hooks, and the layout here is the system's. Two elements, one part, `className`/`style` on the outer one (TextField's and Table's split). **`BreadcrumbSeparator` is REFUSED and the item draws its own chevron** (Kushagra's call, put to him with both call sites side by side). shadcn has the caller place one between every pair and leave it off the last, which is three things this system does not hand to a call site: it is layout wearing a part's name (`Row`'s and `ComposerRow`'s finding, where five of v1's eleven parts were exactly this), it makes an off-by-one rule the caller's to keep by hand, and it lets each call site pick the glyph — where appearance is resolved output. Drawn in the item it cannot be forgotten, cannot be doubled, and cannot be a slash on one page and a chevron on the next; `:last-child` in the stylesheet is the whole cost of "and not after the last one". It is a real `<svg>` at `glyphStroke` rather than a `content: "\203A"`, because a text chevron is drawn by whatever face the line resolved and would paint at that face's weight beside a Select's chevron drawn at the system's — the 2026-08-23 two-grids defect arriving by a different road.

**A crumb is not a `Link`, and the tone is why.** `Link` rests on `accent` and always STAMPS it, because a link's whole job is to be found inside a paragraph — and `type.css` re-scopes all three foreground roles onto the stamped family's ink trio, so a crumb built out of `Link` could read a family's greys and never the system's. A crumb carries no meaning: it is a location, so it reads the TONE-LESS roles, which is the pair Tabs reads for the same reason. `tone` and `emphasis` are refused with the colour — the three ranks (page / crumb / punctuation) are the design, not rungs to pick from. What it does inherit from Link is the two refusals that are about being text: not a `.kui-control`, and no target, on SC 2.5.8's inline exception. The ring is drawn here for Link's reason (no skeleton reaches it). It is NOT the second — a directory walk finds ten focus rules across the package, and the ordinal was wrong when it was written (ultracode audit 2026-09-01). What holds them to one designed value is the walked law in `recipes.test.ts`, which is the guarantee; a count in three homes with no reader is the shape that goes stale, and it did.

**The underline is a carve-out, not a drift.** Link's is unconditional on WCAG 1.4.1, whose case — technique F73 — is a link INSIDE A BLOCK OF TEXT, where hue is the only thing separating it from the words around it. Nothing in a breadcrumb is inside prose: every item before the last is a link, the chevrons say where one ends and the next begins, and the current page is a different ink in a different position. So the line rests TRANSPARENT rather than absent — its metrics never move, and §8's paint clock has a colour to carry, where a `text-decoration-line` appearing would arrive in one frame.

**The glyph is priced off the LINE.** `--kui-ct-icon` is the control family's designed icon box and a breadcrumb has no control box to hang one on — the fraction wall's ninth instance, reached by inheritance rather than by arithmetic this time. A glyph standing between two runs of words is a property of the line those words set, which is the mark family's rule (`--mark-N` IS `--line-height-N`) and the atoms' `1lh` box arriving at a third family. `breadcrumbGlyph` is 0.75, and it prices the SEPARATOR alone. It covered both glyphs for one hour; the ellipsis became a control the same day and its dots ride `--kui-ct-icon` like every other glyph inside one, which is the right ladder for a thing that HAS a control box. The clause claiming a proportional relationship between the two is retracted rather than left standing above its own retraction — measured, the chevron runs 12/18/24/46.5px across steps 1/3/6/9 while the dots are pinned at 16 (ultracode audit 2026-09-01). The gap is `--layout-space-2`, read by the list AND by the item, because two tokens would be two numbers for one rhythm and the chevron would sit closer to one neighbour than the other.

**`size` is stated once and inherited.** A step on the shared ramp, on the nav, defaulting to 2 — §15's label-and-meta rung, because a breadcrumb tells you where you are and where you are is not what you came to read. No crumb states one — with the ellipsis as the stated exception, because it IS a Button and a Button takes the control ladder, not this one; its box is priced by the control layer at that family's own default. Tabs' "a bar of mixed sizes is not a thing anyone means" governs the words, which is what the step is about.

**`BreadcrumbPage` and `BreadcrumbEllipsis` are parts because the ANNOUNCEMENT forces them** — the criterion, twice. `aria-current="page"` is the one thing on the whole component a reader cannot see; shadcn's `role="link" aria-disabled="true"` beside it is refused, because both halves are false (there is nothing to follow, and nothing was disabled) and `aria-current` on plain text is what the APG's own example carries. And a truncated path drawn as three silent dots announces the crumbs that remain as if they were the path, which is a lie about where the listener is — so the ellipsis carries a name and its dots are `aria-hidden`.

**`maxItems` is refused, and the ELLIPSIS IS THE OPENER (reversed 2026-09-01, same day, twice).** It shipped as shadcn's static marker with the openable form left to composition — and Kushagra walked into the dead one twice within the hour, in the preview and then on the reference page a reader lands on: *"I cannot select or click on the ellipsis, it shows nothing."* He was right and the original screenshot had already said so — shadcn ships an inert `BreadcrumbEllipsis` and then wraps it in a dropdown in the one example anybody copies, which leaves the dead one reachable and makes it the one a reader presses first. Three dots are an affordance; an ellipsis that opens nothing is a control promising something it does not have, which is the builder's `armed` dead-control finding arriving in a component. So the menu IS the component: `items` is REQUIRED, the trigger is a priced icon-only Button, and a dead ellipsis is not expressible — the refusal is in the type, where no mount can assert it. `maxItems` stays refused for the reason it always was (§3 — which levels to drop depends on the room and on which of them mean anything): you render the crumbs you are keeping and hand this the rest. **The rows are PLACES, so they are links, and that opened `render` on `MenuItem`** — a menu of places is a real case this package could not express, and the escape keeps the row ONE target where an anchor nested inside the row would be a second. Two things the reversal DISSOLVED rather than kept: `--breadcrumb-glyph` now prices the separator alone, because the ellipsis has a control box and its glyph rides `--kui-ct-icon` like every other glyph inside one; and the `label={null}` stand-down added an hour earlier — for the double-named node when the marker sat inside a trigger — is gone, since there is now exactly one named node by construction. Net **−8 gzipped bytes** against the first spelling, because deleting the ellipsis's own rules paid for the rest. **The laws are the finding's real lesson**: every ellipsis law read the marker's ATTRIBUTES and all of them were green while pressing it did nothing, so the law now DRIVES a real press and reads the rows that open.

**140 gzipped bytes**, measured in isolation (import the sheet, build, measure; remove it, build, measure again). The figures this section carried before — `+213`, then `net −8` — were read off `budget.json`'s baseline while concurrent work on other components was moving the same file, so neither was ever breadcrumb's own cost. Isolating it is one command and nobody ran it until the ultracode audit's completeness critic asked (2026-09-01). 32 mounted laws, thirteen sabotage passes, each caught by exactly its own law. Three of the laws were instrument bugs before they were laws and all three were caught by their own first run: `tokenOn` answers `0px` for a unitless token (`numberOn` is the one that reads a number, and its docstring says so), a `@ts-expect-error` on an identifier that does not exist is a `ReferenceError` at run time rather than a refusal (the module's own namespace is what states it), and the chevron's BBOX is centred whichever way the glyph faces — `M6 4l4 4-4 4` spans x 6..10 of a 16 viewBox — so the mirror law compared 0 with 0 until it took the tree's `apexOffset` instead. Every fixture in the file carries FOUR items, because "all but the last" and "only the first" give the same answer on two.


## 40. CodeBlock (2026-09-01) — the docs' well, promoted

**It shipped as a stub in `apps/docs/blocks/` on 2026-08-26 and it was always going to cross.** The three-way split recorded that day — a block-level code ELEMENT for the package, the tone-derived syntax THEME as the package's colours, the CHROME as the first source block — is now two-thirds done and this is the two-thirds. A swap law watched the package's exports and failed the day `CodeBlock` appeared there; it has fired, and it is inverted rather than deleted: it now asserts the block reaches the well through the dependency and cannot re-grow a local one.

**It is a SURFACE, not a Card** (settled 2026-08-21 on the builder's export dialog): the code is not an object sitting on the page, it is a well recessed into it, so the pane is the same ground the rest of the page's grounds are. Law-read as an agreement against a mounted `Surface` AND against a mounted `Card`, because the Surface half alone passes for a Card on any day the two happen to agree.

**Display and position only.** There is no state and no behaviour here: copying, expanding, naming and highlighting stay the caller's. §11's inline atom turned block code away in its own doc, on the argument that a block owns overflow, a scroll container, a bound and a place for chrome to float, and none of that grows out of an inline chip without making two components wear one name. That argument is what this component is.

**SIZE PRICES EVERYTHING** — pane padding, corner, the mono step and the arithmetic the bound is built from — on §24/§25/§30's ownership rule: a component that owns its pane AND its text prices both. Read across all four indexes in one law, never two, because two adjacent steps agree under more than one wrong spelling (the composer shipped that fault twice).

**THE BOUND SCROLLS AND NEVER CLIPS.** `maxLines` counts LINES of the well's own code, and bounded means scrollable — a collapse that clips leaves the hidden lines in the tab order and out of reach of a wheel, which is v1's defect made inexpressible. That is also what lets an expand control be a convenience rather than a gate, and why the element ships no expand control of its own.

**The slots take CONTENT, and that is the promotion's real work.** In the docs the caller passed a positioned node and had to reach the pane's wall through `--kd-host-p`, a private hook the element published for a call site to consume correctly — the `--kui-h` trap with extra steps, and a contract nothing checked. The element places the row now: it owns the box (position, reach, the mark that keeps it out of flow), and the caller owns the arrangement inside it. `topbar` and `footer` are nodes and `band` is a flag, deliberately: the well cannot read its chrome's shape off the node, and counting the row's children would be this element guessing at another file's arrangement. What it can be told is the one geometric fact it needs, by the code that decided it.

**Two "PACKAGE GAP wearing a local fix — promote at integration" notes dissolved rather than moved.** The rows are marked `data-float`, so the surface layer's own edge-bleed arms (`:nth-child(1 of :where(:not([data-float])))`, promoted 2026-08-30) already ignore them and the docs' hand-written `margin-block` rule is deleted — which is what the mark was added for. The scroller's corner is stated for this component alone and the general rule stays open: a `border-radius` on a viewport clips its own content at the corners, harmless for a well of inset text and not harmless for a panel of rows that reach the walls.

**A band is reserved for a row that reaches TWO WALLS**, never for one control in a corner (2026-08-31, Kushagra: *"the one with no filename… the top left just looks weird"*). And the two rows do not rest at the same distance — the top at a fixed layout step, the bottom at the pane's own inset, 12 and 16 at the default index. Both numbers were judged where they are, one at a time, and neither against the other, so unifying them is a taste call and not a promotion's to make; it is stated once in the stylesheet instead of living in two call sites where nobody could see that they disagree. The bottom row asks for the pane's inset in the one spelling true in both arrangements — `var(--kui-cb-host-p, var(--kui-sf-p, 0px))` — because an index-keyed spelling is wrong twice over: a component sheet may not branch on `data-size`, and a hosted well's size need not be its host's.

**THE SYNTAX THEME IS THE PACKAGE'S, AND IT IS NOT IN THE COMPONENT'S STYLESHEET.** `codeTheme` in the colour config states the assignments; the generator emits `--code-*` into every scope that moves the inks. The package ships no highlighter — point one's CSS-variables mode at the `--code-` prefix and every colour it emits resolves against §15's solved ink ladder, which is the whole argument for not shipping a stock highlight theme: that is a palette somebody else picked at contrasts nobody measured, the one place a design system usually gives up and ships a photograph of an editor.

Its home was decided by a shipped law rather than by preference. The first spelling declared the tokens on the element, and `recipes.test.ts` refused it: a component stylesheet may not name a family, because §7's tone indirection exists so that a component never reaches past it. A theme is a cross-family assignment, so it belongs where the families are declared. **The second spelling then emitted it ONCE at `:root`**, on the argument that it names ROLES and a role is what carries the mode — and that is wrong in this system for the oldest reason in it: a custom property is substituted where it is DECLARED (§6), so a root-level `--code-token-keyword: var(--accent-ink)` bakes the root's accent and a light `<Theme>` inside a dark document paints its code in the dark family's inks. Measured, and caught by the law that walks all four of the light palette's cases. Fourth time that rule has had to be re-learned.

**Refused**: highlighting (a grammar per language, and the tokenizer belongs to whatever builds the pages), wrapping and a switch for it (a wrap puts a break where the language has none), a copy button, line numbers and a collapse (behaviour and markup, not the well — and a collapse takes lines out of reach), `tone` and `emphasis` (code has no meaning of its own to colour, and no block of it is louder than the next).

+429 gzipped bytes, most of it the theme in thirteen scopes; 13 mounted laws, six sabotage passes. One attempted sabotage was INERT and is recorded rather than dropped: an appended `overflow-y: hidden` on the viewport lost the cascade, so it proved nothing — the bound's law is falsified by removing the bound instead, which is the fault it is actually about.

## 41. The Button done state (2026-09-02) — what the Toast refusal owed

**§29 refused Toast and recorded three obligations. This is the second of them**, and it was the cheapest: *"a copy button showing a done state, where the eyes already are — which is not a toast and is better on every axis."* Until now the refusal was asking call sites to build the alternative themselves, and one of them had: `apps/docs/blocks/copy-button.tsx` hand-rolled the whole thing.

**`done?: boolean`. The state is the app's and the drawing is the system's** — §29's own rule for `onDismiss` one component over. A control that ran its own timer would forget on reload and would decide, for every app, how long "just now" lasts.

**It does not block the press, and that is the difference from `loading`.** Loading blocks because the action is still running; done means it finished, and copying twice is an ordinary thing to want. A button that goes dead for two seconds after succeeding is worse than either state.

**It draws a tick and says nothing.** A drawing is not a name: assistive technology announces the accessible name, so a labelled button changes its label and an `iconOnly` one changes `aria-label`. The system cannot write those words — they are in the app's language, not its. The prop's doc asks for them; nothing can enforce it.

**THE MOTION IS A SWAP, NOT A DRAW, and the distinction from the mark family is real.** §8 answers "a tick appears" already: a checkbox's tick draws along its own stroke, `pathLength={1}` so CSS never measures it, IN and never OUT. That is a state you SET and it persists. A done state is a momentary report that reverts, and there are two glyphs rather than one appearing on an empty box — drawing in and out on a two-second round trip reads fussy. So the tick arrives the way a small object arrives: it scales up out of a seed under a blur and paints in, and the button's own glyph leaves the same way.

Kushagra asked for exactly that (*"a small scale down + blur + appear, like we have done motion in other tools"*), and the first counter-proposal — the floating family's own entry recipe — was refused before it was built: that recipe is licensed by MASS (§24 lets a dialog blur because depth of field is a property of a large box, and blur is the only thing the system may honestly animate on content it does not own), and a 16px glyph at a ~1.17px painted stroke has none. `doneBlur` is small on purpose and is the dial most likely to go to zero, with the bench left in — the rim-saturate precedent.

**Clocks are §8's existing two, and no new one is minted**: the fade is paint (short, eased, no spring), the scale is geometry (a spring). In on the tick's own `--motion-mark` and the calm spring, out on `--motion-press` and `stiff` — which is the system's exit spring by name, because an exit that bounces is an object that did not mean to leave. The asymmetry is entirely in the durations, §8's own shape for a pair sharing one character.

**Both glyphs are mounted always, and that is the mechanism.** React would unmount the outgoing one the instant `done` flips, and an unmounted element cannot leave — the exit would simply not exist. Stacked in one grid cell they cannot reflow around each other either. So passing the prop AT ALL, even `false`, is what mounts the tick; a button that never asks renders exactly as it always has, law-asserted as the absence of the anatomy.

**THE WIDTH TRAVELS** (Kushagra's call, put as a choice: travel it or reserve the wider word). `Copy` → `Copied` is a real geometry change, so it rides the geometry clock. `interpolate-size: allow-keywords` is what makes an intrinsic width interpolable at all, and where an engine lacks it the width snaps — which is what it does today, so the feature is additive and its fallback is the current behaviour. The channel lives in the shared control layer as a HOOK (`--kui-ct-width`, `0s`) so the transition list keeps one home and nothing else in the system starts animating: the first spelling put a live `inline-size` in that list and would have animated the width of every field, select trigger and segmented track, which two of the skeleton's own clock laws caught.

**The tick is the checkbox's tick, and finding that out found a third home.** `system/glyphs.ts` holds the paths the package draws itself once a second component needs one — path DATA rather than components, because each consumer wraps it differently and a shared component would grow a prop per difference. The source law written with it caught `menu.tsx` writing the same tick a third time. **The mounted law comparing the two `d` attributes could not fail** and its own sabotage said so: once both import the constant they agree by construction, and they would go on agreeing if one drifted back to a matching literal. What is checkable is that nobody writes the path down — the stroke's own 2026-08-23 source-law argument, one fact over.

Two defects in the first implementation, both found by laws rather than by reading: an icon-only button grew TWO swaps (the leading arm did not exclude `iconOnly`, so a button with no leading slot wrapped `undefined`), and **the caller's own glyph fell out of the shared icon-box rule** — that rule matches an svg one or two levels down, the swap adds a third, and an `<svg>` with no intrinsic size is 300x150 by CSS's replaced-element default. The fix is the shared layer's, not the component's: `[data-glyph]` is a third arm on the icon rule, a structural attribute like `[data-slot]` and never a component's class. `recipes.test.ts` refused the component-local spelling, which is the law that has kept that ladder in one place through four components.

+204 gzipped bytes. 13 mounted laws, ten sabotage passes; one sabotage survived and cost a law its place (above), and one law was repaired on the way — the skeleton's clock law read its paint channels by POSITION (`durations.slice(0, 3)`), which is a claim about where they sit in the list rather than about them sharing a clock, so appending a channel failed it for the wrong reason.

## 42. ContextMenu (2026-09-02) — the menu family's second placement

**Not a second family, and the export count is the argument.** Everything a context menu shows is a menu: the same panel, the same rows, the same glyphs, the same portal contract, the same flight machinery. What differs is where it comes from and what summons it. So it ships **three exports** — `ContextMenu`, `ContextMenuTrigger`, `ContextMenuContent` — and you compose the rows out of `MenuItem`, `MenuGroup`, `MenuLabel`, `MenuCheckboxItem`, `MenuRadioGroup` and `MenuSub`.

shadcn/ui ships a full parallel set, and Base UI re-exports the very same components under both names — `ContextMenu.Item` IS `Menu.Item`, in their source. A second name for one thing is the fault, not the fix: this package already refused a shadcn name when the name LIED (§26, `TabsTrigger`, because a trigger opens a floating layer and a tab opens nothing), and it refuses one here when the name is merely a duplicate. The rows work inside it because they are the same components, not because anything was wired to make them — which is exactly what a mounted law asserts.

**`ContextMenuTrigger` is a component whose job is to catch an event on its children**, which this system has refused twice (`BreadcrumbSeparator`, `ComposerRow`'s grouping parts — both layout wearing a part's name). It survives §10's criterion because the work is not visual and there is a lot of it: suppressing the platform's own menu, the long press that stands in for a right-click on touch, the point the panel is placed at, and the state the region wears while its menu is up. It PAINTS NOTHING — no fill, no border, no cursor — because a right-click is a gesture over content you can already see, and a region that announced itself would be a control. `render` is the escape for a region that is its own element.

**Placement is the system's, and here there is not even a default to override.** `MenuContent` takes `side`/`align`/`sideOffset`; this takes none, because there is nothing a call site could usefully say — the panel's corner goes on the cursor and the viewport decides which corner. It deliberately does NOT pass `useRestingAnchor()`: that virtual anchor reports a trigger's resting box, and handing it over would replace the point with the region.

**IT FLIES FROM THE POINT — the family's entry, unchanged, with the right box.** The first spelling got this wrong and Kushagra caught it by eye (*"something is wrong with animation, and I dont get it, we literally had to copy paste menu"*). I had given the panel a pose of its own — the landed box breathing from 0.92 — on the argument that a region has no silhouette worth flying out of. Half right: the region IS the wrong box, and the answer is to give the flight the right one, not to take the flight away. Measured, the pose read as a twitch — 8% of a panel over 345ms, with no unfurl and no body squish, where a menu grows out of a small box.

**So the seed is a SIZE, and it is zero.** `seedSize` on the direction context is the one thing this component supplies: `anchor` stays "which node is this component's in-flow one" and this is "what does the panel come out of". A size and not a rect, which is the whole of why it needs no event handler — Base UI's positioner has already put the panel's corner on the point, so the silhouette's POSITION is the panel's own corner and there is nothing to measure. The x and y offsets are zero, which is `beside`'s own sentence (§22, 2026-08-17) on both axes rather than one, and direction-blind for the same reason.

**That deleted everything the first design invented**: `CONTEXT_PLAN`, `ContextFloatingBody`, the `kui-menu-point` mark, the self-keyed pose in `menu.css`, `contextEntry` and `--context-seed`. A context menu's panel is now spelled exactly as a submenu's — the family's classes, no mark of its own, no recipe of its own — which is what "copy paste menu" means when it is true, and a law asserts the class list to keep the invented pose from coming back. `kui-menu-anchored` stays off for the reason it is off on a submenu: the width floor means "never narrower than the trigger you pressed", and a point has no width. **Net −84 bytes against the version before it**, which is the evidence the second answer was the simpler one.

**The cursor-tracking spelling was refused by a shipped law, and the law was right.** The first repair recorded the point through `onContextMenu` and `onPointerDown` handlers on the region, and `recipes.test.ts` reported an interaction-time handler — a listener on every press over a canvas, to learn a coordinate the layout already knew. Being refused is what produced the simpler mechanism.

**Two instrument findings, each of which produced `none` on a correct package.** `settle()` lands a panel by writing `transition: none !important` inline, and `[data-unfurling][data-seed]` declares `transition: none` deliberately, because the seed frame must not animate into itself. A motion law here has to decline to settle and wait for the flight to land under its own power. **The third was WRONG and the audit 2026-09-02 corrected it in all three homes:** the laws opened with `detail: 1` under a comment saying a synthetic right-click without it "opens a panel that never animates". `data-instant="click"` has not zeroed a clock since 2026-08-19 — `FLIES_ANYWAY` exempts it in the runner and surfaces.css excludes it in the stylesheet, held in agreement by a law — and a REAL right-click's `contextmenu` carries `detail: 0` (measured: the mousedown is 1, the contextmenu is 0). So the fixture produced a state no person can produce and removed the shipped one from every law in the file. The `detail` is gone.

**The laws that mattered did not exist at first, and their absence was demonstrated.** Under the first design, swapping the panel back onto the family's anchored plan left all thirteen laws green — the seed law read the STYLESHEET (true either way) and the clocks law read a LANDED panel (also true either way), so neither could see which box the entry was posed from, which is the entire decision. They now read what the runner WROTE: `--kui-seed-w/h/r` and `--kui-from-x/y` are all `0px` for a summoned panel and the trigger's real box and offset for an opened one, with a mounted Menu beside them as the vacuity guard. The clocks are asserted as an AGREEMENT with that Menu rather than as a list of their own, which is what "the family's entry, unchanged" means as a claim. Deterministic, where a first-frame measurement would be a race.

**A reduced-motion law was written for the invented pose and went with it.** It is worth recording because it could not fail and took three spellings to learn why: a mounted reading cannot isolate a component's own stand-down, since the harness's stillness sheet zeroes every transition and under `asksForStillness` the panel never gets a flight — so it read `none` on a correct package AND on one with no guard at all. The two source-reading spellings that followed passed against a deleted guard too, one by walking the CSSOM and finding nothing, the other by splitting the stylesheet on `@media` and swallowing the rules that follow the block. With no recipe of its own, the panel takes the family's guard and needs no law here at all.

**Audited 2026-09-02 (ultracode, six lenses each with its own adversarial verifier; 23 raised, 15 survived, deduping to nine defects) and the four that reach a person are fixed.** Three of them are ONE mistake three times, and it is this repo's most-recorded shape: **a mechanism written correctly for the case its author had in mind, applied to a case they did not.**

**`seedSize` leaked into every submenu.** It means "THIS panel was summoned out of a point", and it is read by every descendant flight — while `MenuSub` builds its context by spreading the parent's (`{ ...parentDir, anchor }`). So a submenu of a context menu wrote `--kui-seed-h: 0px` and `--kui-seed-r: 0px` where the identical markup under a plain Menu writes its trigger ROW's own 15px box and corner, and `--kui-seed-dy` then translated that flat sliver half a seed ABOVE the row it was supposed to come out of — §22's "a side-opening panel keeps the row's height and corner" silently deleted whenever the outer menu was a context menu. `MenuSub` states the field back to its unset meaning; a submenu is never summoned, because it always has a trigger row.

**"The corner is on the point" is true only of an UNSHIFTED panel.** A context menu's positioner runs `shift({ crossAxis })` with `flip.mainAxis` disabled, so a panel that would overflow the bottom slides UP while `data-side` stays `bottom` and nothing in the placement attributes says it moved. Measured in the builder: a click 408px down an 800px window put the panel's top at 301 — 107px above the pointer — with the seed painted at the panel's own corner, so the panel grew out of a point a third of its own height from the cursor. The offsets are no longer literals: Base UI's own `transformOrigin` middleware already detects that state and publishes the anchor point's y in `--transform-origin`, so the runner READS it. That is the honest fix rather than the tempting one — tracking the cursor is exactly the spelling the "no JS at interaction time" law refused when this component was built, and the number is already in the DOM.

**"Already placed" is true only of a reopen on the SAME anchor.** The 2026-08-20 caught-reopen branch continues a panel from where it is rather than replaying its entry, and every member it was written for — menu, select, popover — reopens on its trigger. A summoned panel is the family's first member whose second gesture carries a NEW place, so the catch produced exactly what that reversal was made to stop: measured with a real right-click, the same popup element moved 289px inline and 155px block between two frames, at full opacity, with no seed and no unfurl. A summoned panel has no taken-back gesture to protect — the only way to reopen one is to summon it again — so it always flies.

**And the fourth: the platform's menu drew over our own.** Base UI suppresses `contextmenu` for the region and for its backdrop, a `position: fixed; inset: 0` sibling of the positioner — so the suppression covered the whole viewport EXCEPT the one rectangle the panel occupies, and a right-click on a row came back `defaultPrevented: false` with Chrome's menu over the Kookie one and the Kookie one still open underneath. §42 names suppressing the platform menu as one of the four jobs licensing `ContextMenuTrigger`; doing it for the region and not for the surface the region draws is half a job. The panel prevents it, scoped by a context rather than by `seedSize` — the two are different claims, which is precisely why a submenu must inherit one and not the other.

**Five of the fourteen laws could not fail, and every one is the DEGENERATE-FIXTURE rule.** The `size` axis had no reader anywhere in the repo — `ContextMenu` supplies its own `MenuSizeContext.Provider`, a path no Menu law covers, and deleting it left all fourteen green while `size="4"` rendered at index 2; the law that should have caught it was titled "byte-identically to a Menu's **at the same index**" with both sides at the default. The §20 agreement law read five attribute strings and no computed value, under its own docstring claiming the panel "must resolve the same ink a row inside the in-flow Theme does" — an attribute comes back equal because it was written verbatim. Its hostile set was also missing `contrast="high"`, the one axis that re-declares the whole ink and border palette. And every fixture opened at one point in a window where no shift can occur, which is why the seed law asserted `--kui-from-x/y === "0px"` as the guarantee — **a law that would have failed on the correct value** in every cell where the panel has to move.

**One sabotage was kept after it SURVIVED, rather than the law being stretched to cover it.** A rule painting the panel red on coarse pointers hits the twin as well, so an agreement law goes on agreeing — recorded in the law itself, with the falsification that does belong to it (a `PortalScope` that resets an axis) and a note that "no rule paints the panel red" is the stylesheet's claim, not §20's. 18 mounted laws, nine sabotage passes; the ledger above is corrected in the same commit.

**Zero gzipped bytes for the whole placement** — it ships no CSS, so there is nothing for the budget to move; the `+84` this line carried until the 2026-09-02 audit was a stale first-design figure. 18 mounted laws, nine sabotage passes.

## Open questions / deferred

**A dialog could not scroll its body, and one composition silently lost content — RAISED 2026-08-20, CLOSED 2026-08-21.** Two separate faults wore one symptom. The first was never the dialog's: `max-block-size: 100%` on a scroll viewport cannot resolve against an indefinite parent, so any pane sized with `max-height` handed the viewport its CONTENT's height (a 160px pane holding a 496px viewport, 336px gone) — fixed 2026-08-20 by making the scroller itself a flex column. The second was the dialog's own, and it was worse than logged: `height` did not work either, because `.kui-dialog-body` stands between the pane and the caller's children and every rule in the surface layer's scroll block asks about a direct child. Closed by the two clauses in §3 above — the body is a column when it holds a scroller, and the member rules reach through it. The same measurement found the fault in Card that made this more than a dialog bug: a plain pane with neighbours had no column at all, which is the clause beside it. Six laws, six sabotage passes.

**And whether it should cap itself at the window closed the same day (Kushagra): it should not.** The premise I raised it on was wrong — I called the uncapped shape "two scrollbars", and the page is scroll-locked while a dialog is open, so the only two that can ever appear are the panel's own and the list's. What is really true of the uncapped shape is quieter and was worth measuring: a list with no stated height does not scroll at all, the panel grows to hold it whole, and the title scrolls away with everything else. That is a composition the call site chose, not a defect. The height stays the call site's, exactly as a Card's does.

Also unrepaired, and found by the same audit: **a `mt="bleed"` INSIDE a scroll viewport pulls content somewhere a scroll container can never reach.** The bleed resolves against the PANE's padding, and a viewport only restates that padding on the block sides the scroller actually bled — so in a card with a heading above the list, a vertical bleed inside the list pulls it 24px into a strip with no scroll offset that can show it. One custom property cannot hold two different per-axis answers, and the honest reading is that a vertical bleed inside a scroller whose top is not the pane's top has nothing to bleed TO. The inline axis is exact (measured 0.00), because that side is restated unconditionally.

**Closed 2026-08-21, all of it — and the shape was one mistake in four places: a wrapper that declares a few props and drops the rest in silence.** Measured with `id`, `aria-label`, `data-testid` and an `onKeyDown` on `DialogContent`: none reached the element, none failed to type-check. That is the Select audit's blocked-`id` finding in a third home, and here it had a second victim — a dialog with no `DialogTitle` had **no accessible name and no way to be given one** (`role=dialog`, `aria-labelledby` null, `aria-label` null, no warning), because the obvious repair was accepted and discarded. Every part now takes its element's ordinary props, spread BEFORE the system's identity so a call site cannot take `data-size`, and a dev build warns when a panel has neither a title nor a label.

**`onOpenChange` gained the reason and the refusal.** It was `(open: boolean) => void`, so a dialog could be told that it closed and never why — which makes the guard every form dialog owes, "you have unsaved changes", impossible to write. Base UI hands over both and the wrapper was throwing them away. The second argument is now `{ reason, event, cancel }`: the reason strings are Base UI's own, kept rather than translated (they are already plain, and a mapping table is a second home plus a way to drift), but the union is declared in this package, and three laws provoke a real Escape, a real outside press and a real close press and read the string back — which is what catches a rename upstream. `cancel()` refuses the dismissal.

**Content wider than the panel was being deleted, and that was one day old and not the dialog's.** A pane clips since the bleed shipped (§3, 2026-08-20), which is what lets a child reach the edge without squaring the corner — and it turned every overflow from a visible spill into a silent removal. Measured in a size-2 dialog: an ordinary share link at 481px inside a 440px panel, a 12-column table at 718, a `<pre>` at 952. Two halves ship against it.
