Text that CAN wrap now does — `overflow-wrap: break-word` on the Theme root, which inherits, so one declaration covers a component's text, a call site's text and a portal's; `break-word` and not `anywhere`, because `anywhere` also shrinks min-content width and would quietly re-size every flex and grid item in the library. What is left is content that must not wrap, where the answer is a ScrollArea and the pane rule now makes that work — so a **dev warning** on a clipping pane (Card, Surface, Dialog: the three that hold content a call site wrote) says the overflow is unreachable and names the fix. A warning and not a repair, because there is none to make: CSS resolves `overflow-x: auto` beside a clipped `overflow-y` to `hidden`, which is a scroll container, which is what the flight rules spent two days keeping panes out of.

**CLOSED THE SAME DAY, and the document said otherwise for two (corrected 2026-08-22).** `Surface` shipped on 2026-08-20 — hours after this was raised — and its own sentence names this exact case: *"a bed inside a card that holds something quieter — a code block, a settings group"*. The builder's export block is the working example, and it was converted from a Card to a Surface for precisely this reason. Measured in both modes: on a white card a ground reads 0.966, and on a dark card (0.080) a ground reads 0.070 — one step quieter in each, with the family-less hairline. What remains genuinely open is the narrower half this paragraph also asked: whether a ground takes TONE, for a tinted callout-like region. It does not today, and no composition has needed it. The paragraph below is kept for that question and for the argument that produced it.

**The original entry (raised 2026-08-20, the Card audit's nesting pass) —** Kushagra, on card-in-card: nested cards "feel wrong… they're designed to be independent" — and the literature agrees on the mechanism (Apple's boxes guidance: a border is a strong signal, nesting borders reads busy and constrained, subgroup with padding and alignment; Material's card definition: a card is a single self-contained instance; the card metaphor itself — objects don't contain objects). The system's verdict: card-in-card is RENDERED correctly (nested seals separate by border, law-tested) and DISCOURAGED — the preview's nesting section shows the quiet vocabulary instead (spacing, the hairline, type hierarchy). What the pass exposed is that the quiet vocabulary is one voice SHORT: Apple's third grouping tool is the background step (the iOS secondary/tertiary background ladder — an inset region one shade off the surface, no border), and Kookie cannot say it. Fields and marks have wells; surfaces don't; Box has no fill prop by design (appearance is resolved output, §13), so an app wanting an inset group today reaches for the style escape or a nested card — the exact pattern being discouraged. The likely shape is a Well/Inset surface (the tinted-surface direction the look axis once reserved for `filled`'s growth — the axis is deleted since 2026-08-20, so this open question is now that direction's only home), with §11's questions attached: does it take tone (a tinted callout-like region), does it host controls, is it a Card variant or its own component. Waits for a real composition that needs it — the tone-set rule — and the Card preview's nesting caption points here so the need has somewhere to register.

**A control that has no content of its own is still asked to hug it — and a flex ROW has no way to overrule it (raised 2026-08-20, Kushagra, from the builder: "even here if I wanted the two buttons to fill, I have no way").** Two halves of one gap, both measured in a mounted browser.

**The controls.** `.kui-control` is `inline-flex`, so every control shrink-wraps. That is right wherever the width IS the content — a button is as wide as its label, a checkbox as wide as its box, a Kbd as wide as its cap. It is wrong for the members with nothing inside: an empty TextField falls through to the browser's own `size` default of roughly twenty characters, a number nobody in this system chose. Measured in the shipped `/preview`, the four filter-field specimens render 174 / 204 / 235 / 270px — twenty characters at four type steps, tracking the font rather than any designed ladder. TextArea has the same shape (`cols` is refused, so its inline size is also the platform's), and a Select trigger's would jitter with the chosen option. **Progress and Slider already opted out** with `inline-size: 100%`, for exactly this reason — an instrument has no content either — and progress.css records the safe spelling: fill the space, but stay shrinkable, because `flex: none` beside `inline-size: 100%` shipped 381px of bar inside a 300px row. So the likely answer is that the contentless members join the instruments, and the content-shaped ones keep hugging. Not done here because it moves every field in every app.

**The parent.** Flexbox puts main-axis distribution on the CHILD (`flex-grow`) and offers the parent nothing: measured, `justify-content: stretch`, `justify-items: stretch` and `align-items: stretch` all leave a button at its natural 75px in a row. Grid puts it on the parent — `justify-items: stretch` is the default, and two buttons in two equal columns measure 296px each — and a column stretches its children because there width is the CROSS axis. So of the three layouts this package ships, the parent can decide in Grid and in Stack, and cannot in a Flex row. That is CSS, not a Kookie choice. What CSS does allow is a parent RULE rather than a parent property: `& > * { flex: 1 1 0 }` measures 296/296 (equal, natural widths discarded) and `& > * { flex-grow: 1 }` measures 273/319 (proportional, the longer label stays wider) — one declaration either way, and AlertDialog already does the equivalent with its own two buttons in grid. So the open question is whether `Flex` earns a distribute-style prop, which of those two meanings it takes, and whether a layout reaching into children it does not own is acceptable when dividing space is precisely a layout's job (§3).

**Two call sites are visibly wrong today, and they are the evidence the gap is real rather than theoretical**: `apps/docs/app/preview/specimens.tsx:198` and `showcase.tsx:622` each wrap a search field in `<Box flexGrow="1">`, which grows the box and not the field — measured 174px of field inside 755px of wrapper, and 204 inside 288. The code states an intent it does not get, and it looks correct while reading. Left unfixed on purpose: the one-line workaround is to make the wrapper a `Stack` (a column of one item, which stretches its child — measured 184 → 195px), and adopting a trick nobody would guess is the wrong thing to spread through the docs while the real answer is still open.

**Sectioned multi-accent has no mechanism, and §7 stopped claiming one (raised 2026-08-20, the doc–code sweep).** §7 documented a `Theme accentColor` prop and a worked nested-Theme example as the sanctioned path; the prop has never existed, so what looked like a shipped capability was a written one. The absence is not obviously a gap — nobody has asked to rebrand a subtree, and the tone set's config-level widening already answers the genuine dual-brand case with an honestly-priced second scale. What is genuinely unanswered is whether a *scope* can rebind `accent` at all: it would mean re-declaring the family's whole role bundle inside that scope, which is mechanically the shape `contrast="high"` already uses, at the same cost of one more generated scale. Recorded as open rather than designed, because a mechanism with no consumer is the entropy this repo keeps paying for. §7 carries the refusal.

**~~The row family's lit identity is written in Base UI's vocabulary~~ (raised 2026-08-09, audit) — CLOSED 2026-08-23 with `Row`, and the guessed answer was the wrong shape.** The entry below is kept verbatim because the guess is instructive. What it proposed was "a Kookie-owned lit attribute the family stamps, with Base UI's two mapped onto it at the Menu boundary" — a translation layer at a COMPONENT boundary. The distinction it was trying to draw is not per component: the same `Row` is roving inside a command palette and not roving in a settings list, so no component boundary can answer it. It is per INSTANCE, and the marker turned out to belong on the row (`data-hover-lit`, §21), stamped when nobody else is driving it. Base UI's vocabulary was never the problem; the missing fact was who owns the cursor. The prediction the entry made about the SECOND member came true exactly as written — `shell.css` grew a private `:hover` rule on 2026-08-16 — and it is deleted now.

The original entry: **The row family's lit identity is written in Base UI's vocabulary (raised 2026-08-09, audit).** §21 declares the family family-first — menu item, command item, list item, sidebar button, one CSS contract on day one — and the contract it locked in is `.kui-row[data-highlighted]` / `[data-popup-open]`, both Base UI *Menu* part state. `data-popup-open` is a submenu-trigger fact no list item has; `data-highlighted` comes from Base UI's roving-highlight machinery, which nothing else emits. So the next member — a Sidebar button, a List item — matches the `@media (hover: hover)` stand-down that returns every `.kui-row` to its rest fill, and matches no rule that ever lights it: the stand-down's cost with none of its benefit, and its author's likely response is a `:hover` rule in a component sheet, which is exactly the per-member drift the family-first argument was made to prevent. §21 argues the family-first choice and never names this cost. The answer is probably a Kookie-owned lit attribute the family stamps, with Base UI's two mapped onto it at the Menu boundary — but that is a decision for the SECOND member, not a repair for the first, so it waits for one.


**Forced colors is NOT SUPPORTED, and that is a decision (2026-08-07, Kushagra: "our library is too premature to solve it").** Windows Contrast Themes — `forced-colors: active` — replaces the page's palette with the user's own, and this library has no handling for it anywhere: `grep -rn "forced-color" src` returns nothing. The consequence is measured rather than assumed, and it is real: under emulation, a **checked radio is indistinguishable from an unchecked one**, because the accent fill is forced to Canvas white while the indicator dot stays white — white on white. Assistive tech is unaffected (the hidden input still reports `checked`), so the loss falls exactly on sighted users of the setting. It predates the axis work: it shipped with Checkbox on 2026-08-05 and survived the 40-agent audit.

It is declined rather than fixed because fixing one control is worse than fixing none — the mode is all-or-nothing per page, so a radio that answers it beside a slider that does not is a *more* confusing screen, not a less confusing one. Supporting it properly means a pass over every control (marks, fills, focus rings, disabled states, the material) plus laws that drive the media query, and that is a body of work this library is too early for. Recorded here so each audit round stops rediscovering it as a defect: it is a known, measured, accepted gap. `prefers-contrast: more` and `contrast="high"` are a different mechanism and ARE supported (§7).

**Color:** section 7 is implemented and law-tested (`src/tokens/color.ts`, 106 laws). What remains is not mechanism:
- **Named extra accent slots** for genuine dual-brand cases (the `accentAlt` path).
- Chroma varies about 2x across hues at the solid band, because each hue sits at its own cusp: indigo holds C .30 where cyan holds C .15. Intrinsic to sRGB, narrowed but not removed by P3. It only shows when two distant hues ship together (a cyan `accent` beside a red `destructive`), and the lever is per-tone `vividness`. Deliberately **not** normalized automatically across the configured tones: that would mean adding a destructive red silently changes how the accent looks, and action at a distance is worse than a documented asymmetry.

**Recipes and axes (sections 8-11):**
- Judge the **material** recipes against real backdrops: three or four hostile photos, a real control, both modes. Expect blur to move +/-6px and alpha +/-0.1. Confirm the brightness floor actually holds. Measure `backdrop-filter` cost on a mid-tier device before the radii lock.
- The **chroma threshold** below which `--accent-solid` remaps to step 12 (section 7), and the L-deltas for its hover and press. Tuning, not architecture.
- Where exactly `--accent-label` sits between steps 11 and 12, per mode.
- ~~How **`quiet`** actually renders~~ — closed at the first real Button (2026-08-03): bare at rest, `transparent` literally, entering the ramp at hover (transparent → soft → soft-hover). Law-tested across every tone. `quiet + bordered` covers the faint-containment case the tint would have served.
- ~~Theme `material` **naming**: `material` vs `allowTranslucency` vs `materials`.~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-16: `material`.** It shipped as a Theme axis that day and §5's props table records the alternative being retired with it — the row explicitly supersedes the `off | on` policy-ceiling reading (`allowTranslucency`), because the value names what the app is BUILT of rather than whether translucency is permitted. Struck 2026-08-26; it had sat in this list as open for ten days after the decision.

**Radius / layout:**
- Concentric radius nesting (inner R = outer R minus inset P). Apple builds the system around it; we defer to v1+1. Confirmed taste-multiplier.
- Shape-by-size fork: keep one shape with scaled radius (current) vs Apple's capsule-at-large-sizes. Leaning keep.
- Surface size cap in `radius="full"` (confirm clamp for dialogs/sheets).
- Space scale top-out (64 component/section level) vs one fixed scale to 128, with responsive page gutters via `clamp()` as a later layer.

**API:**
- Naming of the per-component escape prop (`UNSAFE_` vs `override`). The name is the deterrent.
- ~~**The adornment spelling.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-04, Kushagra: Button renames to `leading` / `trailing`.** `iconEnd` is not RTL-correct, and a trailing slot frequently holds a *control* rather than an icon — which the new slot-hosting rule (§4) makes routine rather than exceptional. The API break is theoretical while the package is unpublished, and the cost of two spellings is permanent.
- ~~**TextField ships no `render` escape.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-05: refused, and pinned by a type law.** Everywhere else `render` swaps the one element that *is* the component; here there are two, and neither can move — the wrapper holds a border the input cannot hold once a slot sits inside it, and the input must stay an `<input>` or the platform wiring the component exists to preserve goes with it. `render` would have to silently mean one of them.
- ~~**iOS zooms the page on focus for any input under 16px.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-05: the input's font takes a floor from the pointer world.** The device axis was only most of the answer, and the hole was bigger than the size 1 it was thought to be: an iPad in landscape is past the handheld width threshold, so it reads as `desktop`, gets the desktop type ladder — 14px at size 2 — and Safari still zooms. The signal has to be *what touches the screen*, so `--input-font-floor` rides the pointer axis (0 on fine, 16 on coarse) and `max()`es the INPUT's font size only. The box keeps its designed height and the label its designed step, so a size-1 field is still a size-1 field. Deliberately not solved by lifting the type ladder: size 1 is the caption step, and a phone that cannot render small secondary text has lost something real to fix one control.
- **Pointer: the numbers.** The coarse sets' values — §16, judged in the 4b matrix. ~~Whether a hybrid device gets coarse geometry via `any-pointer`~~ — **closed 2026-08-05: no.** `any-pointer: coarse` means "touch exists somewhere on this machine" and would permanently inflate a mouse-driven touchscreen laptop, overriding density, a whole axis built so people could choose airiness. `pointer` tracks how the machine is being used *right now* — flip a 2-in-1 into tablet mode and the controls grow, flip it back and they shrink — and every designed cell already clears the WCAG 24px minimum, so what `any-pointer` would buy is only the stricter 44 target. Capability is not use.
- ~~**Device: the tablet band.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-05: there is no tablet band, because the single band that needed one was doing two jobs.** Apple ships one Dynamic Type table for iOS and iPadOS, so a tablet's reading type is a phone's; the only thing that genuinely differed was display type, and that is a line-length fact keyed on width. Split into `handheld` (touch) and `narrow` (width) — section 17. Still open there: both sets of picks, both thresholds, the container version of the narrow band, and the not-yet-claimed platform-minimum type floor. **Newly open, and the real tablet deliverable: window-level size classes for app-shell layout** — nothing in the system answers "which interface should this app show" (container tiers answer a different question), which is what Figma-on-iPad is deciding.
- ~~**LIVE DEFECT (found 2026-08-05, building Checkbox's preview): a Box collapses to zero inline size whenever it must shrink-wrap.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-08: containment is opt-in via the `container` prop** (section 2 — a plain Box hugs its content like a div; only a marked Box is measurable, and only a marked Box pays the shrink-wrap price, now behind a dev warning). The original record, kept for the escapes it documents and the two independent discoveries: Every `.kui-box` carries `container-type: inline-size` so tiers can query it (§2) — and that applies size containment in the inline axis, which means **the contents never contribute to the box's own width**. Measured in Chromium against the shipped stylesheet: a Box as a flex-ROW item computes to **0px** where a plain `<div>` computes to 70px; `align-items: flex-start` in a column, `display: inline-flex`, `width: max-content` and `width: fit-content` are all 0 as well. It renders correctly only where something else hands it a definite width — a block context, or the default `align-items: stretch` — which is why every demo built so far has looked fine and no law caught it. `<Flex><Box>…</Box><Box>…</Box></Flex>` is the most ordinary composition the library offers, and today it silently produces zero-width children with spilling content. Not fixed here (it is nowhere near Checkbox's scope): the fix is a real decision, since containment is what makes a Box queryable, and the candidates — only containerise a Box that has tier props, or `container-type: normal` plus an opt-in, or an intrinsic-size escape — each trade a different part of §2's nearest-ancestor semantics. Needs a mounted law in both directions the moment it is chosen. **Independently re-found the same day by the docs app** (the first real consumer), which is worth recording because the two discoveries landed on different escapes and the pair is the whole picture: it hit a header row holding a nested button group — again the most ordinary composition there is — and shipped around it with `flexGrow`, which takes width from space distribution rather than from content measurement and is therefore the one sizing route containment leaves open. Stating `width` works for the same reason; restructuring so the Boxes are column items works because those stretch. Note also that the generator's own comment has named this for `.kui-theme` since containment shipped — so the behaviour was documented for one of the two elements that carry it and unnoticed on the other, which is why it reads as a surprise rather than a known cost. Whatever is chosen, the escapes above are what the docs currently lean on.
- **Container tiers: wrapping re-targets — NARROWED 2026-08-08 by the containment opt-in.** A plain Box around a subtree no longer changes anything (it is transparent to measurement, law-pinned); only wrapping in a `container` Box re-targets the children's tiers, which is now the prop doing exactly what it says. Recorded 2026-08-04 after a full re-litigation of container-keyed tiers (window-based re-rejected; see LOG). What remains open is only whether that residual deliberate re-targeting needs naming in section 2's cost list.
- **Density: the numbers.** Heights per level, the step offsets, and any per-cell overrides. Also the level names and count (`comfortable` may understate the airy end). Surface padding takes density through the layout-space layer — decided 2026-08-04 (sections 3, 12); the per-level layout-space picks are taste like the rest, the gutter-band hold included. Architecture settled (section 12); values are taste, and they need the size-by-density matrix in the docs app before they can be judged.
- **Scale: if it ever ships.** The factor stays wired and the prop is deferred (sections 5, 13). Reopen only when a real need names the steps, and ship it as designed steps rather than a free multiplier.
- RTL / `dir`. Deferred, architectural room left.

**From the 2026-08-06 audit (REVIEW) — confirmed defects held back because each is a system decision, not a repair:**
- **Does a Button rendered as a link announce as a link?** `<Button render={<a href/>}>` emits `role="button"`: Base UI infers a non-native button and stamps the role plus `tabindex`, so the docs site exposes exactly ONE link to a screen-reader's link rotor and Space — which `role=button` promises — does not activate it. The composition is blessed by §1 and by a law whose own title calls it "a link that looks like a button", so the law pins the behaviour that is wrong. The call: when the render target is an anchor carrying `href`, the native link role should survive, and Base UI's non-button branch should be reserved for genuinely non-interactive elements. Deferred only because it changes a shipped a11y contract and rewrites the law that guards it.
- **Can a TextField shrink?** The wrapper has no `min-width`, and its intrinsic width comes from the `<input>`'s UA `size=20` (~174px measured), so as a flex item it refuses to shrink and `/matrix` scrolls horizontally at 375px — the width where the coarse geometry is judged. `min-width: 0` on the wrapper is the one-liner; whether a field should be shrinkable BY DEFAULT, or whether that is the call site's to state, is the actual question (§4's "components never own outer spacing" has no sibling rule for intrinsic width).
- ~~**`.kui-surface:where(button, a)` resets seven UA properties and not `display`.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-19: the interactive arm states `display: block`** (Kushagra, after the per-component preview's in-use demo reproduced it visually — an inline anchor holding block children shatters into per-line fragments, and every prior specimen had been a grid item, which is blockified for free, so the suite stayed green around it). An interactive card is now the plain card's own box, law-read as the computed `display` on all three elements in plain flow; a call site that wants a shrink-wrapped pressable card states a width, the same as for any block. The original record: a plain Card is a block and `<Card render={<a href/>}>` — the pattern §10 names by hand — was inline, taking a line box and shrink-wrapping.
- **`.kui-surface` never zeroes margin**, so `<Card render={<figure/>}>` or `<blockquote/>` inherits UA margins and owns outer spacing — against the non-negotiable that `.kui-type` (`margin: 0`, citing §3) and the layout mechanism both honour. One declaration plus the mounted law it earns.
- **How is selection state spelled on a control the system does not have yet?** The docs' pickers convey "selected" through tone and emphasis alone, so the accessibility tree is byte-identical before and after a choice changes — including on the contrast picker, which is itself the accessibility setting. `aria-pressed` is expressible through Button's props today, but the right answer is a system one (Segmented Control / Toggle Button are on the component list), and patching the docs page would spend the question rather than answer it.

**From the 2026-08-08 audit (REVIEW) — observations recorded so they stop being rediscovered:**
- ~~**`contrast="high"` reaches nothing on a progress bar.**~~ **SUPERSEDED — re-measured 2026-08-26: the WELL moves, the fill does not.** The original observation (well and fill byte-identical between normal and high in both appearances, 3.06:1 light and 4.13:1 dark) was true when it was written and is false now: `--color-track` rests at `var(--neutral-a4)` and the high-contrast scopes re-declare it as the opaque `var(--neutral-6)` in both appearances, which reaches the progress well, the slider rail and the switch's off-track together. `--tone-solid` is NOT re-declared under the setting (only its hover and active steps are), so the fill holds and the contrast the setting buys comes entirely from the channel darkening under it. That still follows the 2026-08-07 rule rather than breaking it — the instrument's channel is a well the conformance surface treats as an edge would be treated — and the open system question is unchanged: whether the instrument family owes high contrast an accommodation beyond the well's own step. **Neither Progress nor Slider has a law reading the well across contrasts**, which is why this drifted silently for eighteen days; the law is owed by those components' own files.
- **A Separator in a wrong-axis container is invisible but stays in the a11y tree.** `<Stack><Separator orientation="vertical"/></Stack>` computes 1×0; the horizontal case in a row Flex mirrors it. Unfixed on §16's own argument for refusing pseudo-element hit expansion: CSS cannot read the container's axis, so any fix is a guess. The escape is to state the orientation the container has.

**Progress — SHIPPED 2026-08-08, with two questions deliberately left open (§11):**
- **Does a bar earn the size index, and would granting it promote the track into a FAMILY?** Shipped without `size` on the argument in §11: the rail's ladder holds a fraction of the mark, a bar has no mark, and withholding is the additive direction. But the counter-argument is real and was not refuted, only deferred: `--mark-N` *is* `--line-height-N`, so the rail ladder is also "a quarter of a line of text", and a bar sits in text flow under a label where that reading applies perfectly well. If it is granted, the honest shape is not a second ladder but a rename — `--slider-track-N` → `--track-N`, one ladder with two consumers, exactly the promotion the mark family made on its third member and the field family on its third — because a shared thing wearing one consumer's name is the `--kui-h` collision's spelling. Blocked on nothing but the eye: the playground now shows a bar and a rail on one page.
- **Does Progress carry `tone` as colour-as-data?** It ships with the fixed accent identity (§11's binary-controls pattern, stamped so the well stays neutral through `--color-track`). A failed upload in `destructive`, a finished one in `success`, is the exact vocabulary the six-tone widening exists for — and Slider's refusal does *not* transfer, because that refusal was about EMPHASIS ("a form where one slider is louder than the next names nothing"), not about hue. Left closed because adding an axis to a component whose §11 row was written the same day is assignment, not derivation. The question is which meanings a bar can report, and that is answered by a real screen.

**Selection controls — Checkbox SHIPPED 2026-08-05; Radio and Switch pre-scoped:**
- ~~**Checkbox × `radius="full"`: the capsule must not reach it.**~~ **CLOSED — shipped as the mark corner ceiling (§6).** A circular checkbox reads as a radio — shape is role semantics here, and role legibility outranks theme uniformity. §6 already re-prices `full` per band (surfaces to designed corners, controls to h/2); the checkbox is the control band's designed exception at full, capping at a designed step instead of taking the capsule. Same family as the pill padding bump: a place where roundness meets a rule that outranks it. The cap's value is taste, judged in the preview.
- **A mark hosted in a slot stays a mark (2026-08-06, Kushagra — audit D4).** The hosted-control rule pins `height` to the slot's derived box, which is right for a Button and wrong for the one control whose box IS its mark: a checkbox in a field's trailing slot rendered 20 x 24, its corner holding two fractions of two axes, and on a fine pointer it kept its own expanding target inside the container — 36px of reach inside a 32px field, the exact inversion §4's hosted rule exists to prevent. Now both axes come from one expression (the mark, floored by the hosted box where the slot is genuinely tighter) and the mark's own expander is scoped out of slots entirely, so the container-matched coarse target from the shared rule is the only target a hosted mark has. A true type-level block was considered and is impossible — the slots accept any node — so behaving is the fix ("A", over documenting a broken cell nobody can be stopped from rendering). Radio and Switch inherit the geometry.
- ~~**Checkbox × coarse pointer: it stays a checkbox.**~~ **CLOSED, and the target answer changed in the building (§4):** it does stay a checkbox, and coarse does owe it a target — but the target is not 44, it is a control of its size capped at 44, and it applies in BOTH pointer worlds because the fine world's mark is under the minimum too. The box also turned out to grow on coarse after all, without a coarse ladder: the mark is a line of its label, and the handheld band raises the type. iOS's native vocabulary has none (switches for settings, edit-mode selection circles in lists) — but that is an iOS stance, not a mobile truth: Material ships one and the mobile web is full of them (consent, multi-select filters, row selection). Swapping to a switch by pointer would change semantics (a switch commits immediately; a checkbox waits for submit) — a product choice, never a pointer response. What coarse owes it is the target: the visual box stays small and the hit area extends to the 44 target — the hosted-control rule inverted (there the hit area shrinks to the container; here it grows past the box).
- **`readOnly` is REFUSED on Checkbox (2026-08-06, Kushagra), and the reason is that there is no wheel to reinvent.** The HTML attribute is defined for text fields and deliberately not for checkboxes — the WHATWG position is that a read-only checkbox has no useful distinction from a disabled one. Material has an open request from 2019 and never shipped it; Ant likewise; the libraries that do accept it (Base UI included) draw it identically to a live control, which is exactly the defect the audit found here (D5: accepted, resolving to nothing, hover still stepping the fill while the click was refused). Rather than design an appearance for a state the platform says should not exist, the prop is refused by the type with the other closed edges. What is lost: a read-only value submits with the form where a disabled one does not — rare, and an app that needs it sends the value itself. Radio inherits this refusal; a read-only SWITCH is the same question and takes the same answer.
- ~~**Radio is the checkbox's shape sibling, and it is what should promote the family.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-06: shipped, and it did promote — with the slider thumb as the third member landing in the same change** (§4, §14). The circle is role semantics and the radius axis never reaches it (§6); `readOnly` refused as inherited; the group is Base UI's, wrapped with zero CSS.
- ~~**Switch leaves the control height ladder — and now has somewhere to land.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-08: shipped** (§4, §6, §11, §19, §14's fifth repetition entry). Everything the pre-scoping named landed as scoped: the track is mark(n + 1) through the shared join's shifted entry (`--mark-5` emitted to hold size 4), the thumb is the track minus the designed 2px inset with its diameter DERIVING from the channel in CSS, the off-track wears `--color-track`, and the width closed as ONE designed ladder indexed by the track's mark step (34/40/44/48), priced per pointer world through the band picks — so "coarse widens the track" (the iOS 26 direction the pre-scoping predicted) arrives by derivation, not by a second designed pair. The known wrinkle stands as recorded and is law-asserted in both directions: at coarse size 4 the band's 4/5 collapse makes the shift inert and the switch stands level with its checkbox. Beyond the pre-scoping: the capsule and its thumb joined §6's role-corner exceptions (count four), and the whole control left the look axis with the slider (§19 — off is a well, an instrument's part, not a dressed surface). `readOnly` refused as promised; `children`/`render`/`nativeButton` refused by inheritance; width, inset and the well pairing are taste, judged in the playground.

**Feel (not ratios):**
- "Balanced" corner feel, tuned by eye per step.

**Two defects the ultracode audit found in the size prop and the type (2026-09-01).** `<Badge size="9">128</Badge>` painted 56px digits in a 43.39px pill — 10.3px of line box out of each end, at every step of the ramp — because the badge's own `font-size` is (0,1,0) against the type join's `.kui-type[data-size]` at (0,2,0), so a stated size took the join's full step for the digits while the box kept its 0.7 share. Two fixes in one declaration: the rule ties the join at (0,2,0) and wins by file order, and it reads `--kui-ty-line` rather than `1lh`, because `lh` in `font-size` resolves against the PARENT's line — the box was priced on the stated line and the digits on the surrounding one. The join publishes its step's line and font for exactly this. And `<Badge>{count > 0 && count}</Badge>` — the commonest spelling of a conditional badge — typechecks into the counted arm and arrives EMPTY at zero, where it rendered a 6.3px unnamed coloured disc: `React.ReactNode` admits `false | null | undefined`, so the runtime closes what the union cannot, Chip's own answer to the identical four inputs. Named, an empty badge is the dot the bare form means; unnamed, it renders nothing. **The cap-seat moved with them**: `badgeLift` was a share of the LINE, and the gap `vertical-align: middle` leaves is (cap − x-height)/2 — both shares of the FONT — so one fraction of the line was right at steps 1-4 and 2.41px low at step 9, where the law happened to walk only the three steps that could not fail. It is 0.095 of the line's font now, inside a quarter-pixel at every step of the ramp.
